# KNOWLEDGE — Drive lah Business Facts

Feeds: (1) the RAG "company facts" input (IDEAL_STATE §3 — the AI never runs blind), (2) future agents, (3) humans.

- **FLOW-34** IMS CHARGES/PAYS VIA PGW, NOT STRIPE-DIRECT (verified against `tms/tms-incidentals-service` code, 2026-08-09). **Guest charge is PGW-first:** IMS calls `pgw.CreatePayment` (source=INCIDENTS, provider="stripe") and PGW creates/settles the Stripe object — IMS never touches Stripe directly. The request is **ONE payin per incident with THREE aggregate amounts** — `amount_gross` (guest), `amount_host_share`, `amount_platform_share` — **NOT itemized line items** (IMS holds internal `pricingSnapshot.line_items[]` tagged by `meta.leg` charge_guest/payout_host/refund_guest but SUMS them by leg before the PGW boundary). On charge failure PGW's `HandlePaymentFailed(payin_id)` creates the **Stripe dunning invoice** (paymentStatus INVOICE_PENDING) — this is the exact analog of "the team creates an invoice in Stripe today." **Refund** = `pgw.CreateRefund` per payin, newest-first fan-out, single amount each (not a new invoice). **Host payout** = Payouts Service `createPayoutAdjustment` — single **signed** `amountMinor` (+credit/−debit) + refs, not itemized, **STUBBED (not live)**. Incident type drives TWO hardcoded maps: type→PGW `business_type` (7=LATE_FEE/8=CLEANING/9=DAMAGE/10=FUEL/11=TOLL…) AND (finance-side, POL-114) type→COA. **Consequence for the interim tool:** UI = **single amount + incident type**, three-party split derived — **no line-item editor**; and "do exactly what IMS does" ⇒ the interim should ideally call **PGW** (not Stripe-direct) IF PGW is live+callable from finance-api — open decision. *(IMS code, 2026-08-09)*
- **POL-116** DUPLICATE INVOICES ARE HARD-BLOCKED AT INGEST — REVERSES POL's allow-at-draft (Gaurav, 2026-08-09). The going-forward upload flow **cannot add a document that has been seen before.** The existing tiered `duplicate_detection_service.detect` (L1 byte-identical `pdf_content_hash`; L2 semantic vendor+invoice#+amount — both already return `action="block"`; L3 fuzzy/L4 LLM = review only) is now ENFORCED at `invoice_service.create` when a `pdf_content_hash` is present: `action=="block"` → `ConflictError`, no draft created. (Was: create-then-flag `recon.duplicate`, POL-106 only blocked at approval.) Historical bulk imports without a file hash are unaffected. Dedup storage lives entirely on `finance_invoices` (`pdf_content_hash` col + unique indexes `ix_finance_invoices_pdf_content_hash` (exact file) + `uq_finance_invoices_semantic` (entity+cp+invoice#+date+currency)). **Reference decision:** NO separate `DL-INV` ref — `finance_invoices.id` (internal) + `invoice_number` (vendor's) ARE the reference; a minted typed ref (`DL-HP`/`DL-GP`) is only needed later for host/guest payouts (no natural id). **Raise flow = existing upload+extraction** (`attach_document`/`ai_extraction_service`/`vendor_matching_service`) → team ratifies → submit; the COA-anchor gate (trip id + **ticket number**, renamed from "intercom ticket") fires on `submit` (parks `needs_fix` per the POL-107 state machine, `wip/INVOICES_STATE_MACHINE.md` — the lifecycle home; draft is the entry state). New vendor → prefilled from extraction, held draft until finance-approved. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-09)*
- **DQ-96** TWO INTERCOM TICKET TABLES + REGO IS A TRIP-ALTERNATIVE ANCHOR (Gaurav, 2026-08-09). **(a) Ticket lookup — TWO tables:** `intercom_tickets` (Intercom-API sync) is FAST (~0.4s, sorted on ticket_id) but MISSES newer 9-digit / **Receivables / Collections** tickets (e.g. `123076693`, `ticket_id` max there ≈ 99998508). `z_mysql_intercom_tickets` (MySQL mirror) is the SUPERSET (150,191 vs 150,094, identical columns) but a **slow full-scan (~12–24s)**. `enrichment_service.resolve_ticket` hits the fast table FIRST, falls back to the slow mirror only on a miss (extended 45s timeout, `with_thread=False` for the live form skips the heavy `ticket_parts` pull). So a "ticket not working" symptom was really the 9-digit ticket living only in the mirror. **(b) REGO as a trip-alternative:** a cost isn't always trip-specific (towing is **vehicle-level**), so the COA `needs_trip_id` gate is satisfied by a **trip id OR a vehicle rego** (POL: either operational anchor clears it; both individually optional). Rego lives in **`{market}_listings.publicData.license_plate_number`** (AU 8,686 / SG 15,369). `resolve_rego` is **case- + space-insensitive** (`upper(replaceAll(...,' ',''))`; `SMH 7616G`==`smh7616g`), tries au then sg, and **does NOT filter `deleted`** — a towed car is often a **DELISTED** listing (surfaced as `delisted: true`), ordered `deleted ASC, createdAt DESC` so an active listing wins. Stored on `finance_invoice_metadata.rego`; the approval card resolves it to vehicle/host when there's no trip. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-09)*
- **DQ-95** ANCHOR ID FORMATS + THE SHARED ENRICHMENT LAYER (Gaurav, 2026-08-09). Two operational anchors an invoice/claim/payout can carry, resolved LIVE against the ClickHouse replica: **(1) TRIP ID = the human trip code `TA########` (AU) / `TS########` (SG)** — NOT the transaction UUID, NOT the `sequenceId`. Stored in **`{market}_transactions.metadata.tripReferenceCode`** (`JSONExtractString(metadata,'tripReferenceCode')`); ~106k AU (`TA`) + ~96k SG (`TS`) rows carry it. Market is inferred from the prefix (self-describing). **(2) TICKET ID = the Intercom display ticket number** (e.g. `19307007`) = **`intercom_tickets.ticket_id`** — a payment can cite **several**, entered comma/space-separated. (Gaurav said "TATR…" verbally; live data proves the real format is `TA`/`TS`+digits — built to the verified format.) **Shared module `src/services/enrichment_service.py`** is the ONE resolver (ClickHouse reads only, no LLM): `resolve_trip` (by code), `resolve_trip_uuid`/`resolve_trip_any` (a ticket's embedded Trip Reference Number can be a UUID), `resolve_ticket`/`resolve_tickets` (multi), `validate_anchors` (form verdict). `approval_card_service` now delegates to it (was private `_trip_ctx`/`_ticket_ctx`) and resolves the trip DIRECTLY from the entered code (old gap: it only resolved via the ticket). **Real-time validation** at ratify: `GET /api/finance/enrichment/validate?trip_id=&ticket_ids=` → the upload form shows ✓ vehicle·host·guest·window / ✓ ticket title (or ✗ not found) as you type, so a bad anchor is caught at the door, not on the approver's card. It's an ADVISORY signal, not yet a hard submit-block. **DUAL-FORMAT (Gaurav, 2026-08-09):** the HISTORICAL Retool extractions put a **transaction UUID** in the trip field (not a TA/TS code) — verified on prod: `finance_invoices.ai_extraction_raw.retool_ref.description` carries `Trip ID: <uuid>` (e.g. `687c7e14-…`), sometimes with junk like a leading `-`. So the trip resolver accepts BOTH: a TA/TS code → `metadata.tripReferenceCode`, OR a transaction UUID → `{market}_transactions.id` (UUID is EXTRACTED from the string, junk tolerated; market tried au then sg). `resolve_trip_any` is the safe default and is what `validate_anchors` (upload form) + the approval card both use. BONUS: feeding a historical UUID returns the canonical TA/TS `trip_code` too, so old anchors can be upgraded. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-09)*
- **DQ-94** SG IS NOT GST-REGISTERED (for now) — NO separate GST receivable for SG invoices (Gaurav, 2026-08-09). The entity GST flag = `finance_entities.gst_rate`: **Drive lah Singapore Pte Ltd = null/none** (not registered), **Drive lah Australia = 0.10**. So for the GST engine (RA-7/POL-44): AU invoices split input GST to a GST-receivable/control account; **SG invoices do NOT** — the GST line is not separately recoverable while SG is unregistered. Revisit when SG registers. The upload UI's "Dr 1350 GST Receivable on approval" hint must be gated on the entity's gst_rate (skip for SG).
- **POL-117** **NO AUTO-ACTIVATION OF VENDORS (Gaurav 2026-08-09):** every vendor created via ANY path (ratify quick-add, raise-new-vendor, counterparties module) is forced `status=inactive`+`is_verified=False` in `counterparty_service.create` and raises a `vendor-approval` finance task; it goes active only via `invoice_service.approve_vendor`. The invoice submit gate KEEPS the invoice in `draft` (NOT needs_fix — that's a team-fix state; a pending vendor is waiting on FINANCE) if its vendor is **not active**; approve_vendor (sets active+verified) then auto-submits ANY draft awaiting that vendor. **GATE KEYS ON `status != active` ONLY — NOT is_verified (Gaurav 2026-08-10):** new vendors are created `inactive` so status catches them; keying ALSO on is_verified wrongly held invoices for **82 legacy `active`-but-`is_verified=False` vendors** (e.g. Experian, cp 274) that are legitimately usable and have NO vendor-approval task to clear them — invoice 2490 stuck in draft was this bug. is_verified stays a vendor attribute (KYC/bank) but is NOT part of the submit gate.
- **POL-115** APPROVALS ROUTE THROUGH THE EXISTING TASK QUEUE + APPROVAL AGENT — NO SEPARATE APPROVALS SURFACE (Gaurav, 2026-08-09, correcting an over-build). Everything needing approval — invoice, payout, vendor — flows through the ONE mechanism: the **task system** (POL-108) with the **approval agent** (POL-109) summarising the case (Summary + risk flags + confidence) onto the task; the approver acts in their **task inbox**, never a bespoke tab. The AW-2 `finance_coa_config` supplies only the **routing** (who approves, how many steps): on `pending_approval`, the invoice-approval task is assigned to the config's `approver_1` (was hardcoded zilla); **two-step sign-off runs INSIDE the task queue** — `task act 'approve'` → `approval_chain_service.decide`; a non-final step keeps the task OPEN and **reassigns it to `approver_2`**, only the final step posts the JE (via the existing `invoice_service.approve`). The standalone Approvals tab/blueprint I built (AW-7) was REMOVED. **Approver field must be a dropdown of ONBOARDED employees** (`hr_employees`, exclude offboarded), not free text. **CONFIG SCOPE (load-bearing):** `finance_coa_config` applies ONLY to the **request-raise route** (raised invoices / raised payout requests). **Automated economic-event postings** (host P2P revenue, incidentals, all view-driven JEs) post automatically and NEVER consult this config — the gate helpers live only in the raise/approval path, never in economic-events. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-09)*
- **POL-114** INCIDENT→COA MAPPING IS FINANCE-API's JOB, NOT IMS's (verified against `tms/tms-incidentals-service` code+docs, 2026-08-09). IMS **explicitly does not** write ledger entries or map incidents to a chart-of-accounts — its docs state it "emits events consumed by an Accounting Service… does not exist yet." **finance-api IS that Accounting Service** (ledger-plan D-1). So the mapping `incident type → COA + the three money legs` lives HERE, as **finance-owned config** (new `finance_incident_coa_map`, same editable+audited pattern as the AW-2 `finance_coa_config` settings module), NOT in IMS. This is the technical grounding for Gaurav's rule "the team selects the INCIDENT, never the COA — COA maps automatically" (Gaurav, 2026-08-09): the incident-request form offers incident TYPES, our config derives the COA. **Key the map on IMS's real `type_code`(+`sub_type_code`) values** — ~29 codes in `ims_incidental_type_config` (`excess_mileage`, `late_return`, `accident_damage`[guest_injury/car_damage/third_party_property], `tolls`, `parking_fine`, `traffic_fine`, `cleaning`, `damage`, `host_cancellation_penalty`, …) — so IMS cutover is a pure source-swap (the `incident.approved.v1` consumer reads the SAME map). Money legs are minor-units ints: `amount_guest_minor` (+owes/−refund), `amount_host_delta_minor` (+credit/−debit), `amount_platform_delta_minor` (derived). **Build status at coining:** IMS incident model + guest PGW leg LIVE, but **host Payouts Service is a STUB (unimplemented)** and **IMS does not feed finance at all yet** → the interim bridge (host = payout-entry sheet, guest = Raise-flow charge) is genuinely needed for a real while, not a few-week stopgap. *(Gaurav + IMS code, 2026-08-09)*
- **POL-113** TEAMS = SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH ON `users.teams` (Gaurav, 2026-08-07). A "team" is a string enum (core/tech/product/host/finance/…) — there is NO teams table. Membership is a `text[]` array. **Authoritative home = `users.teams`** — an ORG-DIRECTORY attribute (populated by the Google org sync from org units), read by: **KPIs** (`admin-bff/routes/kpis.ts` uses `user.teams[0]` as the team to group performance, then hits the ai-agents KPI service by team name; the finance `user_kpis`/`kpi_definitions` tables key by user_id, NOT team — "team→KPI" is a convention at assignment time, not a FK), **auth** (JWT carries `user.teams`), **admin user-mgmt**, and the org sync. `hr_employees.teams` was a **stale 1-row duplicate** written once at onboard, read only in the HR list's `coalesce(e.teams,u.teams)`. **RETIRED 2026-08-07 (PR #22):** onboard no longer writes `hr_employees.teams`; the HR list reads `u.teams` only; the column/model left inert (droppable later). Teams are NOT moved to the employee record — that would strand teams for non-onboarded staff (hr_employees is only the onboarded subset) and require repointing KPIs/auth/sync. RULE: read/write team membership ONLY via `users.teams`.
- **POL-112** EMPLOYEE IDENTITY MODEL — three tiers, counterparty is the single COA source (Gaurav, 2026-08-07). **(1) `users.is_employee=true` = the employee FLAG** (identity master; POL-102: true for every staff member; 49 today, 44 active). **(2) `hr_employees` = the ONBOARDED subset** — a row exists ONLY once HR onboards (grows from 1 toward 49); "onboarded?" is judged by the hr_employees row, NOT is_employee (which is true for all). **(3) `finance_counterparties` type=employee = the historical roster** (all current+past incl. leavers). Each employee = ONE user → ONE employee counterparty → ONE hr_employee. **Employee counterparties live in a DEDICATED `external_system='employee'` namespace keyed by `external_id=user_id`** — NOT `user_registry` (that's shared by marketplace vendor/host records; a dual-role person like Matheus/Mart (user 87, vendor AND employee) collides under the `(external_system,external_id)` unique constraint; the `employee` namespace keeps them separate). Leavers not in the registry → external_id blank. **The salary/expense COA single source of truth = `counterparty.default_account_code`** (NOT the legacy `hr_employees.salary_expense_code`): payroll resolves the salary account from the counterparty (transitional fallback to the emp column + warning); onboarding UPSERTS it. **CLEANUP APPLIED to prod 2026-08-07** (supervised, backed up `documentation/wip/_local/cp_pre_cleanup_20260807.csv` + `txn_on_639_20260807.csv`): re-keyed all 122 employee cps into `employee` namespace; merged 2 duplicates — cp639(Vighnesh)→cp219 (29 txns repointed), cp230(Sarahh)→cp615, both retired inactive; linked 76 active cps to users, 44 leavers left blank. LEFT (HR process, not data): fill COA on 30 blanks at onboarding; backfill hr_employees by onboarding the 44 active staff; flip `is_employee=true` for users 64 (Ben) + 113 (Mehul) — linked as employees but flag was false. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-07)*
- **POL-111** THE PAY QUEUE — approved invoices awaiting payout, FIFO by approval, manually re-orderable, rejectable with JE reversal (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). When an invoice is **approved** it enters a **pay/payout queue** (the approved-payables list). **(a) DEFAULT SORT = FIFO by approval time** — earliest-approved on top, latest at the bottom; the queue **carries the approval date** as the visible sort key. **(b) APPROVAL AUDIT TRAIL exists** — `invoice.approved_by` + `approved_at` are stamped on every `approve()` (who + when); this is the FIFO key (no build needed for the trail itself). **(c) MANUAL RE-ORDER** — finance can drag items up/down to reprioritise the pay order; needs a NEW priority/position field (default null → falls back to `approved_at` FIFO) + a reorder endpoint. **(d) RE-ORDER AUDIT TRAIL** — every manual move is logged (invoice, from→to position, who, when). **(e) REJECT FROM THE APPROVED QUEUE, WITH REASON** — you can still reject an already-approved invoice. NUANCE (accounting weight): `approve()` already POSTS the bill JE (Dr expense / Cr 2000 AP, sets `journal_entry_id`), so rejecting an approved invoice is NOT a status flip — it MUST **reverse/void that journal entry**, then mark rejected with reason (`rejected_by`/`rejected_at` already exist). Today `reject()` is BLOCKED for approved (`invoice_service.py:800` allows only draft/pending_approval) — must widen + add JE reversal. This queue IS the approval-arm "pay this invoice" surface (same place the pay/pair-to-payment action lives). Status today: audit trail ✅; ordering field ❌; reorder trail ❌; reject-from-approved ❌ (all to build). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-110** TWO AUDIENCES: ACCOUNTING (finance-only) vs REQUESTS (everyone) (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). **Accounting module stays fully intact and finance-gated** — invoices + upload, transactions, reconciliation, payouts, counterparties (the counterparties list STAYS in accounting: it's for viewing a counterparty's history + a future statement of accounts; employee counterparties already hidden from non-admins + 403 on open, keep that), approvals. NOTHING moves out of Accounting. **NEW top-level "Requests" area, next to Accounting, for EVERYONE**, two tabs: **(a) RAISE a request** — one form, four types: **host payout · guest payout/refund · vendor invoice · my claim** — each creates the underlying record and enters the SAME task/approval machinery already live (v2 card → needs_fix/pending_approval → assignee queue); NO new pipeline for invoice/claim. **(b) TRACK requests** — **LIBERAL scope for vendor/host/guest**: look up any vendor/host/guest → see ALL requests raised for them AND what's been paid; **PRIVATE for a person's OWN claim** (they see only theirs). **CONSOLIDATION (this replaces 5 scattered going-forward items):** invoice-status + payout-status fold into TRACK; "what's paid/not paid" (Bottle host-payout lookup over ClickHouse `au_/sg_payout_entries`, POL host-payout) becomes TRACK's host/guest data source, NOT a standalone Retool/Slack page; CLAIMS RELOCATE from the finance module into Requests (Raise: my claim; Track: my claims, private); finance invoice-upload STAYS in Accounting (reverted — the requester's invoice path is Raise→vendor-invoice). The ONLY genuinely-new backend is the **host/guest payout-REQUEST** type. Slack agent stays a later lookup layer over the same data. Sequence: (1) host/guest paid-status data (feeds Track), (2) Requests surface Raise+Track, (3) relocate claims, (4) Slack. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)* **IMPL (2026-08-06):** Requests is a top-level `FinanceContainer` sub-module (peer to Collections/Accounting/FP&A/Counterparties), `requiredModule` unset = visible to everyone who can reach Finance. Removed the earlier (wrong-level) Requests sub-tab AND the "My Claims" sub-tab from inside `AccountingModule`. **GATE RESOLVED (2026-08-06, Gaurav):** Requests is its own first-class module, at the same level as Counterparties — reuse the pre-existing `finance.payment_requests` (already in both FE+BFF registries; Gaurav chose reuse over a new `finance.requests` to avoid a synonym). Made it a **BASE module** (`admin-bff/src/constants/modules.ts` `BASE_MODULES`): every authenticated console user implicitly holds it — `withBaseModules()` unions it into the JWT modules list at login + refresh, and `authService.hasModuleAccess` short-circuits `true` for it. Effect: Requests tab visible to everyone AND (since it's a `finance.*` module) the Finance **card** opens for non-finance staff, who then see ONLY the Requests tab. No per-user grants, auto-covers new users, reversible (drop it from `BASE_MODULES`). FE tab gated on `requiredModule: 'finance.payment_requests'` in `FinanceContainer`. The host-payouts API had no module gate already (open). *(2026-08-06)*
- **POL-109** APPROVAL AGENT v2 SPEC — canonical in `documentation/wip/approval_agent/AGENT_SPEC_v2.md` (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). The invoice-approval card must be produced by THIS agent, NOT the contract-conformance `_ai_contract_review` (that was wired by mistake, produces "no contract" for all — contracts don't exist). **Payment-request data model:** ANCHORS (captured; today PARSED from Retool free-text, future ENTERED by requester) = `trip_id` (**LEADING**: a `TA…`/`TS…` code or a transaction id to resolve), `intercom_ticket` (**LEADING**: ticket number OR conversation id), + SUPPORTING `host_id`/`guest_id`/`vehicle_rego`/`listing_id` (never lead with these — they fan out to ALL of an entity's trips), + `retool_payment_id` (TEMPORARY bridge key, retires at cutover). Everything else (type, payee→counterparty, amount, currency, reason, entity, doc, invoice#/date/due) is SYSTEM-PRODUCED, not an anchor. **Agent steps:** (1) extract+store anchors from ALL pending invoices (not a sample); (2) enrich TRIP-LED — Intercom via **MCP** (pull the whole ticket/conversation, summarise) + ClickHouse (resolve the ONE trip, pull that trip's vehicle/host/guest/window; host/guest/rego only corroborate, never lead); (3) **counterparty double-pay second pass** — LLM scan for an unattributed/unmatched payment to that counterparty that could already be settling THIS invoice → flag "may already be paid"; (4) card = **Summary + Risk flags + Confidence score** only. Corrects v0.1 (`scripts/approval_agent.py`): trip-led not rego-led, Intercom MCP not local client, adds the double-pay pass. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-108** APPROVAL = A TASK; ASSIGNEE-GATED pending_approval; AI REVIEW WIRED (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). **(a) ASSIGNEE GATE (hard):** an invoice CANNOT enter `pending_approval` without an assignee — enforced in `submit()` and re-checked at `approve()`. IMPLEMENTED via the existing generic TASK system, NOT a new invoice column: entering `pending_approval` atomically enqueues an `invoice-approval` task assigned to someone (`tasks.assignee_user_id`/`assignee_role`) — that assigned task IS the gate, and the task holds the assignee (no `invoice.assigned_to` column). Default assignee for NOW = `zilla@drivelah.sg` (resolved to her user id; role `finance.invoices` fallback if unresolved), she guards all approvals. FUTURE: once approval authorities exist, routing splits — some invoices AUTO-APPROVE, the rest route to the respective approver (including zilla) by authority/threshold. Triage sets `zilla@` on every one of the 237 that screens clean. **(b) AI REVIEW WIRED:** the triage screen runs the deterministic guardrails (dup/counterparty/COA/fields — the router to needs_fix vs pending_approval) AND the AI contract-review (`_ai_contract_review`, currently dead code) is wired to attach a plain-English assessment + concerns per invoice. ADVISORY ONLY — it never auto-approves (POL: no auto-approve). **(c) APPROVAL IS A TASK, NOT A BESPOKE PAGE (Gaurav, 2026-08-06):** model approval as one task type in a GENERIC TASKS LANE — "approval is a task; there could be other tasks as well." UX: the main page shows only a per-user PENDING-TASK COUNT; clicking opens the user's Tasks page (list); clicking a task opens its detail where the user takes action (e.g. the approval card). Build SIMPLE now (a tasks list + detail + action), expand later. This replaces the earlier "separate Approvals page" idea — approvals live inside Tasks. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-107** INVOICE STATE MACHINE — CANONICAL, defined in `documentation/wip/INVOICES_STATE_MACHINE.md` (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). That doc is the single source of truth for the invoice lifecycle; this bullet is the pointer. **10 statuses** (3 NEW): `draft` (captured, untriaged) → two arms. **Reconciliation arm** (already paid outside the system, e.g. Retool): `reconcile` (awaiting finance to provisionally PAIR the payment) → `paired` (provisional match made, awaiting posting) → `paid`. **Approval arm** (not yet paid): `draft` → approval agent screens → `needs_fix` (exception: duplicate / no counterparty / missing info) OR `pending_approval` (clean, agent-blessed) → `approved` → `partially_paid`/`paid`; plus `rejected`, `void`. NEW statuses = `reconcile`, `paired`, `needs_fix`. **Two hard rules in code:** (1) `paired → paid` is AUTHORIZATION-ONLY (finance pairs, never posts; only Gaurav/controlled run posts); (2) DUPLICATE HARD-BLOCK per POL-106 (a dup-flagged invoice cannot leave `needs_fix` toward approval until the flag is cleared). One machine serves both the historical cutover AND future uploads (future upload: `draft`→agent→`pending_approval`→`approved`→`paid`; already-paid import: `draft`→`reconcile`→`paired`→`paid`). Today's data (2026-08-06): 1,010 stay `paid`; 664 Retool-paid drafts → `reconcile` (50 with a machine match → `paired`); 237 not-paid drafts → approval arm → `needs_fix`/`pending_approval`. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-106** DUPLICATE = HARD BLOCK ON APPROVAL (Gaurav, 2026-08-06, GROUND RULE — non-negotiable). No invoice carrying a duplicate flag may transition toward approval or payment, full stop. On the review side, an invoice CANNOT reach `pending_approval` or `approve` while a duplicate flag stands: the flag must first be cleared as an exception (void the true duplicate, or confirm it is a coincidental shared-doc per DQ-89 and clear the flag). Enforced in the approve path (service HARD-rejects a flagged invoice) AND made loudly visible to finance (a duplicate banner on the invoice). Nobody can approve a flagged duplicate for payment. Duplicate signals available today: `pdf_content_hash` collision (77 in the recon queue, 12 in the review queue as of 2026-08-06) and semantic key (counterparty+invoice_number+date+amount: 7 recon / 3 review). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-105** RECONCILE UX = THE INVOICES TAB ITSELF, NOT A SEPARATE QUEUE (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). The Invoices tab already lists every invoice, so it IS the reconcile queue: add filters/columns (counterparty assigned? matched? provisionally-paid?) and inline the engine's suggested match, rather than building a separate reconciliation screen. **RECONCILE STEP ONE = identify a counterparty for EVERY invoice (and every payment).** `counterparty_id` is the join key both sides match on (POL-97: the payee is load-bearing); an invoice/payment with no counterparty appears in no vendor view and cannot be matched, so completing counterparty links is the PREREQUISITE action, not an afterthought. Existing machinery to reuse (do NOT rebuild): per-vendor two-panel matcher `VendorDetailView` (Confirm Match → provisional), tiered proposal engine `payline_match.py`, golden posting engine `vr2_post_provisional.py`; the two real gaps are (a) surfacing engine proposals + the post/log action IN-APP (today the posting engine is an offline script) and (b) batch-sum multi-select (N invoices ↔ 1 payment via `amount_applied`, already in the data model). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-104** ONBOARDING & PAYEE-BANK GOVERNANCE (Gaurav, 2026-08-06). **(a) A START DATE IS MANDATORY TO ONBOARD — no start date, no onboarding, no exceptions.** The canonical start = `users.date_of_joining`; the onboard flow requires it (payload `start_date`, else an already-set `date_of_joining`), rejects a missing/invalid one BEFORE any write, and persists it. Rationale: offboarding must bookend a real employment span. **(b) YOU MUST ONBOARD BEFORE YOU CAN OFFBOARD** — offboarding requires an `hr_employees` row (already enforced, POL-102); a not-yet-onboarded person is un-marked (`is_employee=false`), never "offboarded". Added guard: `offboard_date` may not precede `date_of_joining` (can't leave before you joined). **(c) SUPER-ADMIN IS NOT EXEMPT FROM HR** — the `role='admin'` bypass STANDS for the HR module (no special carve-out); admins retain full HR access. **(d) PAYEE BANK ACCOUNTS ARE GATED BEHIND `finance.payouts`** — `/accounting/payee-bank-accounts` GET=read, POST/PUT=write, DELETE=admin; the bank surface is payout-sensitive (Wise mirror + payment routing), not general HR data. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-06)*
- **POL-103** CANONICAL USER↔HR DATA MODEL — identity/IT/access on `users`, ALL personal+employment on HR (Gaurav, 2026-08-05). **`users` holds ONLY: (a) identity** — email, name; **(b) the staff flag** — is_employee; **(c) IT/system + access IDs as ONE JSONB `system_ids`** (slack/intercom/aircall + any FUTURE system — never add a per-system column again; new systems just add a JSON key); **(d) `google_workspace_id`** stays a real column (it is the SYNC ANCHOR the Workspace sync matches on, not a convenience id); **(e) access** → `user_permissions` table (module+level per user_id, already live); **(f) lifecycle** — status, deleted_at, timestamps. **HR (`hr_employees`) holds ALL personal + employment — INCLUDING team management** (Gaurav 2026-08-05: "even team management, everything sits with HR, nowhere in user management"): personal = address, country (DROPDOWN enum), phone (E.164) — personal details live with HR ALWAYS; employment = manager_id (REPORTING LINE moves here from users), entity_id, designation (FREE TEXT, = old org_role), region, **teams (managed from HR, not user-mgmt)**, employee_type, tax_treatment, salary_expense_code, date_of_joining, employment_end_date. **USER-MANAGEMENT UI = IT + ACCESS ONLY** — identity (email/name), `system_ids`, `user_permissions` (access), and a single **"mark as employee" toggle** (`is_employee`); every employment/personal/team field is REMOVED from user-mgmt and managed from the HR module. **DECLARING EMPLOYEE-HOOD IS A USER-MANAGEMENT ACTION, NOT HR (Gaurav, 2026-08-05):** the ONLY way a user becomes an employee is the `is_employee` toggle in USER-MANAGEMENT. The moment it's set, that user AUTOMATICALLY appears in the HR module (as not-onboarded); HR then onboards them. The HR module NEVER declares/creates employees — it is strictly downstream (show is_employee=true users → onboard → offboard). So the pipeline is: **user-mgmt sets is_employee ⇒ appears in HR ⇒ HR onboards**. **Other UI decisions (2026-08-05):** salary shows the COA account NAME not the code; compensation form is adaptive (HOURLY_RATE → "hourly rate" input); employee bank form hides entity (auto = employment entity); every HR data change must write an AUDIT record (who/what/when/old→new). → then `hr_compensation` (salary), `hr_deduction_rules` (CPF/super), `finance_payout_bank_accounts` (bank, POL-101). **STANDARDIZED VOCABULARIES:** `teams` = **FROZEN as-is; the CURRENT values ARE the canonical set — do NOT restandardize/rename** (Gaurav, 2026-08-05): `teams` is KPI-LINKED — `admin-bff/routes/kpis.ts` assigns a user's KPI by `teams[0]` (fallback `core`) and matches against `kpi_definitions`/`user_kpis`, so renaming a team silently reassigns who's measured against which KPI. Canonical live set: core, verification, tech, c&s, resolutions, host, fleet ops, founders, product, finance, data, flexplus, hr. Only cleanup done = stripped junk `na` from 50 rows (of which just 2 were real employees — contractors craig/jabez, `na` their only team, **0 user_kpis rows → zero KPI impact**; restorable from backup). `country` = dropdown enum (fix `Mynnamar`→`Myanmar`); `phone` = E.164; **`region` = the MARKETS they work for (`SG`/`AU`), an ARRAY, irrespective of country of residence — DROP `global`.** MAPPING (Gaurav, 2026-08-05): **`global` → `{SG, AU}`** (works both markets), `singapore` → `{SG}`, `australia` → `{AU}`. Stored as `hr_employees.region text[]` (Aakash backfilled `{SG,AU}`); the 47 not-yet-onboarded keep single-text `users.region` staging (global 41 / singapore 4 / australia 3) and the onboard code applies this map into the array at onboarding. (region is NOT KPI-linked — safe to standardize, unlike teams.); `designation` = free text. **MIGRATION = EXPAND→CONTRACT (Gaurav guardrail: delete NOTHING now):** EXPAND done 2026-08-05 — added `users.system_ids` (backfilled from slack/intercom/aircall), added the personal+employment columns to `hr_employees`, backfilled onboarded rows, fixed `Mynnamar`, stripped `na` from teams; OLD `users` employment/IT columns KEPT (live Google-sync + HR module still read them) → CONTRACT-drop them only AFTER code reads the new homes. While an employee is NOT-yet-onboarded (no hr_employees row) their employment data stages on the `users` row; onboarding promotes it into hr_employees. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-05)*
- **POL-102** HR EMPLOYEE STATE MODEL + ONBOARDING GOVERNANCE (Gaurav, 2026-08-05). Clean two-flag state, nothing more: **(a) is this user an employee? yes/no** (`users.is_employee`) — separates real staff from vendors/service/external accounts that also have a Workspace login. **(b) if employee, are they onboarded or offboarded?** — onboarded = has an `hr_employees` row (+ compensation + deductions); offboarded = `hr_employees.employment_end_date` set (past employee, auto-excluded from payroll). **The HR module shows ALL employees, current AND past** (is_employee=true, both states) — a past employee must still appear. **Onboarding happens ONLY from the HR module** (no other surface). **Population rule:** Gaurav supplies the definitive employee list (the `is_employee` flag is currently unreliable — only 1 set); by that filter every real employee appears in the HR table as **NOT-yet-onboarded** (today only Aakash user2 is onboarded); HR then onboards them one-by-one with start dates, or marks them offboarded. **"Choose not to onboard" = they are not an employee** (is_employee=false) → they simply don't belong in HR. So the decision tree is exactly: user → employee? (no ⇒ out of HR) → onboarded | offboarded. **`drivemate.au` is INTERNAL** (Drive lah Australia's domain) — jake@drivemate.au is an EX-employee (offboarded), NOT an external account; the employee roster spans both @drivelah.sg and @drivemate.au. **STORAGE (decided + implemented 2026-08-05):** the onboarded/offboarded state is NOT `onboarding_status` (which drifted — Aakash was onboarded yet still read `PENDING`; treat it as DEPRECATED). Authoritative signals: **`users.is_employee`** = is/was staff, stays TRUE through offboarding (only false for non-staff); **`hr_employees` row present** = onboarded; **`hr_employees.employment_end_date` set** = offboarded (past); **is_employee=true but NO hr_employees row** = not-yet-onboarded (the state all 48 are in now). **FIX APPLIED** to `hr_onboarding_service.offboard_employee`: it used to flip `is_employee=false` on offboard (which would HIDE past employees from HR) — now it keeps is_employee TRUE, only stamps `employment_end_date` on users + hr_employees, and validates "onboarded" by the hr_employees row (not the flag); counterparty still set inactive to stop future payouts. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-05)*
- **POL-101** THE IDENTITY→PAYMENT CHAIN: user_id → counterparty → bank account — DO NOT CONFLATE, BANK LIVES IN FINANCE (Gaurav, 2026-08-05). **`user_id` is the ultimate/master key** for the whole admin system (access, tasks, claims, HR). For PAYMENT, an employee also has a **counterparty** (finance side), linked back to the user — the link is `finance_counterparties.external_system='users'` + **`external_id`=user_id** (there is NO literal `user_id` column on counterparties; external_id/external_system IS the FK; e.g. Aakash user2 → cp272 external_id='2'). **Each counterparty has its BANK ACCOUNT in the finance master `finance_payout_bank_accounts`** (cols: counterparty_id, payee_type/payee_id, wise_recipient_id, account_number, bank_code, currency…) — the SAME table + code path used for vendor payouts. So the canonical chain is **user_id → employee counterparty (external_id=user_id) → bank account (finance_payout_bank_accounts.counterparty_id)** — identical to how vendors work. **DECISION: bank details must NOT live on HR/`users`.** Onboarding currently ALSO writes `users.bank_account_number`/`bank_code` — that duplication is to be removed (write bank to the finance master via the employee counterparty; drop the two `users` columns). One bank master for ALL payees (vendors + employees), one payout path, single source of truth. Onboarding already creates the employee counterparty (`hr_onboarding_service`, external_system='users'), so the hook exists. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-05)*
- **POL-100** INVOICE RECON = A CONFIDENCE LADDER, NOT A MATCH — the doctrine learned from the 2026-08 vendor-recon scale-up (Pickle, 2026-08-04). Live state at coining: 1,949 invoices → 1,065 paired (880 posted + 185 provisional), 884 open (530 clean-data, 354 data-defective). Seven lessons, each load-bearing: **(1) Pairing is TIERED by certainty, strongest first** — reference (invoice# in bank text, definitive) → amount+**temporal-gate**(txn_date ≥ invoice_date)+same-vendor → currency/entity-neutralized (FX/IC) → OCR-reopen → fungible-FIFO (POL-99) → manual. Every match row is stamped with its tier in `source` so posting/audit knows how far to trust it. **(2) The PAYEE is the load-bearing key** (POL-97): almost every tier needs invoice and payment to agree on WHO was paid, so fixing the counterparty MODEL (folding duplicate vendor IDs, GT-vs-GT-Trust name variants, broker-vs-underwriter Penguin/GT) unlocked more pairings than any matching cleverness — the insurance consolidation alone recovered ~25 structurally. **(3) Amount coincidence WITHOUT a reference is a trap at every scale** — the 140 cross-payee were 96% coincidental collisions; inv678 nearly stole inv1636's $5k payment. Cross-payee is trustworthy ONLY with a reference number. **(4) Reference numbers are gold but inconsistently carried** — Penguin BPAY embeds the invoice#→trivial; GT direct transfers carry nothing→45 stuck. Push source systems to carry refs; where structurally impossible, fungible-FIFO is the honest fallback. **(5) Fungibility is a legitimate recon MODE** (POL-99) — same-vendor same-amount reference-less settlements have unrecoverable AND irrelevant identity; the ledger outcome is identical. **(6) The remaining hard problems are NOT matching bugs — two distinct beasts: BATCH-SUM** (one payment = Σ many invoices: Penguin's $174k "May Dec 25" BPAY, monthly Income/Tokio statements — needs subset-sum logic, the next real build) and **BAD SOURCE DATA** (354 open = stub dates/no counterparty/no amount — needs recovery from Retool metadata, a data problem). **(7) The GOLDEN RULE is the one invariant that never bends** — never pair a payment already carrying ANY match row (whole-table check + write-time re-assert); it is what makes stacking tiers safe. Coverage ceiling before data-recovery ≈ +530, most gated behind batch-sum or payments-not-yet-in-feed. *(Pickle synthesis, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-99** FUNGIBLE FIFO PAIRING FOR REFERENCE-LESS, SAME-VENDOR, SAME-AMOUNT SETTLEMENTS (Gaurav, 2026-08-04). When a vendor's invoices carry NO invoice number and its payments carry NO reference, and both are round near-identical amounts to the SAME counterparty (the GT excess case, DQ-87), the specific invoice↔payment IDENTITY is unrecoverable — but it is also **fungible**: any payment settles any like-amount invoice into the SAME account for the SAME amount, so the ledger outcome is identical regardless of which pairs to which. POLICY: pair such invoices **FIFO** — oldest invoice grabs the EARLIEST unpaired payment on/after its date within amount tolerance (`max($2, 1%)`, which absorbs the +$1 surcharge). Confidence MEDIUM, `source=fifo_fungible`. **The golden rule is absolute and overrides everything:** never pair a payment that already has ANY row in `finance_invoice_payment_matches` (checked against the whole table + re-asserted at write time), so this can never steal a reference-confirmed payment. Only applies to date-good invoices; stub-dated (1900/1901) with no recoverable date stay open. Applied 2026-08-04: 32 GT excess invoices paired via `scripts` FIFO (`created_by=gt_excess_fifo`), 0 double-use. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-98** AUSTRALIA/SINGAPORE INSURANCE COUNTERPARTY STRUCTURE (Gaurav ops-team, 2026-08-04). **AU has a broker→underwriter split; premium ≠ excess.** **Premium lane:** invoices raised by the BROKER **Penguin Risk** (cp119), paid via the bill-pay platform **DEFT** (folded into Penguin as alias) → COA **5035** (Trip Premium); premiums are large ($9.6k–$855k). **Excess/claims lane:** billed AND paid directly to the UNDERWRITER of the period → COA **5036** (Excess/Deductible); excess is small (capped ~$5k). AU underwriter timeline: **Hollard/Insuret** (early) → **GT** (mid) → **CRI** (since Mar-2026). Insuret IS Hollard's claims arm (invoices literally say "Insuret Claims Department / The Hollard Insurance Company") — folded into **Insuret** (cp86, canonical per Gaurav) as one underwriter. **Singapore: direct insurers, no broker** — **Tokio Marine** (cp144) and **Income Insurance** (cp694, NTUC folded in — NTUC Income rebranded to Income) each bill and are paid directly, doing BOTH premium (5035) and excess (5036), so NO single default COA — classify per invoice by size (large=premium, small=excess). Final insurance counterparties: Penguin(+DEFT), Insuret(+Hollard), GT, CRI(create when it appears), Tokio, Income(+NTUC). Consolidations applied 2026-08-04 (id+name repointed, aliases set, old cps deleted). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-97** COUNTERPARTY = THE PAYEE (WHO WE REMIT TO), NOT THE BILLER — THE UNIVERSAL RECON KEY (Gaurav, 2026-08-04). The counterparty stamped on an invoice must be **who we actually pay**, not who issued it. Biller and payee usually coincide but diverge in insurance (broker Penguin bills a premium, we pay DEFT/Penguin; a claims-admin bills an excess, we pay the underwriter). Aliases (name→cp) CANNOT resolve this — the same biller name maps to different counterparties by invoice CONTENT. Universal solution, two steps: **(1) determine the payee from the invoice itself** — read the remittance / "pay to" / beneficiary / bank-account details, else infer from the invoice's nature (premium→broker, excess→underwriter); this step does NOT use the counterparty book. **(2) resolve that payee to a counterparty** via name+aliases. This makes the invoice's counterparty = the transaction's counterparty (both = who we pay), so pairing works by construction. Requires: add a `payee`/`remit_to` field to the vision extraction, and key the invoice matcher on the payee. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-96** INVOICE INGESTION MATCHES ON NAME-ONLY VIA LLM — IT DOES NOT USE ALIASES (verified 2026-08-04). TWO separate counterparty resolvers exist: **(a) categorization engine** (transaction enrichment, Phase 1) — deterministic, matches raw counterparty_name/description against `cp.name` **+ `cp.aliases`** (aliases ARE used). **(b) invoice ingestion** (`vendor_matching_service.match_or_create` → `llm_match_vendor`) — tax-id exact match, else a Haiku LLM that is given ONLY `id: name [type]` per counterparty (**aliases NOT passed**), else auto-creates a new unverified counterparty. CONSEQUENCE: the alias merges we do (Hollard→Insuret, DEFT→Penguin, NTUC→Income) fix TRANSACTION categorization but NOT future invoice ingestion — a new "Hollard" invoice would fail to match (LLM never sees "Hollard" as an alias) and auto-create a duplicate. FIX: feed `cp.aliases` into the invoice LLM prompt (`id: name (aka: aliases) [type]`) so both engines share the alias registry. Also (POL-97) key the matcher on the payee. *(verified from code, 2026-08-04)*

## 1. Entities & structure (ENT)

- **ENT-1** Only 3 real legal entities: Drive lah Pte Ltd (SG), Drive lah Ventures Holding (SG), Drive lah Australia Pty Ltd (AU). *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
- **ENT-2** "Drive lah Fleet" (SG) and "Drive mate fleet" (AU) were MOCK QuickBooks entities created only to segregate RMS money; their GL folds into the parents. *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
- **ENT-3** "Drive mate" / "DRIVE MATE MELBOURNE" is the AU trading name — our own name in bank text, never a third party. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **ENT-4** Ventures Holding is a fundraising/holding vehicle, not an operating company; its parties are the cap table, lenders, and corp-service firms. *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
- **ENT-5** Related entities are NEVER counterparties — intercompany rides on entity ids; entity names in bank text belong to the transfer/IC machinery. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*

## 2. Products & money flows (FLOW)

- **FLOW-1** Two products, two margins: P2P (owner self-manages; platform fee) and RMS (we manage the car; extra management fee → higher margin). Long-term variants: Flex+ and Flex+ RMS. *(P2P_RMS doc, 2026-03)*
- **FLOW-2** On RMS trips WE are the registered host: the guest's payment is revenue recognized ONCE at Stripe; the "host share" flows to OUR OWN Stripe connected account, then settles into our bank. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-10)*
- **FLOW-3** QB booked that settlement inflow as "Due to Fleet" and payouts to real car owners as "Due from Fleet"; the mock Fleet entity existed to do the consolidation elimination ($100 rev − $80 self-payout ⊕ $80 rev − $60 owner cost ⇒ $100 rev − $60 cost). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-10)*
- **FLOW-4** With Fleet folded away, that elimination happens at mapping: settlement inflows = internal transfers (never P&L); payouts to real RMS owners = 5001. Mapping settlement inflows to revenue would double-count. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-10)*
- **FLOW-5** "Due to SG" exists only in the mock Fleet entity's books (its mirror of SG's "Due from Fleet"); Fleet has no bank account, so those lines never appear in a bank replay. *(verified, 2026-07-10)*
- **FLOW-6** Historical RMS revenue cannot be split from P2P at bank level — booking-level RMS data is required for the 4000 vs 4001 split. *(agreed, 2026-07-10)*
- **FLOW-7** "Ventures-Euro" was the business partner's PERSONAL/bureau EUR account: investors paid into it on behalf of Drive lah; he later transferred to the company. Every conduit flow has TWO legs — investor→partner (the real capital/loan event, recognize once) and partner→company (a settlement transfer, NOT a second contribution). *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
- **FLOW-8** The claims/incidentals world (guest damage) runs its own vendor ecosystem — towing, smash repair, assessors, locksmiths — which entered the live DB via invoice/AP workflows, not bank statements. *(verified, 2026-07-23)*
- **FLOW-9** Remote staff are paid via Wise in currency batches (INR/MYR/AUD) whose memo text describes the batch ("Wise INR Human Resource Salary Payment") — those strings are payment descriptors, not parties; prime rule material (~469 lines). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*

- **CP-2** Dirk-Jan **ter Horst** is the co-founder/director; the name "Dirk-Jan ten Brink" was wrong — never use it. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*

- **CP-24** ~~CPF payments settle the payroll-accrued liability → 2300 CPF Payable.~~ Superseded: **CPF party defaults to 6001 Employer CPF (SG) — expense, not liability** (Gaurav: "counterparty default to expense not liability"); the payroll knock-off still claims CPF lines against runs when they exist. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*

### Investors
- **CP-26** Investors and lenders are ONE type (investor): the same party may hold equity AND convertible loans — the instrument is a property of the transaction (e.g. Stevu Beheer BV holds both). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-27** Ventures' counterparties are 100% the cap table + lenders: share-capital investors → 3000; EUR-conduit contributors (Derkjan Lutgert, Kalle Blom, Marten Hatzmann, Peter Stam, Nick Leijten) default 3000 with two-leg conduit flows (FLOW-7). ComfortDelGro's Ventures line is a loan → 2400. Sebastiaan W Koeling and "Anusha Krishnakumar & Sivaramakrishnan" (joint account) are investors → 3000. *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01 + 2026-07-23)*

### Never counterparties
- **CP-28** Banks and payment rails (Wise, Commonwealth Bank, "Card transaction", "Transfer", Stripe settlement text) are NEVER counterparties. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-29** One-off merchants (restaurants, hotels, petrol, retail) never become counterparties, even when identifiable. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-30** Debt collectors (ABC Debt, Ecollect.com) — removed by choice; lines handled individually. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-32** QB-only payees pre-created (rules existed, no transaction evidence yet): SEON ("SEON1", "EONS TECHNOLOGIES" = fraud-detection SaaS → 6701); Eurokars, Komoco, City Auto, Cheng Auto Body works (SG car dealers/workshops → 5032, some known only by account numbers); LoK ("LOK NGEE", no default). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-33** CIRCLE BUILDS = CircleCI (one global party, 6701; "CIRCLECI.COM" alias). "Biz Giro" is GIRO descriptor text, not a party; "BIZ RR GIRO" is a CPF alias. QB's Dropbox→docsend and docsend→Hotjar rule wirings were errors — discarded. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-35** "Caretakers" are individuals paid to look after RMS cars (historically 5001): Quang Vu Nguyen, Shannon Yeo, Tan Lay Suan Judy, Sabrina Lin Yan Xiu — deliberately NOT pre-created as parties (replay learns them). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-36** Car-listing photographers → 6103 Marketing - Asset Creation: Christian Alvarez, Benjamin Lai, Chuan Quan Koh, Sapna Prakash Dabade, plus an unidentified one known only by bank acct 565251121001. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-37** Ho Sze Yie is called "Jared" — PH support team → 5063. "Ana Ash" may be bank truncation of Anas Ashfaq — verify at replay. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-38** Care Corporation provides the PH team's HEALTH INSURANCE → new account **6004 Staff Health Insurance** (added to COA v2 2026-07-23; distinct from product insurance 5031/5035 and claims 6010-14). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-39** Paddle (merchant-of-record billing platform) deliberately NOT a party — replay learns its lines. Cycle & Carriage is an RMS leasing host → 5001 (live 5003 corrected). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **CP-41** Google sub-products billed as subscriptions keep EXCEPTION rules → 6701 (GSuite/Workspace, Google Play, Google Storage); bare Google/Cloud stays 6700; apple.com store text → 6701 against Apple's 6700 default. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **CP-42** Accounting SOFTWARE (Xero, QuickBooks/Intuit) → 6500 Accounting & Bookkeeping Fees, not 6701. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **CP-43** Founders' party defaults = 6003 Directors Salary (Gaurav Singhal, Dirk-Jan ter Horst). Jay Balan = salaried on-ground staff → 5061 (acct 6218104856). Vighnesh R Vighnesh + Mythri S = employees → 6000; Noraisa Domado + Emmanuel Jacobo = PH support → 5063; "Nipa Riten Mody" = alias of Ankish Mody; Benjamin Gotto Smith = AU employee → 6000 (not the lawyer the mined rule guessed). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **CP-44** Device-connectivity vendors → 5030 (Liberty Wireless joins KORE). Domain registrars → 6701 (GoDaddy joins SGNIC). Dev-tool SaaS → 6701 (Fingerprint, Papertrail, Uptime, Glocksoft/2Checkout, Superblog, Stonly). ComfortDelGro (CDG + Rent-A-Car merged) + Eurokars = RMS leasing hosts → 5001; Sabrina Lin Yan Xiu → 5001; YS Auto works → 5032 (acct 601344831001). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **CP-45** DRIVE LAH AUSTRALIA PAYROLL — ACE provider, AU staff, and Q1/Q2 FY2026 PAYG + super (source: ACE reports in `documentation/wip/ACE Super and PAYG/`, 2026-08-14). **Provider = ACE (`expandwithace.com`)**, outsourced payroll + tax for Drive lah Australia Pty Ltd. **Laura McDaid** — Finance & Payroll Officer (QBO Payroll; monthly pay + quarterly super/PAYG; super paid quarterly by BPay). **Rishabh Chopra** — Accountant, lodges the **quarterly BAS** (requested June-qtr BAS info 2026-07-01). **Edwin Leenstra** — account head; **Simon** — supporting docs. **AU EMPLOYEES (Q2 FY2026 super report — authoritative spellings):** **Craig Letters** (AustralianSuper, member 110259951 — contractor per POL-103), **Matheus Johannes Van der Kooi** (UniSuper, member 14877979 — SAME person as the dual-role vendor+employee "Matheus" user 87, POL-112), **Resya Harahap** (Rest Super, member 710724011). **Q1 FY2026 (Jan–Mar) roster had a 4th: Jacob Hyde** (AustralianSuper) — LEFT before Q2 (absent from Q2 report; last pay 13 Mar). So Q1 payroll = Craig + Jacob Hyde + Resya + Matheus (super ties to $8,857.76); Q2 payroll = Craig + Matheus + Resya. **Su / Pyiee Thin Nwe is NOT AU team (she was SG) — do not include her in AU payroll.** ~~earlier screenshot mis-read them as Craig Lennock / Mathew Johanson / Reece Manansap — WRONG, superseded.~~ **PAYG WITHHOLDING → feeds BAS W1 (gross) / W2 (withheld):** **Q1 FY2026 (Jan–Mar): W1 $87,049.57 · W2 $20,215.00.** **Q2 FY2026 (Apr–Jun): W1 $51,524.18 · W2 $9,802.00** (Apr 16,263.81/3,050 · May 17,251.28/3,285 · Jun 18,009.09/3,467). **SUPER (not a BAS field):** Q1 $8,857.76 · Q2 $6,182.89. - **CP-31** Terminology: they are "counterparties" (parties we do business with — suppliers, investors, employees, related entities), not "vendors". *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
