## 4. Accounting policies & principles (POL)

- **POL-1** Revenue is recognized ONCE at the economic source (Stripe/guest payment); settlement legs between our own accounts are transfers, never P&L. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-10)*
- **POL-2** Anything that moves money (invoice/payroll knock-off matching) is deterministic, never AI. Knock-offs are order-independent (retroactive passes prevent double-count). *(design, 2026-05)*
- **POL-3** The 6700/6701 line: services that HOST or RUN the product → 6700 Infrastructure; tools/APIs bought as subscriptions → 6701. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-4** In-car devices are EXPENSED on purchase → 5030 (not capitalized to 1510); connectivity → 5030; installation → 5064. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-76** Payroll statutory accounts come in expense+liability PAIRS, not two liabilities: AU super = 6002 Employer Superannuation (EXPENSE, P&L) + 2302 Superannuation Payable (LIABILITY, owed to fund); SG mirror = 6001 Employer CPF (EXPENSE) + 2300 CPF Payable (LIABILITY). Ideal accrual: Dr 6002 / Cr 2302, then payment Dr 2302 / Cr bank. (5040 "Host Payouts - Superhost" is UNRELATED — that's superhost payouts, not superannuation.) *(code-verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-75** Invoice approval posts a fixed double-entry: Dr [chosen contra/COA account] / Cr [payable] at invoice_date. ~~the credit leg is HARDCODED to 2000; it does NOT route to a dedicated sub-payable~~ SUPERSEDED by POL-77 (2026-07-31): the credit leg now routes per-account. *(code-verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-77** Per-account AP-credit routing (invoice_service.py `_payable_account_for`): the invoice credit leg posts to `finance_accounts.offset_account_code` of the chosen expense account if set, else generic 2000 Trade & Other Payables. Seeded: 6002 Employer Superannuation → 2302 Superannuation Payable; 6001 Employer CPF → 2300 CPF Payable. Payment JE (approval AND settlement, same- and cross-entity) debits the SAME resolved liability so the sub-payable closes. COA dropdowns only ever show expense/asset/cost-of-sales (never liabilities) — operator picks the expense, system auto-posts the liability leg. To add a new statutory pair, set offset_account_code on the expense account (data-driven, no code change). Migration 048. *(built + verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-78** OFFSET PRINCIPLE (Gaurav ruling, 2026-07-31): the credit (offset) liability of any invoice-created liability is PURELY a function of the chosen COA (expense/COS/asset) — NO counterparty logic. Rationale: the chart already segregates who-you-owe into distinct accounts, so the COA alone decides. `finance_accounts.offset_account_code` is now **NOT NULL DEFAULT '2000'** — every account structurally carries its credit side ("the system always knows the other account, always"). Stamp map: **6010-6014 Employee Claims + 5062 On-Ground Team Expenses → 2303 Employee Claims Payable**; 6002 → 2302 super; 6001 → 2300 CPF; 6000/6003/5061/5063 (salaries — payroll-posted, stamped for safety) → 2304 Salaries Payable; 9000 Income Tax Expense → **2305 Income Tax Payable (newly created)**; everything else → 2000 Trade & Other Payables. GST is NOT stamped (per-line tax split owned by the GST engine, POL-44/task#13). Two-leg flow unchanged: claim Dr expense / Cr offset; payment Dr offset / Cr bank. ~~NOT NULL DEFAULT '2000' on every account~~ SUPERSEDED by mig 050 (Gaurav ruling): offset is scoped to the PAYABLES-BOOKING debit side only — kept on EXPENSE/COST_OF_SALES/ASSET (2000 default + exceptions), set **NULL on REVENUE/LIABILITY/EQUITY**. Resolver falls back to 2000 when offset is NULL, so payables booking is unaffected. Migrations 049+050. *(built + verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-79** OFFSET reverse direction (revenue→receivable) is the mirror of POL-78: Dr receivable / Cr revenue → Dr bank / Cr receivable; default receivable = 1200 Trade Receivables, claims → 1220 Other Receivables. DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT (Gaurav, 2026-07-31) — the offset engine exists only to book PAYABLES; revenue/liability/equity offsets are kept NULL. Note: most Drive lah revenue is recognised at the Stripe source and settles via Stripe bank accounts (1017-1024), so a true trade receivable only applies to invoiced revenue (insurance recoveries, B2B, host charges). *(forward design, not built, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-80** INVOICE COUNTERPARTY MATCHING (Gaurav ruling, 2026-07-31): invoice ingestion matches the extracted counterparty name via a cheap LLM (Haiku) **ALWAYS — never fuzzy string matching**. Candidate set is the ENTIRE counterparty book — every type (vendor/employee/investor/government/customer) and BOTH active AND inactive. Order: exact tax-ID identity match (a hard identifier, kept) → LLM name match → auto-create unverified counterparty if no match. `vendor_matching_service.match_or_create` / `llm_match_vendor`; prompt lists `id: name [type]`. Supersedes the earlier fuzzy-then-Haiku, vendors-only approach. *(built + live-verified: "Jen Reyes"→employee 1.0, "Comfort DelGro Taxi"→ComfortDelGro 0.85, 2026-07-31)*
- **DQ-68** ALIAS/MATCHING AUDIT (read-only, 2026-07-31): replayed categorization L1 (exact/substring on name+aliases, first-match-wins) over all 4242 txns → **189 ambiguous** (≥2 counterparties match). GOOD: 0 currently assigned to a non-matching party (nothing grossly wrong today), but first-match-wins makes it fragile. Two root causes: **(A) Polluted aliases** — raw bank-statement lines dumped into the aliases array. Worst = **520 CPF** (87 ambiguous): its aliases hold a full NGROK card line + `PAYMENT/TRANSFER OTHR S$ <person> PayNow: 201900261M` lines carrying UNRELATED individuals' names. Aliases must be canonical name variants, NOT raw statement dumps. **(B) Residual duplicates the manual CSV missed** — 433 Quickbooks↔302 Intuit Quickbooks, 175↔654 Riezel Basilio Villanueva (exact dup), 163 Craig Anthony Letters↔696 Craig Letters, 126↔652 Rohde Smash Repair(s), 326↔327 WOTSO, 76 GT insurance↔693 Global Transport. 398 Google↔399 Google Ads overlap may be intentional hierarchy (fix by removing GOOGLE*ADS aliases from 398, not merging). Full list: `documentation/wip/alias_ambiguity_audit_20260731.csv`. → RESOLVED same day: see POL-81 (merges + alias clean + tiered matcher took L1 ambiguity 189→0) and DQ-69. *(read-only audit, 2026-07-31)*
- **DQ-69** Bank statement DESCRIPTIONS carry SDIC deposit-insurance disclaimer boilerplate — e.g. an ANTHROPIC card purchase's description ends "…Monies and deposits denominated in Singapore dollars under the **CPF** Investment Scheme and CPF Retirement Sum Scheme are aggregated…". The words "CPF"/"insurance"/"deposit" in that footer falsely substring-match counterparties (CPF, insurers). Mitigated by tiered L1 (POL-81): an exact counterparty_name hit out-ranks a description-footer substring. Could also be fixed at ingestion by stripping the disclaimer. *(DB-verified txn 5450, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-81** COUNTERPARTY TXN MATCHING — tiered L1 + no first-match-wins (built 2026-07-31, per DQ-68 audit). `categorization_service._match_l1` now scores each counterparty by match STRENGTH: Tier1 exact raw counterparty_name == name/alias → Tier2 name/alias substring of counterparty_name → Tier3 substring of DESCRIPTION (weakest — bank footers live here). Unique best-tier match wins; a tie AT the best tier returns ambiguous → routed to L3 LLM (never guessed). Killed the old first-match-wins loop. Combined with (a) 6 duplicate merges (433→302 Quickbooks, 654→175 Riezel, 696→163 Craig, 126→652 Rohde, 327→326 WOTSO, 76→693 GT; 608→602) and (b) global alias clean (raw statement lines stripped: markers debit-purchase/payment-transfer/paynow/value-date/card-xx/giro/fast-payment/direct-credit + Google ADS lines; 1458→687 aliases), L1 ambiguity went 189→0. Existing txn attributions were NOT re-run (forward safeguard; historical had 0 assigned-to-nonmatch). *(built + verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **DQ-67** BUG FIXED (2026-07-31): invoice_service `PREPAID_ACCOUNT_CODE` was `"1200"` but the COA was renumbered so **1200 = Trade Receivables** and **1300 = Prepayments** — multi-month amortization invoices were debiting Trade Receivables instead of Prepayments. Fixed to `"1300"`. (`GST_INPUT_ACCOUNT_CODE="1350"` still correct = GST Receivable.) *(code-verified, 2026-07-31)*
- **POL-5** Development-agency platform work is CAPITALIZED → 1710. In-house R&D-software policy (capitalize vs expense) still open — decide at replay. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-6** Refund-type income is NOT grants: refunds/rebates → 7001; grants → 7000. *(2026-07-23)*
- **POL-7** Security deposits we HOLD from guests/hosts are a liability → 2110 (not the 1310 asset). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-8** Counterparties = entities/people we have a RELATIONSHIP with; one-off transactions never create parties. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-9** Exactly 4 counterparty types: vendor · employee · government · investor. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-10** Scope follows the ERP master-data principle: identity is global (multinationals → one record, `entity_id NULL`); people, local businesses, statutory bodies are entity-scoped; founders/directors get one global record. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-11** One string points at ONE party — canonical names and aliases must never collide across parties (mechanical gate). *(2026-07-23)*
- **POL-12** IDENTITY belongs to enrichment (names + aliases on the party); rules decide ACCOUNTING only — a rule may condition on a counterparty but never assign one. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-13** Rules fire BEFORE counterparty defaults; a rule that merely names a known party is harmful redundancy — such patterns are dropped, the party default governs. *(2026-07-23)*
- **POL-14** A party's default account is a fallback, not a law — multi-role parties exist; rules and knock-offs override per line. *(Gaurav, 2026-06-01)*
- **POL-16** Rules are NEVER deleted — only deactivated (`status=INACTIVE`); the engine evaluates ACTIVE rules only. History stays auditable. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-17** The rules model/engine must not support counterparty as an ACTION (assignment) — counterparty is a CONDITION only; code change queued: engine ignores + validation rejects assignment fields, columns dropped in a later migration. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-23)*
- **POL-18** Prefer a LEAN rule book: for small-count text patterns (travel cards, one-off refunds, A/R collection texts, ex-people), do NOT load convenience rules — replay review + the feedback loop handle them. Rules are for high-value deterministic streams only. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **POL-19** Historical payroll is NEVER reconstructed as runs — historical salary/statutory lines book as direct expense via party defaults; real payroll runs begin H2-2026. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **POL-20** The AP/accrual leg exists only where invoice data exists (≈Jul-2025 onward); earlier periods book expense-on-payment — the accrual+settlement pair nets to the same P&L. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **POL-21** QuickBooks' trial balance is a CROSS-CHECK REFERENCE, not the source of truth — QB carries known errors (mis-payees, generic buckets, wrong accounts) and our arbitrated treatments deliberately diverge (Fleet elimination, RMS, Arena, device expensing…). Diffs are adjudicated case-by-case; the finalised finance-api statements become the new truth once Gaurav signs off. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-24)*
- **POL-22** Counterparty status semantics: `active` = current relationship; `inactive` = DORMANT but real (ex-vendor/ex-employee) — enrichment uses inactive parties too, because historical transactions must still categorize against them. Records that are WRONG (artifacts like a party named after a bank-text prefix, e.g. "Debit Purchase") are DELETED outright, never kept inactive. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-25)*
- **POL-23** Test charges on our OWN platforms (company-card test bookings on Drive lah / Drive mate — bank text "issued by Drive Mate", "DRIVE MATE MEL") book to **6702 Technology - Platform Testing** as an operating expense — NOT intercompany, and never a counterparty (ENT-3/ENT-5). Both directions (charges and test refunds) hit 6702. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-25)*
- **POL-24** Transfer-rule law: EVERY internal-transfer corridor gets exactly TWO rules — one per side, each scoped to its bank account(s). The side that KNOWS the counterpart carries `target_bank_account_id` and books the one JE; the side that CANNOT know (e.g. a Wise top-up can't tell which bank funded it) is CLAIM-ONLY (target empty): it claims the txn as a transfer in Phase 0.5 — so enrichment/AI can never misread it as P&L — and gets attached to the knowing side's JE. One movement = one JE, two statement lines. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-25)*
- **POL-25** Functional currencies: DL Singapore = SGD, DL Australia = AUD, Ventures = SGD (Ventures transacts largely in USD but standalone reporting is always SGD, so books are SGD). Every JE books its debit/credit in the entity's functional currency, converted AT BOOKING TIME at the transaction-date rate; every journal line also preserves native currency + native amount + fx_rate (audit, knock-offs, recomputation). Reporting-time conversion exists ONLY at the consolidation layer, which always presents in USD. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-26)*
- **POL-26** FX-rate regime (simple-but-world-class, per IAS 21 approximation): ① monthly STANDARD rate per currency pair, set on the 1st from a public mid-rate, used for all foreign bookings that month; ② statement ACTUALS override for transfers/conversions where the bank shows both legs (the two amounts define the rate); ③ ONE FX gain/loss P&L account absorbs knock-off/settlement differences (Wise spread becomes visible); ④ month-end revaluation of foreign-currency monetary balances DEFERRED (disclosed simplification at current scale); ⑤ consolidation eventually average-rate P&L / closing-rate BS from the same table. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-26)*
- **POL-27** Intercompany law: IC entries exist ONLY where cash crossed entity lines — exactly three cases, all system-generated: ① direct entity-to-entity transfers (cross-entity corridor rules, two linked JEs, knock-off at statement-actual rate per POL-26, spread → FX gain/loss); ② cross-entity INVOICE settlement (invoice is addressed to an entity — document fact; payer books Dr IC–owner / Cr Bank, owner books Dr Expense / Cr IC–payer); ③ cross-entity PAYROLL settlement (run belongs to the employer entity, same pattern). Knock-off SEARCH is global (payment can come from any entity's bank); invoices/payroll runs stay entity-owned. Undocumented cross-entity benefit books as the PAYER'S OWN EXPENSE — no IC, no discretionary on-behalf marking, no periodic allocations (they do not exist in this system; material cross-entity costs get an intercompany invoice instead). IC accounts: SIX net accounts (one per counterpart entity per book — sign = who owes whom), replacing the twelve QB-era due-from/due-to 8xxx accounts; mirroring = three pair comparisons after currency translation. **CREATED 2026-07-26 (entity-scoped, category Intercompany): 8200 IC-Australia (SG) · 8201 IC-Ventures (SG) · 8210 IC-Singapore (AU) · 8211 IC-Ventures (AU) · 8220 IC-Singapore (Ventures) · 8221 IC-Australia (Ventures).** IC rule book LOCKED: twelve rules IC-1..IC-12 (new category INTERCOMPANY_TRANSFER, full-entity-name patterns per DQ-16, account-scoped, Ventures legs carry ≥1,000 amount floor, cross-entity pairing = two linked JEs knocked off at statement-actual rate per POL-26). ALL five QB relic rules (#155/#202/#23/#235/#236) RETIRED 2026-07-26. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-26)*
- **POL-28** Midway-accounting opening balances ("conversion balances", Gaurav 2026-07-26): books open at 2026-01-01 with BANK/CASH balances only — one POSTED JE per entity (source `opening_balance`, dated 2026-01-01), each bank COA debited at its statement-proven pre-first-txn balance (derived: first imported txn's running_balance − amount; Stripe Platform = ClickHouse `sum(net)` before 2026; foreign-currency accounts converted at the Jan-2026 standard rate with native preserved), plug to **3200 Opening Balance Equity**. ALL other assets/liabilities/equity open at ZERO — a disclosed simplification: the BS is cash-complete but 2026-forward otherwise; some zero-opening accounts may go "impossibly" negative (pre-2026 deposit refunds, prior-quarter GST) — that is missing history showing itself, not an error. **Phase B (2019→2025 replay) later replaces the opening JEs with real history and dissolves 3200 into true retained earnings/capital**; interim upgrade available anytime by lifting QB Dec-31-2025 conversion balances for big non-cash items. Booked 2026-07-26: JE 2460 (SG, plug 66,142.08 SGD) · JE 2461 (AU, plug 202,771.59 AUD) · JE 2462 (Ventures, plug −95.07 SGD). SKIPPED pending data: Wise PKR (no txns + no rate) · Ventures Wise SGD/USD/AUD + DBS USD/EUR (no 2026 data imported; needs Nov-Dec 2025 DBS statements) · Stripe Connect (no feed by design, POL-24). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-26)*
- **POL-29** CUSTOMERS (hosts/guests — people who receive payouts from us or pay us in the ordinary course of the platform) NEVER get counterparty records — no point; the directory is for parties we manage: vendors, employees, investors, government, banks. Customer flows book straight to their P&L accounts (e.g. 5025 incidental payouts, 4000/4025 receipts) with identity left in the bank text; repeat customers ride the corpus/AI, not the directory. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-28a** In-transit amendment to POL-28 (found via the Stripe recon, Gaurav-approved 2026-07-27): a Stripe-opening equals pre-2026 Σnet **plus payouts in transit at Jan-1** (payout left Stripe pre-2026 but landed in the bank in 2026 — the bank leg books against Stripe again, so without the in-transit line the opening double-counts the payout). Booked: JE 6233, Dr 1017 25,000 / Cr 3200 (SG payout txn_1SkJk6 left Dec-31, landed OCBC 3001 Jan-2). Same logic applies to ANY account pair straddling the opening date. Also: balance-date law — a displayed balance is ALWAYS as of the SYNCED-THROUGH date (coverage), never the last-transaction date; synced_through == balance_as_of by construction, and a divergent pair is a bug. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-32** Insurance payment classification (Gaurav rule, 2026-07-27): a direct payment to an insurer **above ~5–10k = ALWAYS a premium** (5035/5031); below that = **claim excess/deductible (5036)**. SG specifics: Tokio Marine's repeated 3,270 = the standard per-claim excess; Income Insurance payments (0.5–6k) = excesses; Tokio 10k/20k = premiums. AU: premiums are paid "all over the place" (broker DEFT annual 69,472 + assorted; invoices will clarify at A-1) — but the >5k rule still holds; sub-5k Hollard/GT/Allianz payments = excesses. Insurer *incoming* = claims recoveries or commissions (SG Income commissions → 7000). Gray zone 5–10k: judge by shape/invoice. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-33** Phase-A categorization floor (Gaurav, 2026-07-27): the engine NEVER categorizes any transaction dated before the books-open date **2026-01-01** — enforced in `categorization_service` (`BOOKS_OPEN_DATE`, applied even under explicit txn_ids). Pre-2026 rows (e.g. the 358 all-history Stripe payout lines pulled by the balance-transaction sync, 229 SG back to 2019 + 129 AU back to 2022) are ALREADY inside the opening balances, so booking them would double-count. They are LEFT AS-IS (IMPORTED, untouched) and belong to the **Phase B** historical replay, which will categorize them as part of rebuilding real pre-2026 history. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-34** Counterparty-default DIRECTION guard (Gaurav, 2026-07-27): every counterparty TYPE has a normal money direction, and a default account auto-books ONLY when money flows that way — vendor/employee/government = OUT (we pay them), investor = IN (they pay us), bank = both, customers never in the directory (POL-29). A wrong-direction hit is an exception — a vendor paying us = refund, us paying an investor = loan repayment/dividend, an employee paying us = claw-back — and routes to REVIEW, never books blind against the default (this is the root-cause fix for the GLOBAL-TRANSPORT / CPF / poisoned-alias misbooking class). Enforced in `categorization_service._default_direction_ok` (CP_TYPE_NORMAL_DIRECTION map); `type` is a REQUIRED field on every counterparty (model nullable=False + CounterpartyCreate `type: str = Field(...)`), so the guard always has its input. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-35** THE THREE PHASES (locked, Gaurav 2026-07-28) — the master timeline for all finance-api work: **Phase A = H1-2026** (Jan–Jun 2026, the current pilot; midway-accounting opening balances per POL-28, cash-complete books, the base that must be right before anything else). **Phase B = 2019–2025 historical replay** (rebuild real pre-2026 history; replaces the POL-28 opening JEs, categorizes the reserved pre-2026 rows per POL-33). **Phase C = going-forward from July-2026 onward** (live operations: continuous categorization, live payroll, scheduled Stripe sync, per-transaction GST split). **WE ARE IN PHASE A.** Every policy that differs by era (GST method, opening balances, categorization floor) states its Phase-A / B / C behaviour explicitly. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-36** AGENT / NET REVENUE (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — Drive lah is an **AGENT** on the marketplace, not the principal: on the trip/rental leg the HOST supplies the car to the guest and Drive lah merely facilitates. Consequences, all locked together: ① **Revenue = Drive lah's commission / service fee (net take-rate)**, NOT gross GBV; the host's share is a **pass-through** (collected on the host's behalf, owed via Host Payables, never Drive lah revenue or COGS). ② **GST is on the commission only** (our taxable supply), which keeps aggregated GST turnover under the **$10M** threshold → **cash-basis BAS is permitted** (POL — see the GST design) → net GST **refund** position (input GST on our costs > output GST on commission). ③ Some streams may be PRINCIPAL (Drive lah's own supply — subscriptions, insurance products, some incidentals) → those stay GROSS revenue; the agency-vs-principal split per stream is the open mapping. The old accountant's FY2025 file treated Drive lah as principal (GST on gross GBV → net payable) — that was the **incorrect** calculation Gaurav discarded; the revised 2026 file uses cash-basis agent treatment → ~19k/qtr refund. This is the same call as CFO finding AUD-A. Applies A/B/C; H1 (Phase A) currently books GROSS and must be re-presented to net. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-37** REVENUE + GST COMPUTATION ARCHITECTURE (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — one category-level dataset (the economic-event views; each event_type = a category), aggregated THREE ways that never conflict: ① **P&L / valuation = ACCRUAL** — revenue earned when the trip happens, matched against **accrued host payable** (a liability earned per activity, NOT the cash payout); net revenue = gross earned − accrued host share, computed **per category** with **per-category take-rates** (trip take-rate at trip level ≠ incidentals ≠ subscription); GMV (gross) + take-rate reported on top for valuation. ② **GST output = CASH COLLECTED** — output GST = 1/11 of the net commission portion of cash actually collected in the quarter (cash-basis, agent per POL-36). ③ **GST input = CASH PAID** — input GST on expenses actually paid (tax-invoice rules: <A$82.50 no invoice needed, ≥A$82.50 need a valid tax invoice with ABN). GST refund = input − output (net refund position). **Host has TWO distinct numbers: accrued host payable (P&L) vs actual monthly cash host payout (settlement + GST).** They reconcile over a period; hosts are paid MONTHLY in both SG and AU. Categories confirmed roughly: collections = trip fare · incidentals (distance/excess/tolls/fuel) · subscriptions · insurance recoveries (4030, on top); payouts = host trip earnings · host damage payouts · host fuel/tolls. Sources: gross from Stripe views, host figure from the payout entry sheet. Stripe fees stay a separate expense (not netted). Refunds and customer deposits (2110) + GST are excluded from gross. **P&L (accrual) and GST (cash) are kept deliberately separate** — the AU cash-basis-agent requirement. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-30** NO IC pair-linking (amends POL-27's "two linked JEs" and retires the A-18 pair-linking task): each entity books its own IC leg INDEPENDENTLY against its net IC account — no formal cross-book link is created or needed. Consolidation's IC elimination nets the accounts automatically; the mirror-tie check (three pair comparisons after translation) is the completeness control; any post-translation residual = FX spread (trued up periodically to 7100) or a missing/in-transit leg (already flagged by bank-side AWAITING pairing + the recon checkpoint). Rationale (Gaurav): linking adds bookkeeping machinery with no accounting effect. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-31** Transfer-pairing law (extends POL-24; from the 2026-07-27 mispair postmortem): transfers enter the lane ONLY via transfer rules (Phase 0.5, before enrichment/AI); the KNOWING side books the single JE, the other line ATTACHES to it. Candidate selection: different account (a pair inside one account is impossible) + opposite sign + ±2% amount + ±5 days; ties broken ONLY by evidence — shared bank-reference token (DQ-14 CT refs) then tightest date; if evidence can't decide, the engine REFUSES to guess and leaves the leg awaiting for a human (a wrong staple costs hours, a non-guess costs one click). Both-sides-know corridors (e.g. C1 #2/#26): the second knowing rule must attach to the counterpart's existing awaiting JE, never book a second JE for the same cash. Staged IMPORTED rows are valid counter-candidates (out-of-window legs, e.g. Dec-31 payout → Jan-2 bank arrival). Known open gap: 1-to-N arrivals (100k sent, credited as 2×50k) defeat exact-amount pairing — manual re-pair, automate only if it recurs. *(Gaurav walkthrough, 2026-07-27)*
- **POL-15** A counterparty seed loads by UPSERT (normalized name + aliases); never blind-insert against a live directory. *(2026-07-23)*

- **CP-35** DIRECTOR/RELATED-PARTY LOANS (Gaurav, 2026-07-27): "Loan J ter Horst" (485,000) and the recurring "Loan DJ" series (141,230 + 50,000 + 50,000 + 15,000 = 256,230) are ALL from **Dirk-Jan ter Horst** (cp 268, existing EMPLOYEE/director, salary default 6003) — total 741,230 AUD. The 71,520 USD Ventures DBS loan (txn 11235) is **Gaurav's own director loan** (cp 260). Both booked to NEW account **2405 Related-Party / Director Loans** (distinct from 2400 arm's-length, 2410 convertibles). These people are MULTI-ROLE (salary out → 6003; loans in → 2405); POL-34's direction guard correctly sends their incoming money to review rather than the salary default. **The Mar-27 42,000 "Direct Credit 507141 Misc" is NOT a loan** — payer ID 507141 = the Drivemate platform (same ID as the DrivematePayout credits); it's platform money labelled "Misc", VOIDED out of 2400 and returned to review — Gaurav to identify (OPEN). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **CP-34** FIN-NOR HOLDINGS LIMITED = an INVESTOR holding a **convertible loan** in Drive lah (H1'26 receipts 470,430 AUD into AU, booked 2400). Convertible notes are debt-with-conversion — at AUD-D resolution consider a dedicated account (e.g. 2410 Convertible Notes) separate from ordinary loans, plus terms/interest/conversion conditions from the note documents. Investor money from Fin-Nor is always financing — never revenue. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **FLOW-13** AU CUSTOMER-DEPOSIT holding account (Gaurav, 2026-07-27): a dedicated **Stripe Connect custom account** (`acct_1JkKmQQWb9mOwfae`, registered singhal.gaurav@gmail.com but it is a COMPANY deposit-holding account, NOT Gaurav's personal money) holds guests' security deposits off-ledger. Periodic manual sweeps to the company CBA bank arrive as "Direct Credit 507141 Misc" (507141 = the Drivemate/Stripe platform payer id; also on the small routine DrivematePayout credits — the distinguisher is the large amount + "Misc"). A sweep books **Dr bank / Cr 2110 Customer Deposits Held** (a liability owed back to guests — never revenue, never a loan). H1: 42,000 AUD swept 2026-03-27 (txn 7118, JE re-booked to 2110). Rare + generic wording → NO auto-rule; recognise via this fact + review. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-27)*
- **FLOW-20** FOUR SUBSCRIPTION TYPES (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — every Stripe invoice with `subscription IS NOT NULL` is a subscription, of FOUR kinds, currently ALL lumped into `subscription_invoiced` → 4010: ① **Flex+** (long-term rental) — line item contains "flex+" — AU H1 **111,502** / SG **22,899** (SG subs are MOSTLY Flex+) ② **Device** (Drive lah GO / Good Mates / Best Mates / GPS / lockbox / keyless / BYOD, host-charged per FLOW-14) — AU **84,952** / SG small ③ **Insurance** — line item "insurance"/"insure" — AU **42,571** / SG none ④ **Payment plans** — signal: `metadata.payment_plan` (a sub id) OR line item "payment plan for <name>" — AU **21,243** (320 invoices) / SG none. **SIGNALS live in BOTH the line-item description AND the invoice metadata.** Targets: Flex+ → 4002 · Device → 4010 · Insurance → 4011 · Payment plans → NOT revenue (see FLOW-21). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-21** PAYMENT PLANS are a DOUBLE-COUNT (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — payment plans are Stripe subscriptions created to let a guest pay an EXISTING invoice (usually an incidental) in installments. The original invoice is ALREADY recognized as revenue (incidentals_invoiced → 4025 + 1200 receivable). Today the payment-plan installment invoices ALSO land in `subscription_invoiced` → 4010 as NEW revenue = **double-count** (AU H1 ~21,243). CORRECT treatment: payment-plan installments **SETTLE the original invoice's receivable** (Dr bank / Cr 1200), NOT new revenue — exclude them from subscription revenue entirely. AU links the installment to the original invoice via `metadata.payment_plan` / "payment plan for" line item; the exact original-invoice back-link is the thing to nail so the settlement hits the right receivable. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-22** SUBSCRIPTION SPLIT — build at VIEW level (Gaurav authorized me to create new ClickHouse views, 2026-07-28). Split `subscription_invoiced` (the "voice"/subscription view) into type-specific views (flexplus/device/insurance) + carve payment plans OUT of revenue (settle receivable, FLOW-21). ALSO the crucial `host_subscription_collected` view (device subs DEDUCTED from host payouts) needs the same type-distinguishability in future — most of it is device today, but make it splittable later. **Flex+ P2P/RMS split is HARD and DEFERRED: Flex+ has NO connect account, so the connect-account rule (FLOW-18/19) does NOT work for it.** Instead the mechanism must be derived from the **car REGISTRATION NUMBER** on the Flex+ invoice → look up our LISTING data → find the car's owner → P2P vs RMS. Messy now, improve later; for H1 Flex+ mechanism split may be approximate or held. **BUILT 2026-07-28: 5 MECE type-views per market (`view_{AU,SG}_a_subscription_{flexplus,device,insurance,payment_plan,other}_invoiced`), each tying to the cent vs the parent (AU 325,569.71 · SG 25,205.36). AU: Flex+ 125,726 / device 98,554 / insurance 46,521 / payment-plan 45,628 / other 9,141. SG: Flex+ 22,899 (91%!) / device 187 / other 2,120 (no insurance/payment-plans).** REMAINING: ① wire the type-views into events (Flex+→4002, insurance→4011, device→4010; **payment-plan view → receivable-settlement event Dr bank/Cr 1200, NOT revenue** per FLOW-21) ② resolve the "Other" bucket (AU 9,141 / SG 2,120 — invoice `lines` lazy-loaded as a URL, need the line detail) ③ RETIRE the broad `subscription_invoiced` views (both markets) once wired ④ Flex+ P2P/RMS via registration-number→listing (deferred) ⑤ same type-distinguishability for `host_subscription_collected` (payout-deduction, mostly device) later. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-14** DEVICE SUBSCRIPTIONS are ALWAYS charged to the HOST, never to guests (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — the telematics-device subscription is a host cost, and it is Drive lah revenue → 4010 Subscription Revenue - Device. **TWO collection mechanisms, both valid and both must exist:** ① **invoice** — `subscriptions_invoiced` (Dr 1200 Receivable / Cr 4010, recognizes revenue once) settled by `subscriptions_paid` (Dr bank / Cr 1200, clears the receivable — NOT revenue again); ② **payout-deduction** — `host_subscription_collected` (Dr 2120 Host Payables / Cr 4010, one entry: reduces what we owe the host AND recognizes revenue). Both credit 4010 **once each for DIFFERENT hosts** → additive, NOT a double count, PROVIDED the two source views cover disjoint host-months. **Live H1'26: SG uses BOTH (invoiced 25,205.36 + deducted 6,413.03); AU uses ONLY invoicing (325,569.71; deduction event wired but zero data).** Open check: confirm the invoiced-view and host-collected-view populations don't overlap on the same host-month (would be a real double count). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **ENT-6 / FLOW-15** INCIDENTALS CATEGORY TAXONOMY (future) = the **tms-incidentals-service** (IMS) — **NOT live yet (going live ~2 weeks, Gaurav 2026-07-28); use it for the CATEGORY list only, not GST or live wiring.** Config-driven NOT a fixed enum. Canonical taxonomy = `ims_incidental_type_config` table (replica of `docs/prd/IMS - Incident config final.xlsx`): **29 top-level `code` values** + many `subCode`s. The 29 codes (verbatim, incl. real typos): accident_damage · car_functionl_issue_at_pickup · car_functionl_issue_post_pickup · car_in_maintenance · car_rules_not_followed · car_stolen · chargeback_via_payment_provider · cleanliness_issues_at_pickup · cleanliness_issues_post_pickup · deposit_not_paid_back · distance · excess_mileage · fuel_refund · fuel_related_issues · guest_cancellation_unforceen_circumstances · guest_incapacitated · host_cancellation_penalty · illegal_activity · infringement · key_related_issues_during_trip · key_related_issues_post_trip · late_return · not_able_to_access_the_car · not_able_to_locate_car · pickup_technical_failure · previous_trip_car_not_returned · tolls · unauthorised_driver · wear_and_tear. Direction per type = **8 boolean legs** on the config (charge_guest · refund_guest · pay_guest_beyond_refund · charge_host · payout_host · charge_third_party · pay_third_party · non_finance_system_actions) executed in fixed order; third-party = workshop/towing/assessor (payee_user_id, not first-class entities). IMS is pricing-agnostic (amounts only: amountGuestMinor/amountHostDeltaMinor/amountPlatformDeltaMinor); Pricing+PGW compute money. A naive finance list (tolls/damage/cleaning/fuel/mileage/other) MISSES: fines/infringements, smoking/pets/odour, late-return chains, chargebacks, cancellation penalties, fuel_refund (a credit), fraud/illegal-activity, key/access, car-functional. *(agent-extracted from tms-incidentals-service, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-38** INCIDENTALS GST — **NOT YET SETTLED (Gaurav, 2026-07-28).** IMS is NOT live (going live ~2 weeks) and its GST treatment is **not built yet** — so IMS is NOT the authority for incidentals GST; use IMS for the CATEGORY list only. The general principle from POL-36 still holds (Drive lah is an agent → GST on our commission slice, AU only, SG none for now), and the current-system payout data suggests host-incidental deductions carry 0% GST with GST only on the DL fee — but this is **provisional**, to be finalised when IMS's GST treatment is designed. Do NOT hard-code incidentals GST off IMS. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-16** DISTANCE vs EXCESS MILEAGE — two DIFFERENT things, must not be conflated (Gaurav, 2026-07-28). Drive lah has TWO trip business models: ① **standard** — guest pays for DURATION + an ALLOWED MILEAGE; exceed duration → **late return** charge, exceed mileage → **excess mileage** charge (an incidental penalty). ② **per-km** — guest pays DURATION + **DISTANCE separately** (a per-kilometre charge). The per-km model was historically AU **all trips**; NOW it is AU **hourly trips only** (not daily/long-term). **A distance charge is a REVENUE component of the FARE, NOT an incidental** — so it must be treated as trip/base revenue, distinct from excess-mileage (which IS an incidental). Today both flow to 4024 Incidentals-Excess Mileage (`trip_distance_*` events) — that conflation needs splitting: distance-charge → trip fare revenue; excess-mileage → incidental. Applies historically (AU all-trips) and forward (AU hourly). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-17** FUEL — two flows, opposite directions (Gaurav, 2026-07-28): ① **fuel shortage** — guest returns the car with less fuel → **charge guest, payout host** (standard incidental). ② **fuel refund** — under the per-km business model the guest is **reimbursed for fuel they put in** (the cost flows the other way: guest buys fuel → we refund the guest, charge is reversed). So the Fuel category must carry BOTH a charge direction (shortage) and a refund/reimbursement direction (per-km model). IMS has both `fuel_related_issues` (charge) and `fuel_refund` (credit). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-39** LOCKED revenue + incidentals CATEGORY SET (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — the definitive finance categories going forward (each incidental holds BOTH legs — charge/pay guest and charge/pay host — per direction). **Trip/fare revenue:** ① Base Trip Revenue (duration fare) — **INCLUDES the distance charge; NOT separated (Gaurav REVERSED 2026-07-28: getting historical distance is too hard — part paid directly in Stripe, part via raised invoices — and splitting that further by P2P/RMS is impractical, so distance charge stays PART OF base fare, one account).** Distance charge and base fare are the same thing (4000 P2P / 4001 RMS); the only split on Regular is P2P vs RMS. ~~② Distance Charge (separate)~~ RETIRED. **Incidentals:** ③ Damage · ④ Cleaning · ⑤ Fuel Charges (guest returns less fuel → charge guest/pay host) · ⑥ Fuel Refunds (per-km reimbursement → refund guest; split from charges per FLOW-17) · ⑦ Tolls · ⑧ Excess Mileage (allowance-overage penalty, distinct from Distance Charge) · ⑨ Fines/Infringements · ⑩ Late Return · ⑪ Cancellation Penalties · ⑫ Other/Misc · ⑬ Chargebacks (cost-side 5051). **Damage-handling costs (cost-only sub-lines):** Workshop · Towing · Assessor (the pay_third_party leg). Subscriptions (device, host-charged, FLOW-14) + Insurance recoveries (4030) sit outside this list. New categories vs today's COA: Distance Charge (to fare revenue), Fuel Refunds (split), Fines, Late Return, Cancellation Penalties. NEXT: build the COA accounts + event mappings for the new ones, then map H1 + history onto this set. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-40** PRODUCT × MECHANISM taxonomy LOCKED (Gaurav, 2026-07-28): **2 PRODUCTS** = **Regular** (daily/hourly rentals, the core) + **Flex+** (long-term rentals, sold as recurring subscriptions). **2 MECHANISMS** = **P2P** (owner self-manages; Drive lah is agent; commission/net) + **RMS** (Drive lah manages the car on the owner's behalf; we are the registered host per FLOW-2; extra management fee → higher margin; settlement inflow to our own connected account is an INTERNAL TRANSFER not revenue, real-owner payout = cost). The four GBV accounts = product×mechanism: 4000 Regular×P2P · 4001 Regular×RMS · 4002 Flex+×P2P · 4003 Flex+×RMS (matched host payouts 5000/5001/5002/5003). **HARD RULE: GBV revenue accounts (4000-4003) are fed ONLY by ClickHouse/Stripe economic-event views — NOTHING from bank transactions may post to them** (a bank receipt hitting GBV = a leak to fix). Same discipline for the host-payout accounts. **4000 GBV bank leak found H1: 40,272 (rule #362 PayNow→4000 ×257 = 38,361 + cp-default 1,911) — these are guest bank-payments for INVOICES/INCIDENTALS paid outside Stripe (small: 163 under $100), NOT trip GBV → they settle the receivable (1200), must come OUT of 4000.** **5001 Host-RMS's 191,808 of bank postings is NOT a leak — CORRECTED:** the counterparties are the real RMS fleet/leasing owners whose cars we manage (Bombora 109,956 · Cycle and Carriage 63,050 · ComfortDelGro Rent-A-Car 10,111 · Abwin Leasing · Norman Chan) — RMS owner payouts are legitimately paid VIA BANK (per FLOW-18), so 5001-via-bank is by design. 4001/4002/4003 + 5003 have NO revenue/payout events (RMS + Flex+ revenue unrecognized; FLOW-6: RMS rev needs booking-level data). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **ENT-7** OUR STRIPE CONNECT ACCOUNTS — definitive identifier is the LINKED BANK (Gaurav, 2026-07-28, verified via Stripe API): **SG = OCBC ****1001** (DL Pte Ltd operating account = coa 1000/ba1) · **AU = CBA ****7311** (Drive lah Australia = coa 1015/ba17, the "Savings (7311)" in QB). EVERY Drive-lah-owned connect account settles to that one bank per country — reliable signal, immune to the name typos ("DRIVE LAH PTE. LTD." vs "drive lah" etc.). Account TYPES, all ours: **RMS** (`rms{n}.drivelah` SG ~14 · `rms{n}.drivemate` AU ~13) · **Flex+** (`*.flexplus@` SG ~60, the long-term product's own accounts) · **caretaker** (`*.caretaker@` SG ~4) · **deposit** (AU acct_1JkKmQ singhal.gaurav) · **verification** (SG acct_1Skjf3K it@drivelah.sg → OCBC ****3001). **CALCULATION RULE (Gaurav): for RMS we group RMS + Flex+ + caretaker accounts TOGETHER (all "our-managed"); DEPOSIT + VERIFICATION stay SEPARATE (both SG and AU).** So: a trip settling to a connect account whose linked bank = OCBC 1001 / CBA 7311 (excluding the deposit + verification accounts) = RMS/managed; anyone else = P2P. Exceptions to confirm: a few SG accounts named DL but linked to PERSONAL DBS banks (****5583/0391/5088/1441/9081/5363/2590/6335/4258) — caretaker-personal-payout or need checking. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-19** P2P/RMS SPLIT METHOD — view-free, verified & posted (Gaurav, 2026-07-28): the trip-revenue view stays untouched (feeds the TOTAL to 4000); the RMS slice is computed by joining `au/sg_transactions.providerId` (the host) to OUR connect accounts' `metadata.sharetribe-user-id` (the RMS+Flex++caretaker pool by bank OCBC 1001 / CBA 7311; deposit + verification excluded) using the SAME view join/filter logic, then a monthly reclass moves it **Dr 4000 / Cr 4001**. **Ties to the cent** vs the view total (AU 1,582,708 base · SG 3,101,695). **DISTANCE rule: distance is NOT split at source (too hard — paid part direct-Stripe, part invoiced); instead the month's distance revenue is split by that month's BASE P2P/RMS ratio.** H1 posted: SG RMS 85,812.30 (2.8%, base only); AU RMS 211,342.36 (base 176,799 + distance-by-ratio 34,543; 11.2%). 12 monthly reclass JEs (source 'reclassification'). Same method extends to Flex+ (4002) later. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **FLOW-18** P2P vs RMS is recognized by the STRIPE CONNECT ACCOUNT (Gaurav, 2026-07-28): Drive lah holds connect accounts in ITS OWN name (with its own bank) in both SG and AU. **If a trip's settlement lands in OUR connect account → it's RMS** (we're the registered host, we manage the car); **if it lands in the real owner's connect account → it's P2P**. For RMS the settlement inflow to our connect account flows into our cash and is an INTERNAL TRANSFER, not revenue (FLOW-2/3/4 treatment). **RMS host (real-owner) payouts are a MIX: via the system (payout entry sheet) OR via the bank account** — both are valid, so bank postings to 5001 Host-RMS (to fleet/leasing owners like Bombora, Cycle & Carriage, ComfortDelGro, Abwin Leasing) are legitimate, NOT leaks. This is the opposite of GBV revenue accounts (4000-4003), which must be Stripe-only. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-41** DIRECT GUEST BANK PAYMENTS (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — when a guest pays us **directly into a bank account** (e.g. PayNow into OCBC, outside Stripe), for NOW book it straight to **incidentals revenue** — it does **NOT** settle a receivable. **Receivable settlement happens ONLY through Stripe** (the `_c_` cash views are Stripe-settlement-based, carrying Stripe fees). So bank guest-payments = new revenue, never a 1200 settlement, for now. This fixes the 4000 GBV leak: rule #362's PayNow receipts move OFF 4000 (Stripe-trip-only) → incidentals revenue. **FUTURE STREAM (at IMS launch):** properly match direct bank payments to open vs existing invoices and settle correctly — deferred, noted. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-42** SPLIT-AT-SOURCE, ONE-TO-ONE MAPPING (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — **ALL splitting logic lives at the SOURCE (ClickHouse views), NEVER in finance-api.** No revenue/category/mechanism logic may sit at the finance-api end. The relationship between a source view and a finance-api target (GL account / economic-event template) MUST be **one-to-one**: each view maps to exactly one account, so if the source changes we simply re-point one mapping — the finance-api side stays pure plumbing with zero business logic. This is now the governing law for the whole revenue re-architecture. Consequences: (a) the **P2P/RMS split for Regular rentals**, currently done as monthly reclass JEs at finance-api (Dr 4000/Cr 4001), is WRONG under this rule and must be moved INTO the views (source-split, one view per product×mechanism); (b) **Flex+ P2P/RMS split** must likewise be built at source; (c) **incidentals invoiced type-split** must be a set of source views (one per incident type → one GL account), never a finance-api classifier; (d) SG and AU are treated as **separate view families** — no shared logic (SG has no distance charge, different structure). *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **DQ-20** INCIDENTALS INVOICED — line-item signal + traps (exploration, 2026-07-28). Stripe invoice line items are already stored in `au/sg_stripe_invoices.lines` (full JSON blob); unnest via `ARRAY JOIN JSONExtractArrayRaw(assumeNotNull(lines),'data')`. Each line carries `description` (the type signal — populated ~everywhere), `price.product` (stable prod_ id), per-line `amount` + `tax_amounts`. Invoice-level `metadata.category` is sparse (~10-15%); **line `description` is the real signal.** Traps found: ① **Flex+ LEAK** — Flex+ billed as one-off lines with `subscription` NULL (AU ~$74k / SG ~$48k) → must route to Flex+ revenue, out of incidentals; ② **GST line** — SG invoices carry an explicit "GST" line inside `lines` (SG ~$26k) = tax, must be pulled out, never booked as revenue; ③ **"payment for remaining balance"** (AU ~$91k / SG ~$20k) = likely a prior-receivable settlement (double-count risk), needs ruling; ④ SG `distanceTransactionId` is NEVER populated (0 rows) — SG distance/mileage/late-return live only in line descriptions; ⑤ SG `incidentals_invoiced` has a 2nd arm of direct `sg_stripe_charges` (no invoice → no line items) that can't be line-split. First-pass keyword bucketing covers ~82% AU / ~86% SG by value. *(exploration, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-43** LOCKED INCIDENTALS CHART OF ACCOUNTS (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — the global incidentals-revenue account set, aligned to the IMS 29-code taxonomy (ENT-6/FLOW-15). **4020** Tolls · **4021** Damage (absorbs excess/repair/loss-of-use/breach/third-party/wear-and-tear/key/car-stolen — Gaurav: loss-of-use & breach NOT separate) · **4022** Cleaning · **4023** Fuel **Charge** · **4029** Fuel **Refund** (contra — per-km reimbursement to guest; fuel MUST be two accounts, opposite directions, never netted — FLOW-17) · **4024** Excess Mileage (SG excess-mileage is a penalty, NOT base fare — SG has no distance charge; must be matched BEFORE 'damage' in the classifier since 'excess' collides) · **4031** Late Return (its own account, separate from Penalty) · **4027** Penalty / Late-Payment Fee (financing + rules: 3%/month late fee, 20% admin fee, unauthorised_driver, car_rules_not_followed, illegal_activity, host_cancellation_penalty, previous_trip_car_not_returned) · **4028** Infringement / Fines (`infringement` — distinct from tolls) · **4026** Platform Fees (our processing/service/admin fee — no IMS code, it's our fee not an incident) · **4025** Other (residual). Adjacent: **4030** Insurance Recoveries (insurer-side claim receipts, FLOW-12). **NOT incidentals income (excluded by design):** chargeback_via_payment_provider (contra-revenue), deposit_not_paid_back (2110 liability), service-failure/goodwill codes (car_functionl_issue_*, not_able_to_access/locate_car, pickup_technical_failure, guest_incapacitated, guest_cancellation_unforceen) = guest REFUNDS not income, `distance` → 4000 base (AU only). Mapping is many-IMS-codes → one GL account (IMS code = operational subcategory; account = GL rollup), consistent with POL-42 one-to-one view→account. **STATUS: agreed draft, pending AU invoice completeness check before accounts are created.** *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
- **POL-119** **⚠ SUPERSEDED IN PART BY POL-123 (2026-08-14): the four-account (1355/2505) + Rule-A-deferral mechanism described in this bullet is RETIRED — the locked engine is TWO-account cash-only, lane-based (`GST_ENGINE.md` §0). The four-account model was removed 2026-08-13/14 (see POL-120/121); this bullet was left stale. Still valid: the per-entity gate (POL-87), SG-not-registered (DQ-94), and the BAS calc (Σ 2500 credits − Σ 1350 debits).** ★★ THE **SINGLE CANONICAL GST RULING — full spec is `documentation/wip/GST_ENGINE.md`** (the GST Engine principle doc). That doc is the source of truth: the one principle (GST-applicability is a property of the revenue/expense COA, decided once, at whichever event books it; P&L net by construction; GST reaches BAS only on cash), the two-rule model (Rule A accrual defers into 1355/2505; Rule B cash recognises/releases into 1350/2500), the four accounts (1350/2500 realized in BAS; 1355/2505 deferred, invisible to BAS; single-control 2510 REJECTED), the six canonical journal cases (deferred trip revenue, invoice raised, invoice payable, direct revenue, direct expense, host payout), and the BAS calc (`Σ credits to 2500 − Σ debits to 1350` for the quarter). Still valid & complementary here in KNOWLEDGE: **POL-87** (per-entity gate: AU id3 registered, SG id1/2 not), **DQ-94** (SG not registered), **POL-48** (SG-invoice-GST interim). SUPERSEDED mechanism (history only): **POL-44 / POL-118 / DQ-25**. **MODEL LOCKED (2026-08-13, council-tested).** Cash-basis; P&L net by construction; four accounts (1350/2500 realized in BAS, 1355/2505 deferred not in BAS; single-control 2510 rejected). Engine = **ONE function `src/services/gst_service.classify()`** (pure, 15-case verified), **NO rules table** (overkill for one entity/rate — build only on a 2nd GST regime). Reads three facts: entity registration, `gst_applicable_au` (eligibility gate, not the answer), and invoice `tax_amount`/vendor `gst_registrations`. Deferred used only when accrual precedes cash (invoiced AR, vendor bills, host accruals); prepay + direct cash hit realized straight. Payment = **move not reversal** (Dr deferred / Cr realized). **Vendor gate is supreme for INPUT — overrides the account flag** (auto-zeroes SG-IC, foreign SaaS, unregistered vendors). Refunds reverse OUTPUT; deposits excluded (not a supply). Partner account = verification tripwire, not a router. **ONE OPEN DECISION: host-payout claim policy** — `claim_host_by_default=True` (firm practice, Q2 BAS ≈ $13.8k refund) vs gate-on-registration (ATO-conservative, needs RCTI, Q2 BAS ≈ $53.4k payable); ~$67k swing; confirm with Kaveesh before lodging. **Q2 FY2026 retro proof built** (dry-run): `gst_q2_by_txn.csv` (988 cash lines, self-summing), `gst_q2_deferred.csv` (1355 $83k / 2505 $49k @30 Jun), `gst_q2_account_summary.csv`, `GST_Q2_2026_BAS_PROOF.md`. **POSTING TIMING (doc §10a, code-verified 2026-08-13):** GST is built INTO the main JE as lines at creation and posts atomically when the JE posts — going-forward at posting, historical via a backend batch. Lanes differ: economic events + Stripe create JEs POSTED immediately; bank/categorization + invoice-approve create DRAFT (default), flipped to POSTED at confirm/reconcile (`transaction_service:213`) or VOIDed on reject (`:315`). **PAIRING/REPOST/REFUND (doc §10b):** GST is always LINES on the JE, never standalone — so void/repost/re-pair/refund carry GST automatically, NO separate GST logic. Approval JE carries the 1355 deferral (G-4); AP payment JE (`create_ap_payment_entries`) carries the release Dr1350/Cr1355 (G-5). Bank-first-invoice-later parks to 1300 Prepayments (not gst-applicable → no premature claim), GST claimed at pairing (correct cash-basis: claim when holding the tax invoice for an already-paid amount). NOT YET: wire classify() into live posting (G-4..G-8, prod-posting change, supervised per VR-1c). **H1 2026 AU GST CLEANUP RUNBOOK = `documentation/wip/H1_2026_AU_GST_CLEANUP.md`** (planned, not run): 1350 balance $224,544 is 100% `invoice_approval` (GST booked at approval not payment; payment JEs never touch 1350); split pre-2026 $204,661 (paid $202,152 → 1359 suspense / open $2,508 → 1355) + 2026 $19,884 (reclass to 1355). Process = tagged correcting batch (`gst_h1_opening`/`gst_h1_restate`), NOT voiding bill JEs; uniform payment rule Dr1350/Cr1355; output Dr revenue/Cr2500 nets P&L. Real BAS lodged from QuickBooks not this ledger (so restating is safe, prior BAS unaffected). Reversible: void all `source LIKE 'gst_h1_%'` + pre-op backup + invariant checks. Full prior inline mechanism in git history. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-13 — moved to doc, superseding the inline version)*
- **DQ-99** GST INPUT FALLBACK BYPASSES THE VENDOR GATE ON INVOICED EXPENSES (Gaurav + Pickle, 2026-08-14). The classify path for AP-paid (invoiced) expenses is: invoice `tax_amount`>0 → claim it; **NULL tax BUT expense COA `gst_applicable_au` → claim 1/11 anyway** ("invoice = substantiation" fallback, added in the 2026-08-13 config fixes); else none. That middle branch **never checks vendor registration**, so a foreign/unregistered vendor on a GST-applicable COA gets wrongly claimed. **SentriLock LLC** (US, counterparty 130, `gst_registrations = []`, on 5030 Cost of Device Subscriptions = applicable) had **~$836 H1 input GST wrongly claimed** across 7 per-txn JEs (voided on clone 2026-08-14; BAS → Q2 net −$15,216.17, H1 −$22,012.28). **ROOT CAUSE:** the fallback assumes NULL tax = extraction miss (trust the COA) — true only for an AU-REGISTERED vendor; for an unregistered/foreign vendor a NULL tax is GENUINE (no GST charged), so no claim. **FIX (vendor-gate-supreme, POL-118, extend to invoiced path):** only fall back to the COA when the vendor is AU-registered; unregistered vendor + NULL invoice tax → app=False. **NOT SentriLock-only** — this fallback mis-claimed every foreign/unregistered vendor with no invoice GST on a gst-applicable COA; sweep 1B for the rest. **FIXED in `gst_service.classify()` 2026-08-14** (vendor gate now covers the invoiced path: invoice tax>0 → claim it; no invoice GST → claim only if vendor AU-registered, else EXCLUDED/REVIEW; unit-verified). Re-deriving H1 per-txn with the fix gives **input $200,994.70 vs $204,315.54 original = $3,320.84 total mis-claim** (SentriLock $727.85 already voided; ~$2,593 across other foreign vendors, 12 txns). **SWEEP DONE on clone 2026-08-14** (`gst_h1_pertxn.py` now voids ALL posted gst_h1 first → no double-post; 487 voided, 482 reposted with fixed classify): 1350 input now $200,994.70; BAS Q2 net −$14,791.60, H1 net −$19,311.01. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-123** ★★ **THE LOCKED GST MACHINE — cash-only, two accounts, LANE-BASED (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). SUPERSEDES the four-account council model in POL-119/`GST_ENGINE.md` (now §0 of that doc).** Two accounts only: **1350** input, **2500** output. No 1355/2505, no deferral. **GST fires ONLY on cash movement; accruals get nothing.** The engine runs this exact ordered process: **(1) Entity gate** — entity GST-registered? AU id3 yes, SG id1/2 no; else stop. **(2) Bank-leg gate** — does the JE touch a bank account (set = `finance_bank_accounts.coa_account_code`)? No bank leg → accrual → stop. Direction from the bank side: bank DEBITED = cash in = OUTPUT; bank CREDITED = cash out = INPUT. **(3) Branch by LANE:** **Lane A economic events** — no invoice concept; blindly, if the event's contra COA is `gst_applicable_<market>`, take 1/11 in that direction; no invoice/vendor check. **Lane B bank/categorization** (evaluated just before the draft JE is created) — look at the contra: not gst-applicable → no GST; gst-applicable → **is the txn LINKED to an invoice?** linked (contra is the payable/receivable it settles) → use the **invoice's own `tax_amount`** (paid-or-not irrelevant, the bank leg proves cash moved); not linked → **vendor GST-registered?** yes → 1/11, no → no claim. Posting: output `Cr 2500` net the contra; input `Dr 1350` net the contra; BAS = Σ 2500 credits − Σ 1350 debits per quarter. **DIFFERENCES vs the retired four-account model:** two accounts not four (deferral removed); cash-only trigger via the bank-leg gate (old Rule A accrual-netting gone); dispatch by LANE not one contra-blind `classify()`; invoice detected by the txn↔invoice LINK not inferred from the contra. **PARKED:** deferred-revenue prepay (cash into 2100, an unflagged liability) gets NO GST here; revisit only if trip-prepay output GST must be captured (would need 2100 flagged or a deferred-revenue-taxable rule). Aligns with the two-account cash-only model in STATUS §2.14 (POL-120/121). **THREE COA PROPERTIES carry the whole rulebook (no hardcoded account sets in the engine):** (1) `gst_applicable_au` — taxable supply; (2) `gst_reverses_output` — refund/chargeback accounts (5051/5052/5053/5054/5055/5037) reverse output (Dr 2500) instead of claiming input; (3) `gst_claim_by_default` — host + incidental payout accounts (2120, 5000-5003, 5020-5025, 5032-5034, 5040-5044, 5050) claim input WITHOUT the vendor gate (the §8b host-claim decision, per-account switchable, pending Kaveesh). **MAPPING FIXED (Gaurav, 2026-08-14):** flagged the clearing/payable accounts that carry a taxable supply — 2100 Deferred Trip Revenue, 1200 Trade Receivables, 2120 Host Payables → `t` (so cash settling them takes GST via the pure contra-flag machine); 1310/1330/1900/7000 → `f`; 4027/4028/6502 → `t` (6502 Fines is tax-questionable, double-check before lodging); 1300 stays `t` per Gaurav (contradicts POL-119 §10b, doc to reconcile). **MOCK VALIDATED (H1, clone, 2026-08-14):** the locked machine reproduces the trusted `gst_h1_pertxn` classifier — OUTPUT ties to the cent ($176,685.48), INPUT within $290.56, the whole residual being one account (5011 Stripe Platform Adjustments, claimed by Lane A vs vendor-gated by trusted). `documentation/wip/gst_h1_mock_locked.py`. **FINAL DESIGN (Gaurav, 2026-08-15) — ZERO extra markers, NO schema migration.** All marker designs RETIRED (two COA flags → template flag → in-code event set — each rejected in turn): (a) `claim_by_default` was a crutch for missing vendor registrations (DQ-101) — the vendor gate + a correct vendor list IS the bank-lane input decision; (b) **refunds get NO special-casing at all (Gaurav ruling): a refund out lands as INPUT (Dr 1350) by the pure machine.** Box 7 net GST is IDENTICAL to the reversal treatment (1A/1B effects cancel exactly); accepted trade-off = 1A/1B grossed up by refund GST (~$39k H1 each side) and derived G1 includes refunds. The ONLY property the machine reads is `gst_applicable_<mkt>` on the COA. Mig 059 deleted — nothing to migrate. Final H1 mock: box 7 net within $2,458 of trusted (Cycle & Carriage correctly excluded ~$990, no-counterparty payouts ~$1,392 VR-SWEEP-3, 5011 Stripe intentionally claimed +$291, minor dispute-direction effects). **SUPERSEDED text below (2026-08-14 flag-based status):** **IMPLEMENTATION — MACHINE LOCKED + WIRED (2026-08-14, `finance_work`):** all three lanes live and honoring the three COA properties — `_lane_a_gst` (Lane A, bank-leg gate + contra flag + refund reversal), `_resolve_gst` (Lane B, flag + invoice-link/vendor gate + refund reversal + claim-by-default), `_input_gst_reclass` (invoice payment). New `gst_service.account_reverses_output` / `account_claim_by_default` helpers. Migration **059_account_gst_reverse_and_claim** adds the two boolean columns (model + `to_dict` updated). Unit: **21/21 pass** (`pr1_gst_hook_verify.py`, incl refund-reversal + claim-by-default). Mock: OUTPUT ties, INPUT within $290.56 = the 5011 Stripe Platform Adjustments claim (Gaurav: **claim it**, so locked is the intended answer, trusted was the miss). 5011 claimed via Lane A. STILL TO DO before prod: run mig 059 on prod + set the three flags on prod COAs (the clone changes: 1310/1330/1900/7000→f, 4027/4028/6502→t, 2100/1200/2120→t applicable, refund set→reverses_output, payout set→claim_by_default), all supervised VR-1c; deploy the engine code. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*

- **DQ-101** THE BANK-LANE "PAYOUT" GST IS A VENDOR-DATA GAP, NOT A MACHINE GAP — FIX THE VENDOR LIST, NOT A FLAG (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). In H1, ~$17.3k of input GST (86 bank-lane JEs, sources `counterparty_default`/`needs_review_resolution`/`ai_classification`/`categorization_engine`) was claimed on host/incidental payouts that hit the bank statement DIRECTLY (not via the Stripe economic-events lane): fleet-host payments to **Bombora** (id 504, $109,956, AU Pty Ltd) and **Cycle & Carriage** (id 63, $10,870), plus real third-party vendors booked to Incidentals-Payout accounts — **Linkt Melbourne** tolls (310, $31,542), smash repairers **AUTO & GENERAL** (659), **PORT MOTOR TRADERS** (663), **Jonkers** (710, already AU-reg w/ ABN 39064338683), towing, assessors. The `gst_claim_by_default` COA flag (bypassing the vendor gate) was a CRUTCH masking that these registered AU vendors had EMPTY `gst_registrations` — AND it WRONGLY claimed ~$988 on **Cycle & Carriage Leasing Pte Ltd, a SINGAPORE vendor** the vendor gate would have correctly excluded (a real mis-claim sitting in the current H1 numbers; Gaurav: leave the posted H1 as-is, don't re-fix). **CORRECT FIX = the vendor list:** mark real AU vendors registered (AU entry in `gst_registrations`, ABN optional), leave foreign ones (SG Pte Ltd) out, and the normal vendor gate handles the bank lane — no `claim_by_default` needed and Cycle & Carriage correctly dropped. **DONE (clone):** registered 6 AU vendors (504/310/659/663/668/673); with `claim_by_default` OFF the H1 input gap vs trusted fell from **−$16,142 to −$2,093** (the residual = Cycle & Carriage correctly excluded ~$988 + ~$1,085 payouts with NO counterparty attached + Wash and Go ~$21). **NEXT:** full counterparty sweep to register all real AU vendors + attach missing counterparties, then DELETE `gst_claim_by_default` (column + code). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **DQ-100** THE ECONOMIC-EVENTS LANE IS ACCRUAL-STRUCTURED — CASH-BASIS GST CANNOT BE COMPUTED ON A PROJECTION JE (Gaurav + Pickle, 2026-08-14). The `finance_je_templates` for entity 3 book revenue as an ACCRUAL in one JE (`trip_revenue_p2p` Dr 2100 / Cr **4000**; `incidentals_cleaning` Dr 1200 / Cr **4022**) and collect the CASH in a SEPARATE JE later (`incidentals_paid` Dr **1019** / Cr 1200; `trip_distance_invoice_paid` Dr 1019 / Cr 1200). All 4xxx revenue accounts are `gst_applicable_au=true`. A GST hook keyed on "credit is a gst-applicable revenue account" therefore FIRES on the accrual leg (wrong timing — GST recognised before cash) and MISSES the cash-collection leg (which credits the receivable 1200, not revenue). Because recognition and cash live in different entries, **cash-basis output GST cannot be derived from a single `project_month` projection JE** — the PR-1 economic-events hook was built then REVERTED for exactly this. **RULE:** GST fires on the CASH legs (bank-match / per-cash-transaction classification, mirroring `gst_h1_pertxn`), never on the accrual projection. **CONSEQUENCE:** going-forward OUTPUT GST on the Stripe revenue lane (which flows through economic events / payout imports, not bank-statement categorization) is currently UNCAPTURED — needs a dedicated per-cash-transaction output-GST pass. Input GST IS covered (invoiced bills via `create_ap_payment_entries`; direct bank expenses via categorization). *(Gaurav + Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-122** ★ **AU PAYROLL — GROSS MODEL, PAYG→2301 & SUPER→2302 PAYABLES, STICK TO OUR NET (Gaurav, 2026-08-14).** AU salary was booked NET straight to expense (no gross, no PAYG/super payable → BAS W1/W2 read ~0). Restated to the proper model on the clone (`documentation/wip/gst_h1_payroll.py`, source `payroll_h1_restate`, reversible), **without altering actual cash — payments only re-pointed.** RULE: **stick to OUR net actually paid; where our net ≠ ACE's net, our net wins** (gross = our net + ACE PAYG). Per-month accrual `Dr 6000 gross + Dr 6002 super / Cr 2304 net + Cr 2301 PAYG + Cr 2302 super`, dated month-end; each ACE-employee salary payment re-pointed `6000/5062 → 2304`. **Two payables to TWO payees:** **2301 PAYG Withholding Payable → ATO, settled INSIDE the BAS payment** (`Dr 2301 + Dr 2500 GST-net / Cr bank` — one ATO payment clears PAYG + GST); **2302 Superannuation Payable → the super funds by BPay** (`Dr 2302 / Cr bank`), NOT the ATO (only late super = SGC to ATO). Roster (super-line = STP employee): Q1 Craig Letters + Jacob Hyde(left) + Resya + Matheus; Q2 Craig + Resya + Matheus. Matheus salary mis-booked to 5062; his May $5,262.29 5062 line is an EXPENSE reimbursement, not salary. **Verified (clone):** W2 = ACE exactly (Q1 $20,215.00 / Q2 $9,802.00); W1 our-net gross (Q1 $88,297.73 / Q2 $52,209.28, diverges from ACE STP gross by ~$1,248/$685 = documented recon); super Q1 $8,857.76/Q2 $6,182.89; 2301 −$30,017, 2302 −$15,040.65, 2304 $0. **NOT YET:** post the two settlements (split the existing Apr ATO payment PAYG↔GST off 2500; date the super BPay vs 2302); run on PROD (supervised). Full runbook = `H1_2026_AU_GST_CLEANUP.md §8`. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-121** ★ **INVOICE APPROVAL BOOKS GROSS — NO 1350 AT APPROVAL (Gaurav, 2026-08-14).** The cash-basis model taken to its source: an AP bill/invoice at approval now books a **2-line GROSS** JE `Dr expense/prepaid(total) / Cr AP(total)` — **no 1350 GST Input line**. GST stays recorded on the invoice (`invoice.tax_amount`); the input credit is claimed later, at the CASH payment, by the cash-time GST engine, which carves it out of the **expense** (`Dr 1350 / Cr expense`) so AP settles at gross and the P&L lands net. **CODE:** `invoice_service.py` bill-JE builder — the `if tax>0` 3-line branch (Dr expense net + Dr 1350 + Cr AP) is **removed**; it always books the 2-line gross JE. (The categorization engine `categorization_service` has a SEPARATE 1350 GST path that is still 3-line but is INERT in prod per DQ-25 — not touched here; align later for consistency.) **INTERIM (accepted by Gaurav):** the expense-side claim isn't built yet, so newly-approved AU bills book gross and their input GST is NOT claimed until the cash-time engine runs — expense sits gross meanwhile. **H1 RESTATEMENT DONE ON CLONE (`gst_h1_source_mutate.py`):** the 63 H1 `invoice_approval` bills (all clean 3-line) were mutated at source — 1350 line deleted, expense grossed by the GST ($19,883.62), remark `gst_source_2026` stamped; the 6 in-period `gst_legacy_redate` correcting JEs (11001–11006) VOIDed; the pre-2026 Dec-31 park (11007, $204,660.77) STAYS. Verified: 1350 balance UNCHANGED, 3200 −$19,883.62, expense +$19,883.62, ledger balanced, **BAS unchanged** (Q2 −$15,581.81, H1 −$22,631.85). Reversible (backup json). - **POL-120** ★ **BAS REPORT ENGINE (AU) — built + verified on clone, 2026-08-14 (Gaurav).** Endpoint `GET /api/finance/reports/bas?entity_id=3&date_from=&date_to=` → `report_service.get_bas()`. Maps the two-account cash model straight onto the ATO Activity Statement: **1A** (GST on sales) = credit movement on **2500**; **1B** (GST on purchases) = debit movement on **1350**; **G1** (total GST-inclusive sales) = 1A×11; **box 7** net GST = 1A−1B; PAYG **W1** = debit movement on wage COAs (6000/6003/5061/5063), **W2** = credit movement on **2301** PAYG Withholding Payable; summary **8A**=1A+W2, **8B**=1B, **box 9**=8A−8B. **TWO PRINCIPLED EXCLUSIONS from 1A/1B, both non-trading:** (1) any GST-control line whose JE also touches a bank account (10xx) is an **ATO/BAS settlement**, not a sale/purchase (see DQ-97); (2) opening-equity parks are handled by the legacy-redate cleanup, not a report filter. **Verified against the clone to the cent:** Q1 net −$7,050.04, Q2 **1A $75,775.38 / 1B $91,357.19 / net −$15,581.81 refund**, H1 **1A $181,683.69 / 1B $204,315.54 / net −$22,631.85 refund**; quarters sum exactly to H1. **W1/W2 are near-zero on this ledger** ($540 wages / $0 withheld H1) — PAYG payroll postings aren't wired yet (data gap, not a report bug). NOTE: **POL-119 + `GST_ENGINE.md` still describe the retired FOUR-account model** (1355/2505 deferred) — the locked model is now TWO accounts cash-only (1350/2500), poster `documentation/wip/gst_h1_simple.py`; those docs need the four→two rewrite. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **DQ-97** `counterparty_default` JES ON **2500** ARE ATO/BAS SETTLEMENTS, NOT SALES GST (Pickle clone scan, 2026-08-14). Two entity-3 JEs tagged `source='counterparty_default'` move 2500 directly against a bank account: JE 3677 (11 Mar) received **$37,722.24** from ATO (Dr 1015 / Cr 2500), JE 4956 (15 Apr) paid **$13,546.18** to ATO (Dr 2500 / Cr 1010), both memo'd *"reclass 9000→2500: ATO = BAS/GST settlement, not income tax"*. These SETTLE the GST liability — they must never count in 1A "GST on sales." - **POL-118** ⚠ **SUPERSEDED BY POL-119** (the single canonical GST ruling — two-account model here → four accounts there; content folded in). Kept for history only. ~~GST TIMING = CASH MOVEMENT; TWO ACCOUNTS, NOT ONE CONTROL~~ (Gaurav, 2026-08-10). **(1) TIMING — the fundamental fix:** GST is recognised at the moment CASH MOVES, never at invoice approval. Input and output GST both post on the cash event, not on accrual. Concretely: the bill/approval JE stops carrying any GST line (bill books GROSS); **output GST** posts when AU cash is RECEIVED (cash-collected event), **input GST** posts when AU cash is PAID (vendor payment / host payout / cost). The current behaviour is WRONG — `invoice_service.approve()` posts input GST to **1350 GST Receivable (Input Tax)** at approval (accrual); **$245,594 across 486 lines already sits on 1350 from this wrong timing and must be reversed + re-posted on the cash basis.** **(2) ACCOUNTS — keep TWO, no third control:** Gaurav wants input and output kept SEPARATE and netted at reporting — **1350 GST Receivable (Input Tax)** (AU cash paid) + **2500 GST Payable (Output Tax)** (AU cash received); the BAS position = 2500 − 1350 for the quarter. The proposed single **2510 GST Control (AU)** is REJECTED — do not create it. SG still posts zero GST (not registered, POL-87). **VENDOR GATE IS PER-COUNTRY, INVOICE-DERIVED (Gaurav, 2026-08-11):** vendors are GLOBAL (341/463 have no entity_id) — one vendor can be AU- and/or SG-registered, so a single `is_gst_registered` boolean is wrong. New col `finance_counterparties.gst_registrations` JSONB = `[{country:'AU'|'SG', registration_number}]` (mig 058); country present ⇒ registered. **Source of truth = invoices:** a vendor that charged GST on an AU invoice IS AU-registered — back-populated **118 vendors (95 AU, 25 SG), ABN/GST no. from extracted `vendor_tax_id`**; new GST invoices auto-add. Helpers: `counterparty_service.registered_in(cp,country)`, `market_for_entity(entity_id)` (3→AU, 1/2→SG). **DIRECT-EXPENSE GST (the hard case — account alone can't decide, since one COA holds both GST and non-GST vendors) = entity registered AND account `gst_applicable_<country>` AND `registered_in(vendor)`**; unknown vendors (no invoice history) default OFF + reviewable. INVOICED purchases just read the invoice `tax_amount`. Legacy `is_gst_registered`/`tax_registration_number` deprecated. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-11)* **HOST PAYOUTS — CLAIMED BY DEFAULT, NO VENDOR GATE (Gaurav practice, 2026-08-13):** all cash paid to HOSTS (host-earnings + host incidentals payout accounts: 5000 Host Trip Earnings P2P, 5001 RMS, 5021/5032/5044/5020/5024 incidentals-payout-host, etc.) is claimed as **input GST by default** (1/11), regardless of whether the individual host is GST-registered — the per-host registration check is NOT applied. This is the POL-44 gross model on the host side (output GST on gross AU revenue − input GST on gross host payouts + costs = GST on Drive lah's margin). The vendor-registration gate applies only to NON-host vendor/direct expenses. **Q2 (Apr–Jun 2026) dry-run under this practice: output 1/11×$1.30M=$117,948; input 1/11×$1.66M GST-applicable spend=$150,654; net = $32,706 REFUND** (accrual-dated; cash-basis refinement next). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-13)*
- **POL-44** ⚠ GST **MECHANISM SUPERSEDED BY POL-119** (the single-control-account / "one 2510" model is RETIRED → four accounts 1350/2500/1355/2505). The **cash-basis principle here is retained** (and now canonical in POL-119). GST — CASH-BASIS, POSTING-TIME, EVERYTHING-APPLICABLE (Gaurav, 2026-07-28) — **AU GST payable for a quarter = the cash that MOVED in that quarter, not accruals.** Cash-basis is the sole basis. **SG = zero (not GST-registered).** MECHANISM: GST is NOT a view and NOT per-category — it is a **posting-time auto-calc to a GST control account**. On EVERY AU cash line, mark GST: **output** GST on all AU cash **received** (cash-collected economic events + any receipts), **input** GST on all AU cash **paid** (host payouts AND direct/non-EE expenses). **Everything AU cash is GST-applicable — do NOT carve out a commission slice at posting;** the agent/net result (POL-36) emerges automatically because gross-output − gross-input(host payout + costs) = GST on our margin, netted in the control account. BAS for a quarter = the GST control-account balance for that quarter. The one build item: a **GST-applicability mapping for direct expenses** (non-economic-event bank postings) — owed anyway. **IMS is only about incident management/categorization later — it does NOT gate GST; GST applies to incidentals cash TODAY** (this refines POL-38: IMS defers *categorization/wiring*, not GST applicability). Coexists with POL-36: P&L shows net commission (agent); GST control nets gross-in−gross-out to the same margin. *(Gaurav, 2026-07-28)*
