Which physical account (713147601001 vs 588154393001) each maps to is UNRESOLVED and MUST be pinned before any statement load (else per-account attribution is wrong — the botched VR-1c load proved it by dumping everything into id1). Resolve by matching known statement opening/closing balances, or prior H1 `ba`-references (H1 work referenced ba1 & ba18). ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (from the `account_number` column, definitive):** **OCBC 1001 (713147601001) → bank_account_id=1** ("OCBC Main account", coa 1000); **OCBC 3001 (588154393001) → bank_account_id=18** ("OCBC", coa 1016). Both entity_id=2 (DL Pte Ltd SG), currency SGD, file_adapter='ocbc'. H1'26 data already sits on the correct ids (id1 = 977 rows, Jun-30 close 6,564.09; id18 = 76 rows, Jun-29 close 25,058.49). The botched load wrongly forced everything to id1. So the load map is: `OCBC_1001/*→ba1`, `OCBC_3001/*→ba18`. *(resolved, 2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-78** OCBC PDF OVER-PARSE ON 2024+ FORMAT (suspected). During VR-1c the OCBC adapter produced **~840 rows for a single 2024 monthly statement vs ~150 for 2020** — a ~5× inflation suggesting duplicate/phantom-row extraction on the 2024+ layout. The parse-audit PASSED self-reconcile (balance chain closes) because duplicated rows can still net correct — so this defect is INVISIBLE to chain-reconcile and needs a rows-per-statement plausibility check + dedupe before trusting a real OCBC load. Verify the true monthly txn count on a 2024 OCBC file before reloading. ✅ **RESOLVED — FALSE ALARM (2026-08-02).** OCBC 1001 monthly counts ramp SMOOTHLY with business volume (≈120/mo 2020 → ≈500-900 2022-23 → peak ≈800-1320 2024 → back to ≈120-270 late-2025); no format-specific jump. Rigorous check on the peak month (2024-04, 1320 rows): **0 exact-duplicate rows, 0 chain breaks** (every row: prev_balance+amount=running_balance), opening 25,757.88 + Σ 10,284.18 = closing 36,042.06 exactly; 2024-07 identical (0/0, reconciles). Phantom/duplicate rows are impossible with a clean chain. The adapter is TRUSTWORTHY on 2024+; the earlier "~840 vs ~150" flag just compared 2024 to 2020 and missed the ramp. *(resolved, 2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-79** OCBC DEDUP FALSE-COLLISION ON DISPUTE+REVERSAL (VR-1c OCBC-1001 load, 2026-08-02). The OCBC PDF fingerprint is `[transaction_date, amount, running_balance]` — normally unique because the running balance is monotonic. It COLLIDES when a payment + its same-amount reversal return the balance to a prior value and an identical debit repeats the same day: 2022-01-01 had TWO real `-21,660.00 CHENG CHUAN MO DISPUTE REPA` debits, each landing balance at 368,471.70 (separated by a `+21,660.00 FAST REVERSAL`). Both share fingerprint `(2022-01-01, -21660.00, 368471.70)` → `import_from_rows` deduped one as a duplicate, dropping a REAL transaction. Effect: ba1's loaded 2022-01 is one -21,660 row short → a single balance-chain break of 21,660 at that point (**38,933/38,934 rows otherwise chain to the cent**). The statement's printed balances are authoritative and unaffected; only the loaded txn set is short. FIX OPTIONS: (a) insert the one missing 2022-01-01 −21,660 row (targeted, closes the chain); (b) refine the fingerprint for dispute/reversal patterns (system-wide dedup change, heavier). The dispute+reversal also echoes across the Dec-2021/Jan-2022 boundary (on both statements) — a normal cross-statement echo, deduped correctly there. ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-02** — restored the missing −21,660 twin (`source='dq79_fix_dispute_twin'`, fingerprint suffixed `DQ79-restored-twin` so it never re-dedups). Jan-2022 now sums to **−313,328.79 = the statement's B/F→C/F delta (393,933.10→80,604.31) to the cent.** *(VR-1c, 2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-80** OCBC-1001 QB↔PDF SOURCE SEAM GAP at Jan/Feb-2020 (VR-1c, 2026-08-02). The account is loaded from TWO sources at the Jan/Feb-2020 seam: QB GL bank txns for Jun-2019→Jan-2020 (634 rows, no statements exist) + bank PDF statements from Feb-2020. QB closing at 31-Jan-2020 = **202,417.29**; Feb-2020 PDF opening B/F = **223,820.89** → a **21,403.60 discontinuity** at the seam. Each slice chains internally to the cent (QB 0 breaks/634; PDF 38,933/38,934). The gap = QB-accounting-view vs raw-bank-feed difference (timing / bank items QB didn't record), a reconciliation item for the historical recon, NOT a load defect. NB QB balances here are the REAL bank balance — negative in mid-2019 during initial funding (Dirk-Jan 15k deposit 25-Jun-2019), recovered to +202k by Jan-2020 — NOT sign-flipped. Extraction: GL section `OCBC DL SG (1001)`, cols Date/TxnType/Name/Memo/Debit/Credit/Balance; signed amount = Debit−Credit; running_balance = QB Balance col; `source='qb_gapfill_2019'`. ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-02 — the 21,403.60 was EXACTLY the account's real opening balance on 06-Jun-2019** (QB started the register at 0; real accounts don't). Proof: QB Jan-31 close 202,417.29 + 21,403.60 = 223,820.89 = the bank's Feb-2020 B/F to the cent (constant offset, not diffuse drift). Booked an opening-balance entry +21,403.60 dated 05-Jun-2019 (`source='qb_opening_dq80'`) + shifted the 634 QB running_balances +21,403.60. Post-fix: QB slice+opening chains **0 breaks/635**, Jan-31 close = 223,820.89 = PDF Feb-open; JE/JL unchanged. OCBC-1001 now chains continuously 06-Jun-2019→Jun-2026. *(VR-1c, 2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-81** AU QB 2021-2022 CBA BALANCE UNRELIABLE (VR-1c CBA load, 2026-08-02). The CBA bank account in the AU GL is section **`1101 Savings (7311)`** (7,506 txns from 2021-08-23; "7311" = account 06-2246-1034**7311**). Loaded the pre-statement QB slice (Aug-2021→Jun-2022, **524** real Deposit/Expense txns, JEs excluded) into ba17. BUT the QB running balance is NOT reconciled to the bank for this early period: QB closes Jun-2022 at **−89,804.85** (impossible negative for a savings account) while the bank PDF opens Jul-2022 at **+98,703.81** — a **188,508.66 seam gap.** AU bookkeeping was incomplete/unreconciled in 2021-2022 (likely deposits QB routed to clearing accounts). Unlike OCBC (clean, single opening plug) and DBS (clean, no gap), CBA's pre-Jul-2022 period CANNOT be reconstructed reliably from QB and needs the ACTUAL 2021-2022 CBA statements (we lack them — CBA statements start Sep-2022). The 524 txns are loaded as best-available RECORD (real activity, useful for vendor recon), balances flagged unreliable; **NO synthetic plug booked** (would paper over bad data). CBA reliable balance history starts Jul-2022. *(VR-1c, 2026-08-02)*
  ✅ **SUPERSEDED same day** — Gaurav provided the REAL 2021-2022 CBA statements (`20210930` inception Aug-Sep 2021 opening Nil→close 8,491.49; `20220330` Dec2021-Mar2022→159,381.17; `20220630` Mar-Jun2022→98,703.81). The 524-row QB slice was DELETED; real statements loaded instead — all self-reconcile and chain into the Jul-2022 statement (98,703.81). Required a CBA adapter fix (DQ-82). **Oct-Dec 2021 statement `20211230` PROVIDED + loaded (2026-08-02): 78 rows, opens 8,491.49 = 20210930 close, closes 5,194.35 = 20220330 open — chains to the cent.** CBA is now FULLY real-statement-backed and continuous **Aug-2021→Jun-2026, no gaps**; the DQ-81 unreliability is fully moot (QB no longer used for CBA).
- **DQ-82** CBA ADAPTER EARLY-FORMAT FIXES (2026-08-02). Two format-drift bugs the parse-audit never saw (older CBA statements weren't in the corpus): (1) a brand-new account prints `OPENING BALANCE Nil` with NO `$X.XXCR` token, so the adapter's opening guard never fired and the whole statement yielded **0 rows** (Statement20210930 = Aug-2021 inception) — fixed via `_OPENING_NIL_RE` seeding the chain at 0; (2) this vintage **glues the 3-letter month to the description with no space** (`02MayDirect Credit…`); only May was hit (Apr/Jun kept their space), so the old `_TXN_START_RE` `\b` boundary failed and May rows silently inherited the previous April date — fixed via a MONTH_MAP-anchored regex + lookahead. Result: 20220630 Apr 125→51, **May 0→74**, still reconciles; no regression (CBA audit stays green). +8 regression tests (fixtures need copying into main to un-skip). Fix: `src/services/csv_adapters/cba.py`. *(2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-83** PAYMENT REFERENCE CARRIES THE VENDOR INVOICE NUMBER — the strongest AP-match signal (2026-08-02). Wise `paymentReference` (and bank raw rows) genuinely contain the supplier's invoice number that ops typed at pay time — "INV-3190749", "ref 4017426", "VH-PI-26-001149", "SC2605-49906", "DM023". Read-only dry run on the 1,112 ready invoices, matching each invoice's number against ITS OWN counterparty's outflow txns (ref+desc+raw, **date-independent**): 865 have a usable specific token, **662 (76.5%) reference-match, 295 with exact amount too** — vs only ~100 auto-matched by exact-amount+±30d. So reference ≈3× the confident auto-match rate and needs no date window. Amount-off ref hits break down as bundled/multi-invoice (123), partial (26), GST/WHT ~10, sub-1% FX/rounding (~208). TWO tokenizer traps to avoid: shared vendor prefix ("SGIN26" matches a sibling invoice) and short numeric runs colliding in raw CSV ("64101" hit ref "INV62872") — match only the FULL normalized invoice number or a ≥5-digit run, normalizing the haystack the same way. Dry-run CSV: `documentation/wip/wave2_dryrun_reference_match_2026-08-02.csv`; probe: `scripts/vr2_reference_probe.py`. *(Pickle dry run, 2026-08-02)*
- **POL-85** "ALREADY MATCHED" FOR INVOICE↔PAYMENT MATCHING MEANS *PAIRED TO AN INVOICE*, NOT *CARRIES A JE* (2026-08-03). When excluding transactions from an AP matching run, exclude ONLY those already applied to an invoice: paired in `finance_invoice_payment_matches` OR already the `payment_txn_id` of a MATCHED master row (an AP-payment JE, i.e. a JE line hitting AP 2000/2300/2302/2303/2305). Do NOT exclude a transaction just because it is RECONCILED/MATCHED with a JE — a **direct-expense** booking (Dr expense / Cr bank) was never applied to any invoice and IS a valid candidate. Verified on prod 2026-08-03: of 3,812 RECONCILED/MATCHED txns, **0 touch AP — all 3,812 are direct-expense**. Over-excluding on JE-presence cost 25 real matches (76→101). When such a direct-expense txn is later paired-and-posted, the posting engine reverses the original direct-expense JE and re-books through AP (design locked 2026-08-03, build deferred). *(Gaurav corrected Pickle, 2026-08-03)*
- ~~**ENT-20** WISE ACCOUNT STRUCTURE — WRONG, superseded by ENT-21. (Claimed only DL-SG + personal existed on the key; that was because `/v1/profiles` returns only a subset.)~~
- **ENT-21** WISE ACCOUNT STRUCTURE — CORRECTED (verified via `/v2/profiles`, 2026-08-03). ONE Wise login (Gaurav's) holds ALL entities as SEPARATE business profiles. `/v1/profiles` returns only a SUBSET (2) — MUST use `/v2/profiles` (our `src/services/wise_service.py` already does and documents this). Five profiles: **Drive Lah Pte. Ltd.** SG `13811029` (our entity 2; SGD 3,875 + AUD/INR/MYR/PKR/USD 0), **Drive lah Australia Pty Ltd** `41524706` (entity 3; AUD 2,710, USD 0), **Drive Lah Ventures Holding** `74921502` (entity 1; SGD 88/USD 26/AUD 25), **REVIO LABS PTE. LTD.** `87418161` (no DL ledger entity — separate company; SGD 6,119), and personal `13811040`. So each accounting entity maps 1:1 to its own Wise business profile → payout entity-match is clean, and v1 CAN cover all three DL entities, each funding from its own profile's balances. Payout source-account picker = enumerate the invoice-entity's profile balances via `/v4/profiles/{id}/balances`. *(Pickle live check, 2026-08-03)*
- **ENT-22** SG DEPOSIT connect account `acct_1EhuMGAcVqeggTlg` is HELD BY **Drive lah Pte Ltd** (SG operating, entity 2) — NOT Ventures. The Stripe `business_profile.name` says "Drive lah ventures holding pte ltd" (2019 registration-era artifact; account created 2019-06-05) and that is what ENT-8's registry check picked up, but the accounting owner is the Pte Ltd. The ledger was already right: POL-64 created held-funds COA 1021 under entity 2. Supersedes the struck Ventures clause in ENT-8. (Gaurav, 2026-08-16)
- **POL-87** WISE API PAYOUT SCA (2FA) = RSA KEY SIGNING, NOT PHONE OTP (2026-08-03). Wise Strong Customer Auth for API-initiated transfers uses a public/private RSA keypair, not a per-payment OTP. One-time: generate keypair, upload PUBLIC key to Wise (owner approves once). Per payout: the fund call returns HTTP 403 + header `x-2fa-approval:<one-time-token>`; the client signs that token with the PRIVATE key and retries with `x-2fa-approval` + `X-Signature` headers; Wise verifies against the registered public key and releases funds. The private key is the second factor and can move money → held as a tightly-scoped secret, ideally on a dedicated BUSINESS API credential (not Gaurav's personal login, which the current key is). *(Pickle, 2026-08-03)*
- **DQ-85** KNOCK-OFF ASSUMES BANK CURRENCY = ENTITY FUNCTIONAL CURRENCY — BREAKS ON FOREIGN-CCY BANKS (2026-08-03). `create_ap_payment_entries` books `txn.amount` straight into debit/credit as if it were functional. True for home banks (OCBC-SGD/entity SG, CBA-AUD/entity AU) but FALSE for every Wise/DBS foreign account. Bit 2 of the same-currency 708: invoices 403/444 = 10,000 **USD** paid from a USD bank owned by the **AU** entity (functional AUD) → booked as 10,000 **AUD** (fx=1), understated ~33%. Both REVERSED 2026-08-03 and reclassified to the foreign-bank/FX bucket. A JE line's debit/credit is always FUNCTIONAL; native ccy+amount+fx are memo fields (see JE 2460: 61.19 USD × 1.285529 = 78.66 SGD). FIX for the productized engine: convert any foreign-bank payment to functional (native × fx) before booking. *(Pickle, 2026-08-03)*
- **DQ-86** FX ON THE HISTORICAL RECONCILIATION FOLLOWS POL-26 STATEMENT ACTUALS — NOT AD-HOC RATES (Gaurav, 2026-08-04). The system's FX regime is **POL-26**: monthly STANDARD mid-rate per pair from a public source (**ECB/frankfurter**, loaded into `finance_fx_rates` by `year_month`), applied at booking by `fx_service.to_functional`; **statement ACTUALS override when both legs are known**; FX spread → one P&L account **7100**; `fx_service` REFUSES to book when no rate is on file (never guesses). For the vendor-recon posting we know BOTH legs (invoice amount + actual cash), so per POL-26② we use **statement actuals**: the invoice side is booked at the invoice's OWN functional amount, the bank side at the actual cash, and the FX difference lives in the **intercompany balance** (POL-27 case ②, 8200 net accounts) — trued up at consolidation, no 7100 line at booking. **CORRECTION:** my first IC+FX poster used blended constants (0.90 AUD→SGD, …) — a POL-26 violation; it was **reversed and NEVER went live** (the 102 same-entity cross-currency and 28 same-functional cross-entity used NO rate at all — payment was already functional). Applied 2026-08-04: **20 IC+FX POSTED** via `scripts/vr2_post_icfx.py` (statement actuals; invoice already in its functional ccy; 0 double-booking, 0 unbalanced, AP nets, IC pair carries the two real amounts e.g. 8210 11,295.56 AUD ↔ 8200 10,379.34 SGD). **DEFERRED (need a standard rate the table lacks — USD has 0 rows, coverage only 2026-01..07):** 24 foreign-invoice IC+FX + 1 USD-from-SG-bank → VR-5 (backfill `finance_fx_rates` from ECB/frankfurter, then post via `fx_service`). **Grand total AP posted = 856.** *(Gaurav, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-95** CROSS-CURRENCY AP POSTING (HISTORICAL) — BOOK AT THE PAYMENT'S FUNCTIONAL AMOUNT (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). For a same-entity invoice denominated in a foreign currency but paid in the entity's functional currency, book BOTH bill and payment at the **payment's functional amount** ("convert at the paired rate"). AP clears to zero, expense = actual functional cash, GST scales with the functional amount and follows POL-87, and the invoice keeps its foreign face (`total_amount`/`currency` unchanged) and is marked paid. No FX-gain/loss line, no rate lookup. **Applied 2026-08-03: 102 same-entity cross-currency POSTED + verified** (0 double-booking, 0 unbalanced, AP cleared, foreign face intact) via `scripts/vr2_post_xcurrency.py` (overrides total/tax/net to functional for the bill build, restores after; same reversible backup as the same-currency engine). **Grand total AP posted now = 808** (706 same-currency + 102 cross-currency). NOTE: the GOING-FORWARD flow differs (POL-88) — bill booked before payment uses the invoice-date FX rate with realized FX gain/loss → 7100 at settlement; this book-at-payment shortcut is for the historical reconciliation only. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-94** NEVER POST A JOURNAL ENTRY TO AN INACTIVE (non-ACTIVE) ACCOUNT — GLOBAL RULE (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). Any JE line whose account is SUSPENDED/inactive must be rejected, never posted. Already enforced: `journal_service.validate_accounts_exist` (called by `create`) returns False if `account.status != AccountStatus.ACTIVE`, so creation fails. This is why the stale cross-entity path (POL-93) can't silently mis-post — it hits the guard and errors. Keep this guard on every posting path (approval, knock-off, categorization, the productized engine). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-93** INTERCOMPANY MODEL IS THE ACTIVE `8200` NET SERIES, NOT THE SUSPENDED `8000`/`8100` SPLIT — AND `invoice_service` IS STALE (2026-08-03). Two IC account sets exist. **SUSPENDED (do not use):** `8000–8021` "IC Due from" + `8100–8121` "IC Due to" (the split receivable/payable model). Only 9 lines ever touched them (8010/8100/8110) and **all are VOID** — zero live. **ACTIVE (the live model):** `8200` IC-Australia(SG books) · `8201` IC-Ventures(SG books) · `8210` IC-Singapore(AU books) · `8211` IC-Ventures(AU books) · `8220` IC-Singapore(Ventures books) · `8221` IC-Australia(Ventures books) — one NET account per (books-entity, counterparty-entity). Live IC postings (42 POSTED) come from the **categorization_engine** using these 8200 codes. BUT `invoice_service._IC_RECEIVABLE_CODES`/`_IC_PAYABLE_CODES` still point at the suspended `8000`/`8100` set, so `create_ap_payment_entries`'s cross-entity path would fail the POL-94 guard. **FIX before cross-entity AP: repoint invoice_service's IC maps to the active 8200 net accounts** (match the categorization engine). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-92** EVERY AP JOURNAL ENTRY CARRIES `reference_number = 'INV-<invoice_id>'` (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). So any journal entry traces directly back to its invoice on the entry itself, not only via the invoice/match tables. Stamped at creation by `invoice_service.approve()` (bill JE) and `create_ap_payment_entries()` (payment JE, both same- and cross-entity legs). `reference_number` was otherwise unused (only 8 stripe JEs use a distinct `STRIPE-…` format, no collision). Backfilled 2026-08-03: 1,418 existing JEs (710 bill + 708 payment) stamped; backup `documentation/wip/je_refnum_backup_*.json`. Trace paths that still exist too: `invoice.journal_entry_id`→bill, `finance_invoice_payment_matches.journal_entry_id`+`invoice_id`→payment, and the JE description carries the invoice number. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-91** REGULAR-FLOW ORDERING IS APPROVE → PAY-IN-SYSTEM → MATCH; CATEGORIZATION ONLY MATCHES ALREADY-PAID INVOICES (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). Going forward, always: an invoice must be **approved in the system before it is paid, and paid before it is matched**. The categorization/reconciliation engine may pair a bank line ONLY to an invoice that is **already marked paid in the system** (paid via the payout module, POL-88) — i.e. the "paid-but-not-yet-paired" set. It does NOT knock off draft or approved-unpaid invoices in the going-forward flow (the auto-create-settlement-at-pairing behaviour belongs to the historical bridge only). This is the hard steady-state rule. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-90** POSTING GOVERNANCE — PROVISIONAL PAIRINGS ARE CONSOLE-CREATABLE BUT NEVER CONSOLE-POSTABLE; POSTING IS TERMINAL-ONLY (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). The admincontrols console may **create and detach PROVISIONAL** invoice↔payment pairings (rows in `finance_invoice_payment_matches`, state=`provisional`, **zero ledger effect**) — but must NEVER **post** them (flip to `logged` / create JEs) from the UI. Posting a pairing to the ledger is a **controlled TERMINAL operation only** (today: `scripts/vr2_post_pairings.py`, supervised), to prevent ad-hoc console posting from making a mess. **Verified current state (2026-08-03) already enforces this:** the BFF exposes only GET/POST/DELETE `/accounting/invoices/matches` (list / create-provisional / detach); there is NO console/BFF route that sets a match to `logged` or creates a payment JE. The JE-creating endpoints (`/<id>/match-transaction`, the terminal script) are NOT proxied to the console. The retroactive-knockoff + manual terminal tagging that resolved the past is a supervised **one-off** where "anything can post to anything" — acceptable only because it runs through the terminal, never the console. VR-7 productization must keep posting OUT of the console. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-89** AP PAYABLE ACCOUNT IS PER-EXPENSE, NOT HARDCODED 2000 — AND THE PAYMENT CLEARS THE SAME ONE (verified 2026-08-03). An approved invoice credits **the payable declared by its expense account**, not a blanket 2000. Every COA row carries `offset_account_code` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 2000); `invoice_service._payable_account_for(contra_code)` resolves it. Ordinary vendor expenses → 2000 (trade AP), but statutory/payroll route elsewhere: Employer Super 6002→**2302**, CPF 6001→**2300**, Income Tax 9000→**2305**, Salaries 6000/6003/5061/5063→**2304**, Employee Claims 6010-6014/5062→**2303**. BOTH legs use the SAME resolver on the SAME expense account — the bill does `Cr _payable_account_for(contra)` and the settlement does `Dr _payable_account_for(contra)` — so a payment always clears the exact payable the bill posted to (a super bill hits 2302 and its payment clears 2302, never 2000). Already implemented; not a gap. *(verified from code, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-88** GOING-FORWARD INVOICE LIFECYCLE IS PAID-THEN-PAIRED (TWO EVENTS IN TIME), VIA THE PAYOUT MODULE (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). Because Drive lah will pay vendors *from its own system* (the payout module — Wise or bank) and mark the invoice **paid at pay-time**, but bank statement lines only import **end-of-month**, payment and pairing are separate events — you cannot pair against a transaction not yet in the system. The lifecycle: **(1) Approve** → bill JE Dr expense / Cr [its payable]. **(2) Payout module pays** → this is the moment "paid": it POSTS the settlement JE Dr [payable] / Cr bank (AP clears, bank down, invoice→paid). **(3) Month-end** → bank txns import, categorization/reconciliation **pairs** each bank line to the settlement already posted in step 2 — a BANK RECONCILIATION that links + marks the line reconciled. **CRITICAL CORRECTNESS RULE:** step-3 pairing of a payout-paid invoice must **NEVER re-post** a settlement JE (it already exists from step 2) — else the payment double-counts and the bank double-drains; pairing only links + reconciles. So going forward the categorization engine meets two cases per bank line: **invoice already paid (payout-settled) → reconcile only, no new JE** vs **invoice approved-but-unpaid (no payout ran, e.g. direct debit) → knock-off, create settlement JE**. "Paid-but-not-paired" is therefore a legitimate first-class going-forward state (NOT to be confused with the legacy Retool `provisional_paid` flag). **Boundary:** payout module CREATES settlements; reconciliation engine CONFIRMS them. NOTE: the current historical bridge (`scripts/vr2_post_pairings.py`, the 721) is the OPPOSITE ordering — bank txn exists first, pairing CREATES the settlement JE — because no payout module ran for legacy invoices; it is unaffected by this going-forward design. Informs VR-7 productization. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **POL-87** GST POSTING IS A PER-ENTITY RULE — REGISTERED ENTITIES ONLY (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). Whether an invoice posts GST is decided **by entity**, as an explicit invoice-posting rule — NOT derived from country or currency (there is no reliable `country`/`gst_rate` flag; `gst_rate` is null on all entities). **GST-registered entities = {3 Drive lah Australia}**; NON-registered = {1 Ventures Holding SG, 2 Drive lah Singapore}. A registered entity posts the 3-line GST bill (Dr expense net + Dr 1350 GST Input / Cr AP total); a non-registered entity posts NO `1350` line — the full GST-inclusive total is expensed as cost (2-line: Dr expense full / Cr AP), because the GST is non-recoverable. This holds **even when the invoice carries an extracted GST amount** (extraction still captures it and keeps it on the record; it is simply not posted). The flag lives on the ENTITY (`finance_entities.gst_rate`: AU id3 = 0.10, SG id1/id2 = NULL) and is enforced in the single shared **bill-JE builder** `invoice_service.approve()` — it emits the `1350` line only when `entity.gst_rate > 0`, else forces the full total to expense. The **same** `entity.gst_rate` is already read by the categorization engine (`categorization_service._get_gst_rate`), so setting AU=0.10 also activates AU GST there — one flag, all engines. The knock-off/payment side never touches GST (payment JE is always Dr AP / Cr bank). Both engines therefore share one rule. Future POL-44 GST-engine work must honor this. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **DQ-85** RETOOL PAYMENT-REQUEST CONTEXT LIVES IN FREE-TEXT, NOT FIELDS (2026-08-04). Of 1,759 Retool invoices, the structured `trip_id` field is populated on only **4** (field present on 1,344, empty). The REAL context sits in the free-text `retool_ref.description` — a semi-structured template the requester typed: **Back-office ticket number** (~544 / 31%), **Rego** (571), **Reason for payment/credit** (554), **Team** (542), **Charged to member** + guest id like `C785178C-0012` (539), occasionally Trip / Invoice Number / "Approved By" / Slack link. The **guest/member id ("Charged to member")** is the golden linkage key to the trip/member; the recurring phrase **"Unable to charge guest"** is the exact risk signal (we pay because guest-recovery failed → approver must verify it's legitimate, not a mistake/duplicate). Implication (AW-8/B9): a pre-approval **enrichment agent** must PARSE the free-text → extract ticket#/rego/guest-id/reason/trip → PULL the linked TMS trip/member + back-office/Intercom ticket → SYNTHESISE one case brief + risk flags, before the approver sees it. *(Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-88** INTERCOM TICKETS = READ FROM `Intercom_db_v2` SYNC DB, NOT THE API (2026-08-04). The Intercom REST API **rejects `ticket_id` search** on current versions ("ticket_id is not a valid ticket search field") and fetch-by-id needs the 15-digit INTERNAL id, not the display number. Reliable source = the **`Intercom_db_v2` MySQL analytics replica** (`analytics-db.cfey08sq43eh.ap-southeast-1.rds.amazonaws.com:3308`, config `ANALYTICS_DATABASE_CONFIG`, copied into finance-api `.env`), table **`tickets`** keyed by the human display **`ticket_id`** = exactly the Retool "back office ticket" number. Columns: `ticket_attributes` (JSON — `_default_title_` carries rego+guest+host; `_default_description_` often carries the Trip ID), `ticket_type`, `ticket_state`, `ticket_parts` (full thread), `category`, `country`. LOCAL client built: `src/clients/intercom_client.py :: IntercomClient.get_ticket_by_number`. PROVEN in the Approval Agent (`scripts/approval_agent.py`): ticket 94099654 → "[Total Loss] 1RT7DP 2016 Nissan Pulsar | Guest Peter Tournier | Host Vishal Kamra" (56 parts); agent cross-verified the $330 invoice against the ticket's $330 payment-request id and flagged the declined-guest write-off. *(Pickle, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-86** TRIP + USER DATA LIVES IN CLICKHOUSE — the Approval Agent's trip connector (verified 2026-08-04). Client: `src/clients/clickhouse_client.py` (host `54.169.212.254:8123`, db `default`; creds in the client/env). Trip = **`au_transactions` / `sg_transactions`**: `id` (Sharetribe UUID), `bookingStart`/`bookingEnd`, `customerId` (=guest), `providerId` (=host), `lastTransition` (what happened, e.g. `transition/customer-cancel-after-accepted-one-hour`), `lineItems`, `payin/payout`. Join to **`au_users` / `sg_users`** (`id`, `firstName`, `lastName`, `email`, `displayName`) on customerId/providerId for guest+host names. Also `au_sharetribe_bookings` / `flexplus_bookings`. PROVEN: one join returns trip dates + transition + guest + host names — the "what was this trip / why pay" answer. **Linking caveat:** the Retool "member/guest" code (e.g. `7398E411-0004`, `C785178C-0012` = 8-hex + 4-digit) is an APP DISPLAY REFERENCE, NOT a transaction/user UUID prefix — extraction must resolve it to the CH `id` (display-ref rule or lookup, TBD). *(Pickle live CH scan, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-89** DUPLICATE INVOICES SHARE A `pdf_content_hash` — SAME SOURCE DOC INGESTED TWICE (Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04). `finance_invoices.pdf_content_hash` has a UNIQUE constraint (`ix_finance_invoices_pdf_content_hash`); duplicates exist because the same PDF was ingested into two+ invoice rows, sometimes with DIFFERENT amounts (e.g. inv881=300 AUD and inv2406=2000 AUD share one hash; inv888 has three copies at ±168.97). **Table-wide: 46 hash groups / 131 invoices are duplicates.** The posting engine surfaces them for free — approve()'s status UPDATE re-asserts the unique constraint and throws `UniqueViolation`, so a dup HARD-STOPS the batch (caught 2026-08-04: inv2406). **RULE: never force-post a pdf-hash duplicate** — hold for review (is it a true dup → VOID one, or two real payments that happened to share a placeholder/screenshot → fix the hash). Of the current provisional-postable set, 4 touched it (888, 2406, 1311, 1036) → excluded via `EXCLUDE_INVOICES` in `scripts/vr2_post_provisional.py`. The other 42 groups are a broader data-quality cleanup (VR-12). *(Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-88** THE 354 "DATA-DEFECTIVE" OPEN INVOICES = 324 RECOVERABLE (missing counterparty/date, amount INTACT) + 30 AMOUNTLESS-JUNK (Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04; supersedes two earlier mis-splits — 158/196 and the "166-recoverable-via-extraction" reasoning, both WRONG). Of the 884 open invoices, 354 are defective on three INDEPENDENT, overlapping axes: **244 no-counterparty · 181 stub-date (1900/1901) · 30 no-amount** (defect matrix: 150 no-cp-only, 97 stub-only, 77 no-cp+stub, 13 no-amt-only, 10 no-cp+no-amt, 7 all-three = 354). **WHERE `total_amount=0` COMES FROM — DEFINITIVE (strict query on the ROW's own `total_amount=0/null` = exactly 30 rows):** in ALL 30, BOTH upstream fields are zero — `retool_ref.amount=0` AND `extraction.total_amount=0`. There is NO row where the OCR had an amount but the row is 0 (ingestion already coalesces retool→extraction). So `total_amount=0` means genuinely no amount ANYWHERE, and those 30 are cancelled/junk (their own text: "DELETE"/"delete"/"NA"/"Please disregard"/"cancelling this request"/"Deleting this request as invoice changed"/incentive-sheet/birthday-cake) → VOID. **The OTHER 324 defective invoices ALREADY HAVE A VALID `total_amount` on the row** — their defect is a missing COUNTERPARTY (244) and/or STUB DATE (181), both recoverable. (Earlier I claimed 166 needed amount-recovery from `extraction.total_amount`; WRONG — those 166 never had a zero row-amount; the amount was populated at ingestion. Amount is a non-issue for all but the 30.) **RECOVERY FIELD MAP (for the 324):** counterparty ← `retool_ref.payee` (structured on only **59** — the real gap) else resolve from `description` payee-name per POL-97 (e.g. inv260 doc="AFCA dispute letter" but retool_ref=`payee:Blake Forsyth, 300 AUD, Insurance claim payout`); invoice_date ← `retool_ref.created_at` (185 present) else `extraction.invoice_date`; COA ← `retool_ref.category`/`sub_category_details` on a plate (3rd-party-payment/Workshop/Towing/insurance-claim/premium) else `extraction.suggested_coa_account`; `description`→ticket/rego/reason/guest-id (DQ-85); `finance_db_id`→Retool PK. **RECOVERY PLAY (VR-11):** backfill counterparty + date (+ COA) for the 324, then they become pairable AND postable; VOID the 30 amountless. *(Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-93** FUZZY COUNTERPARTY MATCHER CROSS-ATTRIBUTES WITHIN NAME CLUSTERS (Gaurav caught, 2026-08-08). The txn categorizer's `counterparty_default` logic uses rapidfuzz `token_set_ratio` over the bank description vs counterparty names/aliases. In a cluster of similarly-named vendors (11 "Smash Repairs"), GENERIC shared tokens (SMASH/PTY/LTD) outweigh the DISTINGUISHING token (A1), so "Sent money to **A1** Smash Pty Ltd" mis-mapped to **SEMITS** Smash (3 shared generic tokens vs A1 Smash Repairs' 2) — worsened because the correct vendor (cp 714) likely didn't exist yet, so the matcher grabbed the closest existing sibling. 3 found+fixed on prod 2026-08-08 (9788→714 A1, 10596→710 Jonkers, 10682→708 Morley) — all RECONCILED, NOT paired, same COA 5032 so **P&L unaffected (attribution-only error)**; added distinguishing aliases (`A1 Smash`/`Jonkers Smash`/`Morley Smash`) so exact-substring beats fuzzy next time. **NEW CAPABILITY:** finance can now self-serve reassign a txn's counterparty — `POST /transactions/:id/counterparty` + a per-row "change" control in the Transactions tab. **RULE (Gaurav):** counterparty is reassignable ONLY when the txn is NOT paired to an invoice — a paired/posted txn is LOCKED (detach the invoice match first); enforced by checking `finance_invoice_payment_matches`. Metadata-only (COA/JE untouched). HARDENING TODO: weight distinguishing tokens over generic ones in the fuzzy matcher (task #6 alias audit). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-08)*
- **DQ-92** THE TASK IS NOT THE SYSTEM OF RECORD — invoice-sourced tasks READ LIVE from the invoice (Gaurav caught this, 2026-08-06). The generic task inbox denormalizes `title`/`amount`/`currency` at submit so the queue can render any task (invoice/claim/payout) uniformly without joining each source. For CLAIMS/PAYOUTS the task owns its amount — fine. For INVOICE tasks the invoice is the system of record, so the frozen snapshot DRIFTS the moment the invoice is edited: team edited inv 242 → 800, but the approval card still showed SGD 1,000 (and could mislead the approver, though `approve()` books the live invoice amount, so the ledger was always correct — display-only bug). FIX: `task_service.hydrate_source(db, dicts)` overrides `amount`/`currency`/`title` (and body.amount/invoice_number) from the LIVE invoice for `source_ref` `invoice:<id>` tasks, applied at BOTH serve points (`GET /tasks` list + `GET /tasks/:id`). No per-edit sync, no data migration, fixes all past+future tasks. RULE: never trust a task's denormalized fields for an invoice task — hydrate from the invoice. *(2026-08-06)*
- **DQ-91** CONSOLE MODULE NAME vs CARD ID vs TEAM NAME — keep them ONE string per module (Pickle, 2026-08-06). The HR console was blocked for everyone but super-admins because THREE names were in play for one concept: the permission **module** granted in `user_permissions` is `hr` (4 admins hold it: dirkjan/gauravs/rahul/zilla; BFF gate `requireModuleAccess('hr','read')`), but the dashboard **card id** was `human-resources` (0 grants — never a real module) and `cardHasAccess` checks `userModules.includes(cardId)` → always false → locked. Compounding it, `hr` was never in the `MODULES` registry at all — it only existed in the `TEAMS` list, so it was an "unregistered" module that worked purely via direct grants. FIX (no DB change; grants were already `hr`): registered `hr` in `MODULES` (both FE + admin-bff), renamed the card id `human-resources`→`hr` so card id == module name like every other card, deleted the `CARD_MODULE_ALIASES` band-aid. **RULE:** a console module has ONE canonical string used identically for (a) the `MODULES` registry entry, (b) the dashboard card id, (c) the `user_permissions.module` grant, and (d) the `requireModuleAccess()` route gate. The `hr` **team** (org-chart axis, `users.team`) sharing the string with the `hr` **module** (permission axis) is fine — different checks — but never let a card id or grant drift to a synonym (`human-resources`). Verified by exercising `cardHasAccess('hr',[…'hr'])===true` / without===false; FE+BFF deployed live 2026-08-06 07:55. *(Pickle, 2026-08-06)*
- **DQ-90** DEDUP UNIQUE INDEXES MUST EXCLUDE THE PRE-LEDGER STATUSES (Pickle, 2026-08-06). The `pdf_content_hash` and `uq_finance_invoices_semantic` partial unique indexes originally excluded only `draft/void/rejected` (mig 051/046). POL-107 added `reconcile`/`paired`/`needs_fix` — pre-ledger states where duplicates MUST coexist (needs_fix is literally where a flagged duplicate lands). Moving a duplicate draft into ANY of them (incl. the live `submit()`→`needs_fix` path for a dup) violated the constraint (`UniqueViolation`), and the first triage `--apply` aborted on it (safe — full rollback, prod untouched). FIX: both indexes now exclude `draft,void,rejected,reconcile,paired,needs_fix`; uniqueness enforced only on live states (`pending_approval,approved,partially_paid,paid`, and dup-detection already routes dups away from pending_approval). Shipped as mig `052_dedup_indexes_exclude_preledger` + applied to prod directly 2026-08-06 (idempotent DROP+CREATE). LESSON: any new invoice status that is NOT a committed liability must be added to these two exclusion lists. *(Pickle, 2026-08-06)*
- **DQ-89** `db_session()` COMMITS EVEN AFTER AN INNER `db.rollback()` — "transactional rollback" testing against prod is UNSAFE (Pickle, 2026-08-04). The finance-api `with db_session() as db:` context manager commits on normal `__exit__`. Calling `db.rollback()` as the LAST line inside the block does NOT prevent persistence: an e2e test that created + submitted a claim, then called `db.rollback()`, still left the claim (`status=submitted`) AND its enqueued task committed in shared prod (had to be deleted by hand afterward). Rollback only undoes work still pending at the moment it runs; any auto-commit/flush behaviour of the session wrapper on block-exit re-commits. **RULE:** never rely on inner rollback to keep a test clean against the shared prod DB. To exercise write paths safely, either (a) run against a throwaway/local DB, (b) wrap in an explicit SAVEPOINT/`begin_nested` and roll THAT back while raising out of the block before the outer commit, or (c) accept it will persist and delete the exact rows by a unique marker immediately after (verify count→0). *(Pickle, 2026-08-04)*
- **DQ-87** GT EXCESS PAYMENTS: +$1 SURCHARGE, NO INVOICE NUMBER, ROUND & IDENTICAL (Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04). GT excess/deductible (POL-86, COA 5036) is settled by direct bank transfers described only as *"Sent money to GT Insurance-Trust"* — **no reference number, no invoice number** (the invoices themselves are `#None`). Invoices are round ($300, $2000, $2500, $3000); the matching payment is consistently the round amount **+$1** (a processing surcharge: 2000→2001, 300→301, 3000→3001). Dozens of identical $2001 / $301 payments exist against dozens of like invoices, so no single payment is distinguishable from another — reference matching is impossible and exact-amount matching is many-to-many ambiguous. This is why 45 GT invoices sat open after every name/amount matcher. Resolved by fungible FIFO pairing (POL-99): 32 of 40 date-good GT invoices paired 2026-08-04; 8 have no available payment (incl. one $0.00), 5 more are stub-dated (1900-01-01) with NO recoverable date and stay open. Contrast the PENGUIN premium lane, which DOES carry the invoice number in the DEFT BPAY reference (*"...BPAY... invoice 225890"*) and matches cleanly — its few open items are batch/catch-up payments (one BPAY covering many months, e.g. a $174k "May Dec 25" line). *(Pickle live scan, 2026-08-04)*
- **POL-86** INSURANCE-VENDOR EXCESS/DEDUCTIBLE COA RULE (Gaurav, 2026-08-03). For claims-insurance counterparties, small-dollar invoices are **excess/deductible** payments and book to **`5036` Cost of Insurance - Excess/Deductible** (COST_OF_SALES). Bands Gaurav gave: **GT Insurance** (cp752) any invoice **< $10k** = excess-deductible; **Income Insurance** (cp694) a few-thousand = excess-deductible; **Tokio Marine** (cp144) ~$3–4k = excess-deductible BUT larger Tokio invoices ($13k–30k) are NOT excess — they are trip-premium / claim settlements and book to **`5035` Cost of Insurance - Trip Premium** (Tokio's own reconciled history confirms 5035 for the large ones). Ground truth: Income/Tokio reconciled payments already use 5036; GT also books 4030 Insurance **Recoveries** (revenue, money IN — do not confuse with excess OUT). Applied 2026-08-03: stamped 72 invoices → 5036 (GT 61 + Income 7 + Tokio-small 4) and 7 large Tokio → 5035; set GT + Income vendor `default_account_code=5036` (Tokio left null — it splits). Backup: `documentation/wip/coa_stamp_backup_20260803_160705.json`. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-03)*
- **DQ-84** NON-RETOOL INVOICES ARE ALL DRAFT + PROVISIONALLY-UNPAID, YET MANY HAVE A REAL BANK PAYMENT (2026-08-03). 188 of 1,947 invoices are non-Retool (no `retool_ref.finance_db_id`); every one is `status=draft` and `provisional_paid=false` (correct — no Retool Closed signal to inherit). 183 have a counterparty (5 do not: ids 1633/1650/1693/1723/1800, all with null extracted vendor_name). Running the PAYLINE amount+date+reference engine on the 175 non-dup, cp-identified ones found **101 defensible matches** (70 HIGH/31 MED, 77 reference-confirmed) against real outflows — so Retool's paid flag under-counts actual payments for this batch. Written to the master as MATCHED (829→930, all payments unique). Engine: `scripts/payline_nonretool_match.py`; proposals: `documentation/wip/PAYLINE_NONRETOOL_PROPOSALS.csv`. *(Pickle, 2026-08-03)*
- **DQ-76** DBS PDF PARSE QUIRK (parse-audit VR-1a, 2026-08-02). The DBS multi-currency adapter's `_parse_line` takes the last two whitespace tokens as (amount, balance) and recovers the sign from the running-balance delta. On **telegraphic-transfer rows** (outward/inward TT) the PDF's withdrawal and deposit columns COLLIDE in text extraction, so the wrong amount/balance pair is grabbed and the balance chain desyncs from that row on — Σamounts then misses by exactly the mis-parsed amount (e.g. Sep-2024 USD off by 65,090.00). Hit **8 of 52 DBS files** (USD 2023-04/05/10, 2024-08/09/11, 2025-04; SGD 2025-02); everything else (OCBC 131/131, CBA quarterly, DBS non-TT) reconciles to the cent. FIX (VR-1b): derive the amount from the reliable printed-balance delta (curr − prev) and/or positional (x-coordinate) column extraction instead of whitespace tokens; gate = each statement self-reconciles opening+Σ=closing. Also: the CBA monthly "TransactionSummary" interim format needs its own period-header pattern, and an `.xlsx` sits in the DBS PDF folder (route or exclude). *(parse-audit, 2026-08-02)*
  ~~**CORRECTION (VR-1b fix, 2026-08-02): the column-collision root cause above was a MISDIAGNOSIS.**~~ Traced against raw PDFs: DBS row parsing and running-balance sign recovery were ALREADY 100% correct (firstBF + Σrows == lastCF to the cent for every failing file). The REAL defect: when a currency section spans pages, DBS reprints `Currency: XXX` + `Balance Brought Forward` + `Balance Carried Forward` on each CONTINUATION page, and the adapter let the last page's brought-forward OVERWRITE the section's true opening anchor (e.g. Sep-2024 USD true open 155,715.10 was overwritten by the page-3 reprint 90,625.10, delta 65,090). FIX = one guard in `dbs_pdf.py parse_pdf`: record `brought_forward` only on the FIRST occurrence per currency (`carried_forward` keeps taking the last); `prev_balance` still re-seeds from each page-break BF for sign recovery. CBA monthly interim got a dedicated `_parse_interim` branch (derives open/close from the running-balance chain — no printed anchors); the redundant DBS `.xlsx` is excluded from the audit (byte-content dup of the passing 012023.pdf). **RESULT: audit 278/278 = 100%**, OCBC/CBA-quarterly unchanged (no regression), +2 regression test files (`test_dbs_adapter.py` multi-page anchor; CBA monthly-interim). *(VR-1b, 2026-08-02)*
- **DQ-75** DBS (Ventures Holding) BANK ACCOUNT OPENED MID-2022, earliest txns **Jun 2022** — NOT 2019 (the Ventures ENTITY's QB books start Jun-2019, but the DBS bank account itself is later). So there is NO Jun-2019→May-2022 statement gap for DBS. Coverage on hand (live re-scan 2026-08-02): continuous **Jun-2022→Oct-2025**, then **Jan–Jun-2026**; **MISSING Nov–Dec 2025** (a 2025+ gap → Gaurav supplies, per POL-82). The DBS filename customer-ID `C394652019G` contains the string "2019" but that is an ID, not a statement year (source of an earlier false-positive). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-02)*
- **POL-124** PAYOUTS DATA MODEL — three layers, not one flat table (Gaurav + Pickle, 2026-08-14). A **counterparty** (every payee is one: vendors, employees, lenders) has 1..N **`counterparty_bank_account`** rows (the real bank account: currency, country, holder, account number/IBAN/BSB/SWIFT — NO recipient id). Each account is registered into 1..N **`payment_channel`** rails (Wise SG / Wise AU / Wise Ventures today; future DBS, bank-file) via **`payout_channel_registration`**, which holds that channel's own `external_recipient_id`. The SAME account registered on two channels has two different recipient ids. Proven on Wise: Dirk-Jan (cp 268) has an SGD Singapore account (recipient 297347886) AND a EUR NL IBAN (289769838) under the SG profile, and ZERO recipients under AU. `payment_channel.our_entity_id` replaces the hardcoded `ENTITY_WISE_PROFILE = {1:74921502, 2:13811029, 3:41524706}` map. Spec: `wip/PAYOUTS_DATA_MODEL.md`; additive migration `059_payout_channels` (clone-verified, prod-pending). *(Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-125** PAYOUT REFERENCE-DATA IS AUDITED (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). Money-routing data (bank accounts, channel registrations) changing = money can be rerouted, so every add/update/delete writes an append-only row to **`finance_payout_reference_audit`** (target_type, action, before/after JSON, actor, ip). Closes the gap where bank-account CRUD had NO trail. Payout STATE changes were already audited in `finance_payout_events` (POL-126). *(Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-126** A PAYOUT IS A STATE MACHINE (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). `PayoutState`: DRAFT → REQUESTED (maker raised; checker required only ≥ SGD 1,000) → SENT (approved, money left Wise) → AWAITING_IMPORT (waiting for the bank line) → POSTED (terminal: bank txn paired + knock-off JE posted); plus FAILED, CANCELLED. Every transition is append-only in `finance_payout_events`. **OPEN DECISION (two arms):** the finance team also pays manually from bank/Wise consoles TODAY — does `finance_payouts` become the SINGLE register for ALL payouts (a `source`/`origin` field: `system_wise` vs `external_manual`, the external arm skipping SENT and reconciling straight from bank import), OR do manual payouts stay only in the invoice↔payment reconciliation lane? Recommend the unified register (mirrors the invoice state machine's two-arm design). *(Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **DQ-100** WISE RECIPIENTS ARE PER-PROFILE AND PER-ACCOUNT (Pickle, 2026-08-14). A Wise recipient id belongs to ONE profile; the same real bank account registered under a second profile gets a NEW id, and an account not yet registered on a profile has none there. Also a payee can have several recipients within one profile (one per bank account/currency). Consequence: the recipient id can never live on the counterparty or the account row — it belongs on the per-channel registration (POL-124). Today 1 of 624 counterparties is mapped; Wise already holds 120 recipients across our 3 profiles (104 SG, 11 AU, 5 Ventures) available to backfill-match. *(Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-127** BANK ACCOUNTS — OUR SYSTEM IS THE MASTER (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). Finance manages a payee's bank accounts (multiple allowed) INSIDE the counterparty record (not a separate module, finance-edit-only). **Add:** enter once with us → we call Wise `create_recipient` per channel → store the registration. **Edit:** Wise recipients are IMMUTABLE (can't change an account number in place — a change is a NEW recipient), so an edit creates a new recipient and SUPERSEDES the registration (old inactive, new active); the account row keeps its identity. **Delete:** deactivate (status flip) + audit, never hard-delete. **Backfill** (reverse, one-time): pull recipients already in Wise, match, link. Changes are done from OUR system, finance-only; a periodic reconcile-pull flags drift from direct-in-Wise edits. All mutations audited (POL-125). Needs new `wise_service.create_recipient` (only `list_recipients` exists). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-128** PAYOUT ↔ PAYABLE ↔ BANK-TXN LINKAGE (Gaurav, 2026-08-14). `finance_payouts` is the single register of every payment finance makes from the console; each row links BOTH to **what it pays** (generalise the invoice-only link to polymorphic `payable_type` + `payable_id`: invoice | payroll | other — today only `invoice_id` exists) AND to **the bank line that settles it** (`transaction_id`, set by the importer on `wise_transfer_id` match, + `match_id` + `journal_entry_id`). Chain = payable ← finance_payouts → bank transaction → JE, traceable both directions. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-14)*
- **DQ-101** WISE RECIPIENT-CREATION RULES (Pickle, 2026-08-14, verified live). `POST /v1/accounts` gotchas: (a) `accountHolderName` must contain **NO numbers** (422 `NOT_VALID`). (b) SGD local (`type='singapore'`) needs `details={legalType, accountNumber, bankCode}` where **bankCode is the 4-digit bank code** (e.g. `7171` DBS — `7171-001` is rejected). (c) AUD = `type='australian'` {bsbCode, accountNumber}; else `type='iban'` {IBAN}. (d) Recipients are IMMUTABLE — no edit endpoint; a change = new create + `DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}` (soft-delete, still referenced by past transfers). The robust way to know required fields per currency is Wise **account-requirements**, not hardcoding. Proven by create+delete round-trips on profile 13811029. *(Pickle, 2026-08-14)*
- **POL-129** WISE TRANSFER = 3 CALLS, MONEY MOVES ON THE 2ND (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-15, verified). (1) quote, (2) `POST /v1/transfers` creates a record — status `incoming_payment_waiting`, **NO money moves**; (3) `POST /v3/profiles/{id}/transfers/{tid}/payments {"type":"BALANCE"}` **funds from balance** — this is the money-move, SCA-gated (403+x-2fa-approval → sign → retry). Wise NEVER auto-debits the balance; every payment is explicitly authorised. Terminal statuses: `outgoing_payment_sent` (delivered) / `funds_refunded`/`bounced_back` (failed, money returned) / `cancelled`. Cross-profile rule: a recipient belongs to ONE profile — paying it from another profile is rejected (`invalid currency ["SGD","AUD"]`), which is why paying Dirk's SG recipient from the AU profile failed. Canonical: `wip/PAYOUT_MODEL.md`. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-130** PAYOUT STATE MACHINE (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). `draft→requested→sent→awaiting_import→posted`, +`failed`/`cancelled`. `sent`=transfer created AND funded (money left balance); `awaiting_import`=set ONLY on a real Wise **delivery signal** (`outgoing_payment_sent`), never on "funded" alone; `posted`=the import paired it. **Delivery confirmation via Wise webhook (`transfers#state-change`) primary + polling `GET /v1/transfers/{id}` backup** — NOT built yet; its absence is the id-8 phantom (we marked success before Wise confirmed; the transfer actually `funds_refunded`). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-131** CATEGORIZATION ENGINE IS THE SOLE MATCHER FOR PAYOUTS (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). A payout reconciles `awaiting_import→posted` ONLY through the normal periodic (daily) Wise **transaction import** + the import-pair hook (VP-5) matching on `wise_transfer_id`. NO one-off fetch of the single transaction, NO parallel matcher. Idempotent via `balance_transaction_id` dedup, so the daily "pull everything (last-sync incremental + overlap)" never duplicates. A refund produces no outgoing line → never pairs → payout `failed`, invoice never `paid`. **Do not build matching in the payout machine.** *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-132** INVOICE `payment_initiated` + PAID⟺MATCHED (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). New invoice state between `approved` and `paid`. Payout fired → invoice `approved→payment_initiated` (money on its way, NOT paid). Reaches `paid` ONLY when the real outgoing transaction imports and the categorization engine pairs it on `wise_transfer_id`. Wise `funds_refunded`/`bounced_back` → revert `payment_initiated→approved` for review. Invariant: **an invoice is `paid` only when a real transaction is matched to it** — `payment_initiated` is the holding state that fixes the id-8 phantom. Invoice side: `wip/INVOICES_STATE_MACHINE.md`. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-133** PAY-TIME SAFETY — BLOCK-AND-REGISTER + REVIEW-BEFORE-PAY (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). If the counterparty has NO active registration on the paying channel, the payout is BLOCKED ("add a bank account for this channel first"), never silently routed to a different-currency/other-profile account (the root of the AU failure — our code grabbed the SGD account). Before firing, the operator sees `payee · account(masked) · channel · amount` and confirms. No blind pay. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **DQ-102** id-8 PHANTOM PAYOUT (Pickle, 2026-08-15). Payout id 8 (invoice 2487, $1 SGD) showed `awaiting_import`/success on our dashboard but Wise transfer 2311266073 was `funds_refunded` — it funded from balance then bounced back (SG balance returned to 464.76, nothing net left). Cause: `_send` marks the payout done the instant `fund_transfer` returns, and we never re-check Wise. Voided to `cancelled` (invoice 2487 back to `approved`, no ledger effect). Root fix = POL-130 delivery confirmation + POL-132 `payment_initiated`. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-134** THREE DISTINCT WISE CREDENTIALS — do not confuse (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-15). (1) **`WISE_API_KEY`** — our bearer token, authenticates US → Wise. (2) **Our SCA keypair** (`~/.wise_sca/wise_sca_private.pem` + `_public.pem`) — OUR RSA keys; we uploaded the public half to Wise and sign fund/2FA challenges with the private half (WE sign, Wise verifies). (3) **Wise webhook public key** (`~/.wise_sca/wise_webhook_public.pem`, path in `WISE_WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_KEY_PATH`) — WISE's OWN production RSA-2048 public key from their webhook "verifying signatures" docs; Wise signs each `X-Signature-SHA256` webhook and WE verify (WISE signs, we verify). Installed + verified 2026-08-15: a signed Wise cancel event verified and drove a payout to failed; an unsigned probe was rejected. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-135** PAID-FAMILY DISPLAY LABELS — the payout state stays honest but the UI reads "paid" (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-15). A payable that we've sent-but-not-yet-confirmed (`payment_initiated`) shows **"Paid (in transit)"**; one moved to the reconciliation arm (`reconcile`, i.e. marked-paid-outside or a paired-awaiting-import) shows **"Paid (reconciling)"**; a fully matched one shows **"Paid"**. Rationale: to the front-end/ops audience "reconciled means paid already", so the label must not read as unpaid while the state machine keeps `paid ⟺ a matched real transaction`. Lives in `admincontrols .../utils/paymentStatus.ts` (`paymentStatusDisplay` + `STATUS_TONE_CLASS`). *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-136** PARTIAL PAYMENTS + MARK-PAID CAPTURE — a payable can be paid for LESS than its remaining balance, on BOTH rails (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). `remaining = total_amount − amount_paid`; any pay action accepts `amount` where `0 < amount ≤ remaining` (tolerance 0.01), leaving the invoice `partially_paid` with the shortfall still owed. **Paying through the system** (Wise): `create_payout(..., amount=)` — operator may lower the amount against the invoice before sending; pairs on `wise_transfer_id` as usual (POL-131). **Marking paid outside the system**: `mark_paid_already(..., amount, source_bank_account_id, reference)` captures WHICH of our bank accounts we paid from (dropdown of our real accounts — CBA, Stripe Connect, Wise AUD/USD, …), the EXACT amount (partial allowed), and an OPTIONAL free-text reference — **no transaction id is asked** (the operator won't have one; the categorization engine still pairs the real bank line on import, POL-131). Every mark-paid writes an append-only `finance_payout_reference_audit` row (`target_type='invoice_mark_paid'`, `action='mark_paid'` — note: `action` column is varchar(16), so the verb is `mark_paid` not `mark_paid_already`) capturing before-status/amount_paid and after amount/account/reference/remaining_before. The audit is part of the deliverable: a failed audit write ABORTS the mark-paid (no silent success). *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-137** PAYOUT CURRENCY = PAYABLE CURRENCY, FUNDED FROM THE SAME-CURRENCY BALANCE, NO SOURCE-SIDE CONVERSION (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). We pay a payable (invoice/payroll) in ITS OWN currency: the Wise quote is `sourceCurrency == targetCurrency == payable.currency`, `targetAmount = payable amount`. The money must come OUT of our Wise balance held in that same currency (our Wise accounts are multi-currency). If we don't hold that currency's balance, or hold too little, the pay is **BLOCKED** with a "top up the <ccy> balance" message — we NEVER auto-convert from another balance to fund a payout. Whatever conversion Wise does at DELIVERY to land it in the vendor's local-currency account (e.g. a USD salary reaching a Philippine bank as PHP) is the destination's business and we don't care about it. Enforced by `payout_service._assert_source_balance(profile, ccy, amount)`, called in `_send` before the quote is created (covers both the under-threshold auto-send and the checker-approved send). Example: USD salary → pay USD from our USD balance; if USD balance is short, block and top up — do not pay from AUD. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **DQ-103** WISE ACCOUNT-HOLDER NAME ≠ OUR COUNTERPARTY NAME — recipient→counterparty mapping needs a HUMAN (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). A Wise recipient's `accountHolderName` is frequently NOT the counterparty we book against: e.g. Gaurav's personal Wise recipient pays into a bank account held in the name of **RR Ventures** — only a human who knows the relationship can map it. So the name-fuzzy backfill (PM-5) is a HINT only; the authoritative map is built by a person. Tool: a self-contained interactive HTML (`documentation/wip/zilla_recipient_mapping.html`, generated by `build_zilla_mapping_tool.py`) listing every Wise recipient across all 3 profiles (SG/AU/Ventures) with a searchable 600+ counterparty picker + comment, autosaving to the browser, exporting JSON. Each row shows the FULL Wise bank details (bank, account no., BSB/SWIFT/IBAN/IFSC/ABA, account type, address, email — not just the account number) so she can identify the account. Pre-suggested rows carry a one-click **"✓ OK, correct"** so she confirms a right suggestion without re-picking; an untouched suggestion still exports (with `confirmed:false`, `from_suggestion:true`) so "leave it if it's fine" works. Export fields per row: `counterparty_id`, `no_match`, `confirmed`, `from_suggestion`, `comment`. Zilla (finance) fills it; we ingest the JSON confirm-gated into `payout_channel_registration` (money-routing, DQ-100). GOAL: every Wise recipient should be a counterparty on our side. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **DQ-104** `finance_payout_bank_accounts` IS NOT DEAD — the HR module still uses it (Pickle, 2026-08-15). The legacy embedded-recipient table also backs the **HR employee-bank-account UI** (`/api/finance/payee-bank-accounts` route + BFF proxy + `hrService.ts`, `payee_type='employee'`). So the PM-4b cutover repointed the PAYOUT ENGINE off it (routing now via `channel_id`/`registration_id` → the channel/registration model) but did **NOT drop the table** — dropping it would break HR. It's retired only when HR migrates employee bank accounts to `counterparty_bank_account` (employees are counterparties, ENT — a separate HR task). Prod currently holds 1 row (counterparty type, the migrated Dirk-Jan account); 0 employee rows yet. *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-138** PAYOUT REGISTER = `finance_payouts` (PM-4b cutover, 2026-08-15). The register table was renamed `finance_vendor_payouts → finance_payouts` (+ `_events`), because it pays vendors AND (next) payroll. Payee routing is `channel_id` (paying `payment_channel`) + `registration_id` (the resolved `payout_channel_registration` → Wise recipient) — the legacy embedded `bank_account_id`/`finance_payout_bank_accounts` fallback is GONE from the engine. `invoice_id` is now NULLABLE (the typed FK for invoice payouts; payroll/other rows carry `payable_type`/`payable_id` and leave it NULL). Model classes are `FinancePayout`/`FinancePayoutEvent` with back-compat aliases `FinanceVendorPayout`/`FinanceVendorPayoutEvent`. Migrations: 060 (additive method/payable_*) then 061 (rename + channel/registration + drop bank_account_id). Apply to prod FOREGROUND + supervised (prod at 059). *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-140** PAYROLL BUILDS ON THE EXISTING HR TABLES — don't reinvent (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-15). The per-employee payroll engine ALREADY exists: **`hr_employees`** (tax_treatment = statutory-handled flag, salary_expense_code = Dr COA, teams/designation = pay group, region, manager), **`hr_compensation`** (effective-dated: pay_type FIXED_SALARY|HOURLY_RATE, gross_amount), **`hr_deduction_rules`** (per-employee CPF/super/income-tax rules incl coa_debit/credit), **`hr_payroll_items`** (one payslip per employee per run: gross/deductions/employer_contributions/net/hours_worked/deduction_lines JSONB), **`finance_payroll_runs`** (the run + status), and `hr_payroll_service.create_run` already computes gross/deductions/net per employee. **Segmented approval = the COA matrix, NOT a COO list:** it's `finance_coa_config` (keyed on coa_code → approver_1/2 + thresholds, the same sign-off gate invoices use) — payroll routes each line's `salary_expense_code` through it, so different salary accounts (salaries vs customer-support salaries) approve separately. **Pay schedule (the ONE genuinely-new field, mig 062):** `hr_compensation.pay_schedule` = **`monthly`** (paid once at month-end, the 2nd run — the DEFAULT, HR sets at onboarding) | **`semi_monthly`** (paid in BOTH runs, `pay_split_pct`% on the 15th default 50, balance at month-end). Two run dates: mid-month (15th) + end-of-month. **Real gaps to build:** pay_schedule ✅(this) · fuller run state machine (draft→calculated→pending_approval→approved→payment_initiated→paid) · segmented approval via finance_coa_config · register fan-out (per-employee net + statutory → finance_payouts, POL-139) · hourly calc (data ready, phase 2). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-139** THE FIVE FINANCE-PAYABLE CATEGORIES + their rail/ledger (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). Every payable flows through the ONE register (`finance_payouts`, polymorphic `payable_type`/`payable_id`); categories differ only by (a) execution RAIL and (b) the liability the payment knocks off. **(1) Invoice** — vendor cp; Wise or mark-paid; Dr 2000-family AP. DONE. **(2) Payroll** — employee cp (POL-112); one RUN fans out into several payables (net salary→2304, super→2302, PAYG→2305, POL-76); AU salaries run via ACE/QBO (record+accrue, not Wise), a contractor may go via Wise. **(3) Guest & host** — interim bridge before IMS Payouts Service + PGW (POL-114/FLOW-34): **GUEST side = the interim tool EXECUTES the payment via Stripe, triggered MANUALLY by the team** (refund/payout; contra-rev / 2110 deposit). **HOST side = the interim tool POSTS to the payout-entries table (`au_/sg_payout_entries`), which then EXECUTES AUTOMATICALLY on a MONTHLY basis** — settles the accrued host payable (POL-37), host accounts 5000-5003; P2P via Stripe Connect, RMS via bank (FLOW-18/POL-40). **(4) Employee claim** — employee cp; Wise or mark-paid; Dr 2303 Employee Claims Payable (POL-78) — an invoice-from-an-employee; RS-3 raise+reimburse was parked exactly at this payment seam. **(5) Misc payout** — rare/unlikely; any cp; no source doc so needs a thin payable row (amount+COA+payee+reason); Wise or mark-paid; Dr chosen COA. **Unifying design:** a per-`payable_type` resolver yielding (payee, amount, currency, liability-to-debit). Build order (Gaurav 2026-08-15): **claims → payroll** → (guest/host, misc later). *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-141** JE CURRENCY = ENTITY FUNCTIONAL; CALLERS MUST CONVERT — AUDIT FOUND 11 GAPS (Pickle audit, 2026-08-15). Every JE line's debit/credit must be in the entity's functional currency (`finance_entities.base_currency`). `journal_service.create` does NOT convert — it trusts the caller and defaults `fx_rate=1`, `currency=base`, `native_amount=amount` (POL-25). The correct primitive is `fx_service.to_functional(amount, ccy, functional_ccy, date)` (POL-26 monthly rate from `finance_fx_rates`; RAISES if no rate — refuses to book unconverted). **Only 2 sites convert:** `transaction_service.py:801` + `categorization_service.py:1953` (`_create_simple_entry`). **NO site guards on currency.** **11 ASSUMES-SAME (book foreign 1:1 at fx=1) — the risk (verified against code on invoice:743 + categorization 1953-vs-2015):** invoice bill `invoice_service.py:743` (archetype DQ-85, hit real data inv444 = 10k USD in AUD entity); AP payment `invoice_service.py:1450/1486/1511` (`:1511` books bank ccy into a DIFFERENT entity); claim bill+payment `claim_service.py:114/141`; categorization `2015` (intra-entity transfer), `2073/2081` (intercompany), `2161/2171` (cross-entity allocation); cross-entity payroll bank leg `payroll_service.py:549`. Intercompany sites are structurally worse: same raw amount into two differently-functional entities → can't eliminate on consolidation. Fix = hoist the `to_functional` pattern from `_create_simple_entry` into a shared helper, call it per-entity/per-leg at all 11 before building lines. Risk only bites when source ccy != entity functional (foreign vendors/contractors, cross-entity). *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-142** MULTI-CURRENCY PRINCIPLE — SOURCE ANY CCY, JE ALWAYS ENTITY CCY, CONVERT AT JE (Gaurav, 2026-08-15). **(1) A payroll run is NOT single-currency per entity** — CS-team salaries run in USD, many wages in INR, inside an SGD/AU entity; a run can be MIXED-currency, denominated in the SALARY/employee currency, not the entity's. This CORRECTS ~~POL-140 "payroll same-currency by construction"~~ — the run-level missing `currency` and the payroll JE sites that assume same-ccy (POL-141 payroll rows) are REAL bugs, not N/A. **(2) The `finance_payouts.amount_sgd` normalized-companion field is an ANTI-PATTERN** (Gaurav "that is exactly the problem"): don't pre-normalize amounts to one currency (SGD) and carry the shadow around. Carry the NATIVE amount only; convert ONCE, to the entity's functional currency, AT JE CREATION, with the fx_rate stored on the line (POL-25/26/141 pattern). **(3) THE RULE: the source document/payable can be in any currency; the GENERAL ENTRY (JE) must ALWAYS be in the entity's functional currency; conversion happens at JE-post, never by carrying normalized companions.** Gaurav is delivering a PR (another worktree) with the invoice currency-handling approach — REBASE `260814_payout_module` onto it, then study how invoice handles it before applying the POL-141 fixes. *(Gaurav, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-144** CROSS-CURRENCY INTRA-ENTITY INTERNAL-TRANSFER PAIRING — FIXED (closes the POL-141 `2015` site) (Pickle, 2026-08-15). The transfer-pairing engine assumed both legs share one currency: `_pick_counter` matched on a tight raw ±2% amount band and the JE was minted from ONE leg's amount at leg-1. A same-entity move where source ccy ≠ target ccy (e.g. 10k out of DBS-USD → ~13.4k into DBS-SGD, both entity 1) could neither pair nor book. **Fix (committed local `b2c92ec`, finance-api-payout):** (a) `_pick_counter` now takes `db` and is currency-aware — cross-ccy legs convert the candidate INTO the waiting leg's currency at the monthly rate (`fx_service.get_monthly_rate`) and widen tolerance to **±5%** (absorbs bank-spot-vs-our-monthly-rate spread); same-ccy stays ±2%; NO rate on file → skip the pair (POL-26), never guess. (b) Cross-ccy **DEFERS** the JE at leg-1 (`journal_entry=None`, `reconciled_journal_entry_id` stays NULL) instead of booking one amount; both the no-feed standalone-match and the awaiting-match branches respect the deferral. (c) `_create_fx_transfer_je` mints the balanced entry once BOTH legs are known (at pairing, or in Phase 0 when the counter arrives): each leg converts independently to the entity functional ccy, residual plugs to **7100** FX gain/loss — the invoice FX pattern applied to two asset accounts. Verified on a local clone against the real code paths + live fx_service lookups (USD −10,000 / SGD +13,450 → pair + JE Dr 1013 13,450 / Cr 1012 13,400 / Cr 7100 50 gain, Posted, balanced). **Cross-ENTITY (intercompany) cross-ccy — 2073/2081 — remains DEFERRED** (Gaurav's call, separate task). *(Pickle, 2026-08-15)*
- **POL-145** LEDGER POSTING ARCHITECTURE — the going-forward JE contract (Gaurav rulings, 2026-08-16). Design docs: `documentation/wip/LEDGER_POSTING_ARCHITECTURE.md` + `JE_POSTING_MAP.html` (2 tabs: current reality + target) + `JE_CREATION_MECHANISMS.md`. The ledger has 23 physical `journal_service.create()` calls (19 logical mechanisms; 4 paired) with NO shared contract for status/currency/line-generation — root cause of three live defects (72 posted-but-unconverted IC groups; 34 draft SG↔AU transfers never classified as IC; amortization never posts). **Target contract, 3 layers:** (1) `journal_service.create` becomes CONVERT-OR-REFUSE — caller passes native+ccy, primitive converts each leg to entity functional, stamps native/rate, plugs residue to 7100, RAISES if no rate (kills the `fx=1` default). (2) A posting resolver keyed by ECONOMIC EVENT generates lines (one rule per event); callers stop hand-building debits/credits. (3) JE lifecycle is SUBORDINATE to the document state machines (invoice/payroll/payout/claim/txn) — born DRAFT, POSTED only via a document ⚡ transition; no inline `status=POSTED`. Enforcement: a pre-commit invariant rejects any JE that doesn't balance or whose lines lack functional-ccy+native+rate. Rollout: Phase-1 (primitive+invariant) first, alone shippable, kills the live fx=1 bug. **Settled sub-decisions:** (a) **GST is a DECORATOR, not an engine** — `gst_service` is pure decision (no `journal_service.create`); posting layer calls `gst_service.classify` while building revenue/expense/bill lines and appends the input/output-GST line; `REVIEW` verdict holds a line for a human; no JE lifecycle of its own. (b) **Intra-entity transfer** = DRAFT at awaiting-match → POST on match (same-ccy exact; cross-ccy trues estimate + 7100). (c) **INTERCOMPANY = INDEPENDENT PER-ENTITY BOOKING** — each entity books ONLY its own leg from its OWN feed in its OWN functional ccy and posts on its own sight (sender Dr IC-Receivable/Cr bank; receiver Dr bank/Cr IC-Payable); NO paired JE, NO cross-entity matching, NO receiver-estimate. The sender books on sight because its cash genuinely left and its books must reflect it without waiting for the receiver. A PERIODIC IC RECONCILIATION trues the two IC balances + books the FX difference (7100 realized for settled treasury transfers; CTA/OCI only for long-term net-investment funding) + flags any unmatched leg. This SUPERSEDES the earlier sender-posts/receiver-estimate/true-up idea. **Legacy found (payroll):** `payroll_service.create_run` (:405) test-only/unwired → removable; `hr_payroll_service.submit_run` (:533, immediate-post) overlaps `payroll_service.submit_for_approval` (:195, approval-gated go-forward) → consolidate to the approval one; `_try_payroll_knockoff` (:910, old) overlaps `_try_payroll_register_knockoff` (:884, new). Knock-off rule: the categorization engine TRIGGERS (matches, emits `PaymentMatched`) but the OWNING domain engine POSTS — so the misplaced payroll-register create at categ:884 moves into payroll_service (invoice/claim knock-offs already delegate correctly). *(Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16)*
- **DQ-106** JE-POSTING SURFACE IS BIGGER THAN `journal_service` — 2 engines BYPASS it, + a 2nd GST impl, + stale code (Pickle sweep, 2026-08-16). Beyond the 23 `journal_service.create()` sites: **(a) Economic Events** (`economic_events/service.py::project_month`) builds `FinanceJournalEntry` + lines DIRECTLY (status=POSTED), template-driven (`FinanceJETemplate` debit/credit codes on STAGED `FinanceEconomicEvent` rows), LIVE (wired via economic_events route/FE tab), assumes fx=1, and carries its OWN GST — `_lane_a_gst` (POL-123 "Lane A") — SEPARATE from categorization's `gst_service.classify`. ~~So there are TWO GST implementations to unify under the one decorator.~~ **CORRECTED (Gaurav, 2026-08-16): the two GST engines are DELIBERATELY different and must NOT be merged** — Lane A = CASH-BASIS (bank-leg, 1/11, accounts 1350 input/2500 output, POL-123); Lane B = ACCRUAL/invoiced (invoice's own tax_amount wins, DEFERRED accounts 1355/2505 until cash settles, POL-87). Different tax basis, different accounts, both correct and locked. Each engine keeps its own GST engine; the posting service carries whichever GST line the engine already computed, it does not decide GST. **(b) Stripe Sync** (`stripe_sync/sync_service.py::sync_month`) also builds `FinanceJournalEntry` directly but has NO route/cron/caller anywhere — dead/dormant. **Stale/dup in-code:** `payroll_service.create_run` (:405) test-only/unwired (live route uses `hr_payroll_service.create_run`); two live payroll accrual posters (`hr_payroll_service.submit_run` :533 immediate-post vs `payroll_service.submit_for_approval` :195 approval-gated) → keep approval; two live payroll knock-offs (`_try_payroll_knockoff` :910 old vs `_try_payroll_register_knockoff` :884 new). **One-off scripts (VR-1c footgun class):** `scripts/vr2_post_{icfx,crossentity,provisional,pairings,xcurrency}.py` — git-tracked VR-2 invoice-pairing backfills (~Aug 2026, "the 728"/"the 102"), inherit app `DATABASE_URL` (PROD by default), only `POST_MODE=pilot|all`, NO prod guard → neutralize/archive if VR-2 posting is done. All folds into the POL-145 posting layer. *(Pickle, 2026-08-16)*
- **DQ-109** NORMALIZED-CURRENCY SHADOW (amount_sgd smell) — SCANNED + FIXED where it lived (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16). The POL-142 anti-pattern (store a pre-converted SGD copy next to the native amount) was NOT everywhere — **invoice and claim are CLEAN** (native amount + `currency`, convert late at JE; invoice `amount_paid` in invoice ccy — the reference pattern). It existed in TWO places, two shapes: **(1) payout register** `finance_payouts.amount_sgd` — a literal SGD shadow; its only real use was the checker threshold, now computed INLINE (`payout_service._to_sgd`), column DROPPED (migration 065). **(2) payroll RUN** — `finance_payroll_runs` stored gross/net/CPF totals with NO currency, summing mixed-currency payslips (USD/INR) into a meaningless number; now each payslip converts to entity functional BEFORE summing, run carries an explicit `currency` field (migration 066), per-employee native stays on `hr_payroll_items`. NOT the smell: `hr_payroll_items.system_gross_amount/system_net_amount` (an audit baseline for adjustments, PR-6, keep); `coa_config.*_sgd` (legit SGD policy thresholds). Rule reaffirmed: NATIVE at rest, convert LATE. All clone-tested. *(Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16)*
- **POL-146** THE KNOCK-OFF LADDER — the going-forward matching + settlement design, BUILT (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16). The categorization engine matches a bank txn down a LADDER, first match wins, later rungs skip: **Rung 0** internal-transfer pairing + counterparty enrichment · **Rung 1** UNIFIED transfer-ID knock-off (`_try_transfer_id_knockoff`): deterministic `wise_transfer_id` match to an AWAITING_IMPORT payout, currency-AGNOSTIC (SGD payable settles a USD payment — amounts need not match), FX-aware, ONE rung for invoice/claim/payroll dispatched by `payable_type` via `_settle_payout_by_type`; the through-our-system path — MOVED into the engine from `payout_service.pair_on_import` (which was invoice-only) · **Rungs 2-4** amount/date FALLBACKS for OUTSIDE-system payments, each FX-aware, guarded on **RECONCILE** (invoice `reconcile` already existed; added `ClaimStatus.RECONCILE` + `PayoutState.RECONCILE`, varchar so NO migration) — the guard = "finance marked it paid-outside, awaiting the bank line", set via `claim_service.mark_reconcile` / `payout_service.mark_reconcile` (routes `POST /claims|payouts/<id>/mark-reconcile`); ONE payroll knock-off (register), the old run-based `_try_payroll_knockoff` retired · **Rung 5** rules/AI. **Manual override:** `categorization_service.manual_knockoff(txn_id, payout_id)` (route `POST /transactions/<id>/manual-knockoff`) — a human pairs txn↔payout and posts the same FX-aware settlement for cases the ladder can't match. **Settlement currency rule:** payable amount is recorded NATIVE on the register (`FinancePayout.amount`/`currency`); the actual debit is native on the txn; both convert to entity functional at the JE, spread→7100 (same-entity) or trued at IC recon (cross-entity paired). `amount_sgd` shadow on the register is the POL-142 anti-pattern, still to remove (item 4, deferred). All clone-tested (`tests/clone_fx/`), NEVER prod. *(Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16)*
- **POL-147** PAYROLL IS AN ACCRUAL — NET CREDITS 2304 SALARIES PAYABLE, NEVER THE BANK (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16). A payroll run mirrors claims (2303) and invoices (AP): a two-step accrue-then-pay. **Accrual** (`hr_payroll_service.submit_run` → `_build_je_lines_and_groups(net_to_account="2304")`): `Dr salary expense (per salary code) + Dr employer-contribution expense` / `Cr each statutory payable (via the per-employee deduction rules' coa_credit_code — CPF 2300, super 2302, PAYG-withholding 2301, income tax 2305) + Cr 2304 Salaries Payable (NET)`. NO bank leg. **Payment** (payout knock-off, `categorization_service._settle_payroll_payout`): `Dr <2304 | statutory:code> / Cr bank`, FX spread→7100 same-entity or paired-IC cross-entity. The **fan-out** (`payroll_service.fan_out_to_register`, run must be POSTED) splits the accrual into register payouts: one NET payout per employee (payee=employee, settles 2304) + one statutory payout per authority (CPF Board/super fund/ATO, settles 2300/2302/2301/2305) — statutory only for `tax_treatment='internal'` staff. **BUG FIXED:** submit_run used to credit the BANK for net (pay-immediately), double-counting the bank (accrual + settlement) and stranding 2304; now credits 2304. Bank no longer needed at accrual → `bank_account_id` optional on create_run / PayrollRunCreate / the `finance_payroll_runs` column (migration 068); the run modal asks only for the ENTITY. Closed the 2301 PAYG (AU) gap in `STATUTORY_AUTHORITY` (was {2300,2302,2305}). Clone-verified: accrual credits 2304+2300 and balances, fan-out yields net+statutory payouts that net both payables to zero. NEVER prod. *(Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16)*
- **POL-148** PAYROLL DRAFT DOES NO FX; FX HAPPENS AT SUBMIT (Gaurav+Pickle, 2026-08-16). A DRAFT payroll run creates NO journal entry and converts NOTHING — every payslip is shown in the employee's OWN salary currency (`hr_compensation.currency` → `hr_payroll_items.currency`; gross/net stored NATIVE). `create_run` tallies native amounts per currency: a single-currency run stores its native total, a **mixed-currency run leaves run-level totals NULL** (migration 069 made `finance_payroll_runs` gross/net/employer_cpf/employee_cpf/cpf_payable nullable).
